## W976. Store-Level Customer Lumber & Plywood Grade Selection & Quality Verification

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer selects lumber, plywood, or engineered wood product and requests grade verification or assistance choosing the appropriate grade |
| **Frequency** | ~6,000–8,000 selections/month chain-wide (~30–40/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–10 pieces per selection; average transaction value PHP 2,000–15,000 |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Lumber Specialist), Customer, Receiving Clerk |

### Background

Lumber and plywood are sold in grades that determine structural suitability, appearance, and price. In the Philippine market, lumber grades include: Select (clear, minimal knots), Common #1 (small tight knots), Common #2 (larger knots, wane), and Utility (structural but with defects). Plywood grades range from A-A (both faces smooth, furniture grade) to B-B (one smooth face, one rough) to C-C (both rough, sheathing grade) to marine plywood (waterproof adhesive for exterior/structural use). Most Filipino homeowners and many small contractors are unfamiliar with grading systems and may: (a) purchase lower-grade material than required (leading to structural issues or aesthetic disappointment); (b) purchase higher-grade material than necessary (overpaying); (c) unknowingly purchase lumber that is wet (high moisture content) that will warp or shrink as it dries. BuildRight's lumber grade selection service helps customers choose the correct grade for their application, verifies quality at point of sale, and provides moisture content testing — building trust and reducing returns.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Application Assessment**: Customer describes their project: (a) application type — structural (joists, beams, rafters, framing), decorative (cabinetry, trim, moulding), exterior (decking, fencing, outdoor furniture), sheathing (wall sheathing, roof decking, subfloor), concrete formwork, or general purpose; (b) load requirements — structural members require grade verification (especially for beams and joists); (c) aesthetic expectations — visible woodwork requires higher appearance grade; (d) environment — indoor (dry), covered outdoor, exposed outdoor, wet area (bathroom, kitchen); (e) budget considerations; Sales Associate recommends appropriate grade: structural → Common #1 or better; decorative → Select or A-grade plywood; exterior → treated lumber or marine plywood; concrete formwork → C-C plywood (single use) or B-B plywood (reusable) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Lumber Selection & Visual Quality Check**: Customer selects pieces from lumber rack; Sales Associate conducts visual quality check: (a) grade verification — checking for: knot size and frequency, splits/checks, wane (bark edge), warp/bow/twist/cup, insect damage (termite tunnels), fungal decay (blue stain, rot); (b) moisture content — Sales Associate uses pin-type moisture meter to test selected pieces: acceptable ranges: kiln-dried (12–18%), air-dried (18–25%), green (>25% — not recommended for interior use); high-moisture lumber flagged: customer informed of potential shrinkage/warping and advised to acclimate before installation; (c) dimensional verification — actual dimensions vs. nominal dimensions (e.g., "2×4" actually measures 1.5"×3.5"); Sales Associate measures critical pieces with tape measure for accuracy; (d) straightness check — sighting along the edge to verify no bow or twist beyond acceptable tolerance (≤3mm per 2.4m length) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min/piece |
| 3 | **Plywood Selection & Grade Verification**: Customer selects plywood sheets; Sales Associate verifies: (a) face grade — both faces checked against grade standard (A: smooth, no knots; B: tight knots, some repairs; C: open defects, knots); (b) core quality — edge check for voids, delamination, and core species; (c) glue bond type — interior (UREA) vs. exterior (phenolic/melamine) — verified by grade stamp or label; (d) thickness — measured with caliper at multiple points; standard tolerance ±0.5mm; (e) flatness — sheet placed flat on floor to check for warp; warped sheets replaced; (f) marine plywood — verified by branding/stamp; genuine marine plywood uses waterproof phenolic adhesive and has no core voids | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min/sheet |
| 4 | **Cutting Service & Purchase**: If customer requests lumber cutting per W169: (a) Sales Associate marks cut points on selected pieces; (b) cutting executed per W169; (c) post-cut quality check — cut ends checked for splits; customer verifies cut dimensions; customer proceeds to POS: (a) grade and quality verified pieces processed at POS with grade annotation on receipt; (b) customer receives care instructions: storage (keep dry, elevated, flat for plywood), acclimation (allow 48–72 hours in installation environment for kiln-dried lumber), and treatment recommendations (termite treatment, sealant application for exterior use); (c) warranty information: lumber is a natural product — warranty covers manufacturing/grading defects only (not natural characteristics or post-purchase handling damage) | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- POS with lumber grade and species master data (W50)
- Moisture meter integration — readings recorded against SKU for quality tracking
- Product attribute master with grade specifications, typical applications, and pricing
- Lumber cutting service (W169) integration
- Customer care instruction printing at POS
- Quality feedback loop — customer quality complaints tracked against receiving batch for vendor quality per W110

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Grade inconsistency**: Lumber grading is subjective and varies between vendors and even between individual graders; what one vendor calls "Common #1" another may grade differently; BuildRight's visual quality check at step 2 provides a second verification layer; consistent vendor grading standards enforced per W669 (vendor contract compliance)
- **Moisture content disputes**: Lumber moisture content changes with storage conditions — lumber tested at 15% in the morning may read 18% by afternoon in humid Philippine climate; readings are a snapshot; Sales Associate must explain moisture variability and recommend acclimation; moisture meter calibrated monthly per manufacturer specification
- **Warp after purchase**: Lumber that was straight at point of sale may warp after customer transports and stores it; this is a natural characteristic of wood, not a BuildRight quality issue; care instructions at POS educate customer on proper storage; returns for warp are assessed case-by-case per W12
- **Termite risk in tropical climate**: Philippine lumber is highly susceptible to termite infestation; Sales Associate must recommend treatment (borate treatment, creosote, or chemical preservative) for all structural and exterior applications; treated lumber premium priced; inventory of treated lumber maintained at stores in high-termite-risk areas (most of the Philippines); mitigated by standard operating procedures and system-enforced validation rules


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "Lumber Selection & Visual Quality Check" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-measure of "measured with caliper at multiple points" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-post of "post-cut quality check — cut ends checked for splits" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Lumber Specialist)**: 1 per store with lumber grading knowledge; lumber quality checks consume ~20–60 min/day at ~30–40 selections/store/month (1–2 selections/day); absorbed by existing Lumber & Building Materials department staff
- **Training**: 16-hour lumber and plywood grading certification per W51; includes hands-on moisture meter training, visual grading exercises, and Philippine wood species identification (tanguile, apitong, lauan, yakal)
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Application assessment: 5–10 min
- Lumber quality check: 5–10 min/piece
- Plywood verification: 5–8 min/sheet
- Cutting & purchase: 5–10 min
- **Total per transaction**: 15–35 min of staff time

## W981. Customer Paint Color Matching from Physical Sample & Digital Photo

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer presents a physical color sample (paint chip, fabric swatch, tile piece, wall scrape) or digital photo for paint color matching |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–12,000 color matches/month chain-wide (~40–60/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–3 color matches per customer visit; average paint purchase 2–4 gallons |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Paint & Finishes) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Paint Specialist), Customer, Category Manager |

### Background

Color matching is the paint department's most technically demanding and highest-value service. Customers frequently need to: (a) match an existing wall color for touch-up or extension; (b) match a specific color from a fabric, tile, or décor element for a coordinated design; (c) replicate a color seen in a magazine, social media post, or friend's home (via photo); (d) match a discontinued paint brand or product; (e) replicate a color from another paint brand's swatch at a BuildRight-comparable price. While BuildRight already offers custom paint mixing per W168 (Custom Paint Mixing & Tinting Operations), this workflow specifically addresses the color matching input — how the customer's physical sample or digital photo is translated into a precise tint formula. Accurate color matching requires: spectrophotometer hardware (color measurement device), color formulation software, and experienced Sales Associates (Sales Associates (Paint Specialists)) who understand color theory, substrate effects, and lighting conditions. In the Philippine market, where many homes have existing paint from multiple brands and decades of layers, color matching is essential for renovation projects.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Sample Assessment & Preparation**: Customer presents color sample: (a) physical samples: (i) paint chip (ideal — flat, opaque, ≥2cm × 2cm); (ii) wall scrape (drywall/plaster with paint layer); (iii) fabric swatch (textured — may affect reading); (iv) tile piece, laminate chip, or plastic item; (v) dried paint sample on paper/cardboard; (vi) existing object (ceramic vase, pillow cover, etc.); (b) digital samples: (i) customer's smartphone photo — taken in store or shown from gallery; (ii) screenshot from social media, magazine, or design website; (iii) brand-specific color code (if customer knows it — e.g., "Dulux Whisper White", "Boysen Laguna Blue"); the Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) assesses sample quality: (i) physical sample: clean, flat, opaque, ≥2cm × 2cm — ideal for spectrophotometer; textured or glossy samples require manual adjustment; (ii) digital photo: lighting conditions matter — photos taken under warm indoor lighting will read differently from actual color; the Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) advises customer that digital photo matching is approximate (±2 ΔE color difference) vs. physical sample matching (±0.5 ΔE) | Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Spectrophotometer Measurement & Formula Generation**: For physical samples: (a) The Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) calibrates spectrophotometer using white and black calibration tiles (daily calibration at store opening per equipment SOP); (b) places physical sample on spectrophotometer reading surface; (c) takes 3 readings from different areas of sample (to average out surface variations); (d) spectrophotometer measures: L* (lightness), a* (red-green), b* (yellow-blue) values in CIELAB color space; (e) color formulation software matches measured L*a*b* values against: (i) BuildRight's tint database — 10,000+ color formulations across BuildRight paint bases; (ii) competitor brand color libraries — cross-reference charts for major Philippine paint brands (Boysen, Davies, Welcoat, Coat Saver, Dutch Boy) and international brands (Dulux, Nippon Paint, Jotun); (f) software generates: (i) closest BuildRight color match with ΔE value (color difference — ΔE < 1.0 is imperceptible, 1.0–2.0 is barely noticeable, >2.0 is visible); (ii) tint formula — specific volumes of each colorant (tint) to add to selected paint base; (iii) recommended paint base — white base (for light colors), pastel base, mid-tone base, deep base, or clear base (for very dark/vivid colors) | Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 3 | **Digital Photo Matching** (for photo/screenshot samples): (a) customer places phone/device on color viewing station — a standardized D65 daylight viewing booth that provides consistent illumination; (b) The Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) visually compares photo color against BuildRight color fan deck (physical swatch book with 2,000+ colors); (c) identifies 2–3 closest colors from fan deck; (d) for more precision: The Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) uses BuildRight mobile app's color matching feature — customer photographs sample under the viewing booth's D65 light; app analyzes color and suggests closest BuildRight color (uses same color formulation database as spectrophotometer but via phone camera — accuracy ±2 ΔE vs. ±0.5 ΔE for spectrophotometer); (e) The Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) presents 2–3 options to customer for visual comparison; customer selects closest match | Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Sample Mixing & Customer Approval**: Before mixing full quantity: (a) The Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) mixes a small sample (50ml) using the generated formula per W168 (custom paint mixing); (b) applies sample to a test card (white cardboard) using mini roller (to simulate actual application method); (c) allows to dry — paint color changes as it dries (wet paint appears darker); drying time: 10–15 minutes with quick-dry additive or 30 minutes standard; (d) customer compares dried sample against original color sample under D65 daylight lamp; (e) if customer approves: proceed to full quantity mixing per W168; (f) if customer requests adjustment: The Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) adjusts formula (lighter/darker, warmer/cooler, more/less saturated) based on customer feedback and re-mixes sample; typically 1–2 adjustment cycles; (g) final approved formula saved to BuildRight color database and linked to customer's loyalty account for future reorder per W969; formula printed on paint can label with: color code, formula, date, store, and customer name | Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 15–25 min (including drying time) |
| 5 | **Full Quantity Mixing & Purchase**: Customer-approved formula mixed to full quantity per W168; the Sales Associate (Paint Specialist): (a) adds recommended accessories to cart: roller, tray, brush, painter's tape, drop cloth, primer (if needed), sandpaper; (b) provides application guidance: recommended number of coats (typically 2), drying time between coats (2–4 hours), surface preparation requirements; (c) for large projects: offers quantity calculator — room wall area ÷ coverage per gallon (typically 30–40 sqm per gallon per coat × number of coats); (d) customer pays at POS; paint formula saved to customer account per W898 (custom paint formula save/recall/reorder) | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Spectrophotometer hardware integration with color formulation software
- Color formulation database — 10,000+ formulas with competitor brand cross-reference
- BuildRight mobile app color matching feature (camera-based)
- POS integration with paint mixing system per W168
- Customer loyalty account integration for formula saving per W898
- Color fan deck management (physical inventory of color swatch books)
- Quick reorder from saved formula per W969
- Paint quantity calculator (room area → gallons needed)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Color metamerism**: Colors that match under one light source (store fluorescent) may appear different under another (home LED, sunlight, incandescent); D65 daylight viewing booth simulates natural daylight for matching; the Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) must warn customer that perceived color may differ slightly under their home lighting; test sample viewed under multiple light sources when possible
- **Digital photo inaccuracy**: Smartphone cameras introduce color cast, white balance errors, and compression artifacts; photos edited with Instagram/filters are especially problematic; the Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) must set customer expectations: "Digital matching is a close approximation. For exact matching, a physical sample is recommended."; ΔE tolerance for digital matching is ±2 vs. ±0.5 for spectrophotometer
- **Substrate color effect**: Paint color appears different on different substrates — the same paint on new drywall, previously painted dark wall, bare concrete, or wood will look different; the Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) recommends appropriate primer per substrate; test sample applied to similar substrate when possible
- **Spectrophotometer calibration drift**: Daily calibration is essential; uncalibrated spectrophotometer produces incorrect readings; the Sales Associate (Paint Specialist) performs calibration at store opening and logs in equipment register per W650; annual professional recalibration by manufacturer


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-match of "matching is approximate" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-generate of "Spectrophotometer Measurement & Formula Generation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-analyze of "analyzes color and suggests closest BuildRight color" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Paint & Finishes) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Paint Specialist)**: Color matching consumes ~17–50 hours/month (~0.5–2 hours/day averaged) at 40–60 matches/month (per the Frequency); absorbed by existing Paint department Sales Associate with specialized color matching training
- **Training**: 24-hour color theory and spectrophotometer operation certification per W51; includes hands-on matching exercises, color adjustment techniques, and competitor brand color library familiarization
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Sample assessment: 3–5 min
- Spectrophotometer measurement: 5–8 min
- Digital matching: 5–10 min
- Sample mixing & approval: 15–25 min (including drying)
- Full mixing & purchase: 5–10 min
- **Total per match**: 33–58 min of staff time

---

## W982. Customer Electrical Load Calculation & Wire Size Recommendation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests assistance with electrical wiring sizing, circuit design, or load calculation for residential or small commercial construction |
| **Frequency** | ~5,000–7,000 calculations/month chain-wide (~25–35/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–3 circuits per consultation; average purchase value PHP 3,000–15,000 |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Electrical) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Electrical Specialist), Customer, Category Manager |

### Background

Electrical wiring is one of the most safety-critical product categories in a hardware store. Undersized wires (too thin for the electrical load) overheat, potentially causing fires — a serious risk in Philippine residential construction where informal (unlicensed) electricians commonly perform wiring work and the Philippine Electrical Code (PEC) is not always followed. BuildRight's electrical load calculation and wire size recommendation service helps customers — homeowners, DIY renovators, and small contractors — select the correct wire size, circuit breaker rating, and accessories for their specific electrical installation. This service: (a) prevents safety hazards from undersized wiring; (b) reduces customer confusion in a technically complex category (THHN wire sizes: AWG 14, 12, 10, 8, 6, 4, 2, 1/0, 2/0, 3/0, 4/0 MCM); (c) increases average transaction value by recommending a complete circuit package (wire + breaker + outlet + switch + conduit + junction box); (d) positions BuildRight as a trusted advisor, not just a materials supplier. The service is available in-store via Electrical Specialist consultation and via BuildRight mobile app with an interactive calculator.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Electrical Project Assessment**: Customer describes their electrical project: (a) project type — new house wiring, room addition, circuit extension, appliance dedicated circuit, lighting circuit, or outlet addition; (b) load information — what will be connected: (i) lighting: type (LED, fluorescent, incandescent), number of fixtures, total wattage; (ii) outlets: number of outlets, intended appliances (aircon, refrigerator, microwave, electric stove, water heater, washing machine); (iii) fixed appliances: air conditioner (HP rating), water heater (kW), electric stove (kW), pump motor (HP); (c) circuit information — dedicated circuit (single appliance) or general purpose circuit (multiple outlets/lights); (d) supply voltage: 220V single-phase (standard Philippine residential) or 380V three-phase (commercial/industrial); (e) wire run distance: from breaker panel to farthest outlet/fixture (affects voltage drop calculation); (f) installation environment: indoor (concealed in wall), outdoor, underground, or wet area | Sales Associate (Electrical Specialist) | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Load Calculation & Wire Size Determination**: Sales Associate performs load calculation using BuildRight electrical calculator (in-store tablet or customer's mobile app): (a) **Total connected load**: sum of all appliance wattages on the circuit; (b) **Continuous load derating**: for loads operating ≥3 hours (lighting, aircon), apply 125% factor per PEC; (c) **Wire ampacity lookup**: system selects minimum wire size that can carry the calculated current (I = P ÷ V) with safety margin; standard Philippine residential wire sizes: (i) AWG 14 (2.0 sqmm) — 15A — lighting circuits, general outlets; (ii) AWG 12 (3.5 sqmm) — 20A — kitchen outlets, general purpose; (iii) AWG 10 (5.5 sqmm) — 30A — window-type aircon (1.0–1.5 HP); (iv) AWG 8 (8.0 sqmm) — 40A — split-type aircon (2.0 HP), electric stove; (v) AWG 6 (14 sqmm) — 55A — sub-feeders, large appliances; (vi) AWG 4 (22 sqmm) — 70A — main feeders; (d) **Voltage drop check**: for wire runs >30 meters, system calculates voltage drop (VD = 2 × I × R × L ÷ 1000); if voltage drop exceeds 3%, wire size is upsized; (e) **Breaker sizing**: circuit breaker rating = next standard size above calculated load current; standard breaker sizes: 15A, 20A, 30A, 40A, 50A, 60A; (f) **Conduit sizing**: conduit diameter sized for number and size of wires per PEC fill ratio (max 40% fill); (g) system generates: recommended wire size, breaker size, conduit size, and total wire length based on run distance | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min (automated calculation) |
| 3 | **Complete Circuit Package Recommendation**: Sales Associate presents recommended materials as a complete circuit package: (a) **Wire**: correct size, type (THHN/THWN for indoor, TF for outdoor), and length (run distance × number of conductors: line + neutral + ground); (b) **Circuit breaker**: correct amperage and pole (1-pole for 220V circuits); (c) **Outlets**: type and quantity — standard outlet (for general use), aircon outlet (heavy-duty, with switch), GFCI outlet (for wet areas — bathroom, kitchen, outdoor); (d) **Switches**: type and quantity — single-pole, 3-way (for staircases), or dimmer; (e) **Conduit and fittings**: PVC conduit, elbows, connectors, couplings, and junction boxes; (f) **Accessories**: wire nuts/terminals, electrical tape, cable clips, and wire markers; (g) **Safety equipment**: wire stripper, voltage tester, and insulated screwdriver (if customer doesn't have); Sales Associate explains each component's purpose and why the specified size is necessary for safety; system displays package price and individual item prices | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Safety Briefing & Professional Referral**: Before purchase, Sales Associate conducts brief safety briefing: (a) emphasizes: (i) never work on live circuits — always turn off breaker before wiring; (ii) wire size and breaker must be matched — never install a larger breaker on undersized wire; (iii) grounding is mandatory — third wire (green/bare) must be connected; (iv) wet area circuits require GFCI protection; (b) system prints safety information card with purchase receipt; (c) if customer is a homeowner without electrical experience: Sales Associate strongly recommends hiring a licensed electrician and offers referral to BuildRight's installation partner network per W138; (d) for complex projects (3-phase, commercial, or whole-house wiring): Sales Associate recommends consulting a licensed electrical engineer and provides contact list of BuildRight's accredited electrical contractors per W600 | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 5 | **Purchase & Project Linking**: Customer purchases complete circuit package; Sales Associate: (a) links purchase to customer's project per W894 (Project Vault) if customer has an active project; (b) for wire sold by length: system captures exact length cut from spool per W463 (catch-weight processing); (c) receipt includes: wire size, breaker size, circuit type, and load calculation summary — serves as documentation for customer's electrician; (d) offers to save circuit specification to customer's account for future reorder per W969 (e.g., customer building multiple houses with identical electrical plans) | Sales Associate / Cashier | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Electrical load calculator module (POS tablet and mobile app)
- Philippine Electrical Code (PEC) wire ampacity table database
- Voltage drop calculation engine
- Product catalog with wire specifications per W50
- Complete circuit package bundling and pricing
- Catch-weight processing for wire sold by length (W463)
- Customer Project Vault (W894) for circuit specification saving
- Quick reorder integration (W969)
- Installation partner referral (W138)
- Safety information printing at POS

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Liability for electrical recommendations**: BuildRight provides sizing recommendations, not engineering designs; incorrect calculations could lead to safety hazards; clear disclaimer on all outputs: "This calculation is a general guide based on standard Philippine Electrical Code tables. For complex installations, consult a licensed electrical engineer. BuildRight is not liable for installation outcomes."; system calculations follow PEC conservative safety margins
- **Customer DIY risk**: Filipino homeowners frequently perform their own electrical work to save costs; while BuildRight cannot prevent DIY work, the safety briefing (step 4), professional referral, and safety information card fulfill BuildRight's duty of care; product liability insurance per W863 provides coverage
- **Wire size confusion**: Wire sizes are referred to differently — AWG (American), sqmm (metric), and local Filipino trade names ("no. 14 wire", "8-sqmm wire"); Sales Associates must be fluent in all naming conventions; system displays wire size in all three formats for clarity
- **PEC code updates**: The Philippine Electrical Code is updated periodically (current: PEC 2017); wire ampacity tables and installation requirements may change; Category Manager monitors PEC updates and adjusts calculator database accordingly; annual review of calculator parameters per regulatory change management per W657


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "Load Calculation & Wire Size Determination" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-test of "tester, and insulated screwdriver" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-generate of "prints safety information card with purchase receipt" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: calculations follow PEC conservative safety margins; operational: displays wire size in all three formats for clarity

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Electrical Specialist)**: Electrical consultations consume ~10–30 hours/month (~0.5–1 hour/day averaged) at 25–35 calculations/month (per the Frequency); absorbed by existing Electrical department Sales Associate with specialized electrical training
- **Training**: 24-hour electrical load calculation and PEC familiarization certification per W51; includes hands-on wire sizing exercises, breaker selection, and safety briefing practice; annual refresher aligned with PEC updates
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project assessment: 5–10 min
- Load calculation: 5–8 min
- Package recommendation: 5–10 min
- Safety briefing: 3–5 min
- Purchase: 5–8 min
- **Total per consultation**: 23–41 min of staff time

## W983. Customer Roofing Material Calculator & GI Sheet/Insulation Sizing Recommendation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests roofing material quantity calculation at store or via mobile app |
| **Frequency** | ~6,000–8,000 calculations/month chain-wide (~30–40/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–2 roof sections per calculation; average 1.4 sections |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Roofing Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Roofing is one of the highest-value, most anxiety-inducing purchases in Philippine home improvement. The country's typhoon belt status means roofing material selection directly impacts family safety. The market predominantly uses corrugated GI (galvanized iron) sheets in lengths of 6, 8, 10, and 12 feet, with gauges ranging from 26 (lightest residential) to 22 (commercial/heavy-duty). Customers must account for roof pitch, overhang, ridge cap coverage, and the critical overlapping requirement (1.5–2 corrugations on sides, 15–30 cm on ends depending on pitch). Incorrect calculations lead to shortages mid-project (delaying the critical task of closing the roof before rain) or costly over-purchases of heavy, difficult-to-return materials. Additional items — insulation (foil-backed foam or fiberglass), ridge rolls, flashings, gutters, fascia boards, roofing nails/screws with washers, and sealant — are frequently overlooked by DIY customers. A professional calculator service increases average order value through cross-selling accessories and builds customer confidence. Post-typhoon demand surges (300–500% above baseline in affected areas) make this a revenue-critical service during the June–November typhoon season.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Roof Measurement & Structure Intake**: Customer provides roof details via: (a) physical measurements — length and width of each roof section (in meters or feet), roof pitch/slope angle (or rise-over-run), number of valleys, hips, ridges, and dormers; (b) house floor plan or blueprint — Sales Associate interprets roof plan dimensions; (c) mobile app — customer uses BuildRight app's roof measurement tool with satellite imagery overlay or AR measurement for existing structures; Sales Associate records: roof shape (gable, hip, shed, flat, combination), number of sections, pitch angle, overhang dimensions (typically 0.6–1.2m in PH residential), existing roof condition (new construction vs. re-roofing) | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 2 | **Material Selection & Specification**: Customer selects roofing material with Sales Associate guidance: (a) GI sheet type — plain corrugated (longest life), pre-painted corrugated (colored, aesthetic), ribbed/standing seam (commercial); (b) gauge/thickness — Gauge 26 (0.45mm, economy residential), Gauge 24 (0.50mm, standard), Gauge 22 (0.63mm, premium/typhoon-rated); (c) length — 6ft, 8ft, 10ft, 12ft standard; custom lengths available via W988 welding/fabrication partner; (d) color (pre-painted only) — tile red, brick red, forest green, ocean blue, brown, white, beige; (e) insulation type — reflective foil (economy), foil-backed foam board (standard), fiberglass blanket (premium); (f) accessories — ridge cap, apex cap, valley flashing, gutter system, fascia cover, bargeboard, roofing screws with EPDM washers, butyl tape sealant | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Automated Quantity Calculation**: System computes: (a) total roof area per section (plan area ÷ cos(pitch angle) for sloped surfaces); (b) effective coverage per sheet (gross sheet width minus side overlap of 1.5–2 corrugations ≈ 57–61 cm effective width; effective length = sheet length minus end overlap of 15–30 cm based on pitch); (c) sheets required per section = (roof width ÷ effective sheet width) × (roof slope length ÷ effective sheet length), rounded up to full sheets; (d) ridge cap pieces (linear meters of ridge ÷ cap coverage per piece); (e) valley flashing (linear meters of valleys ÷ effective flashing overlap); (f) insulation area = total roof area (same as sheet plan coverage, no overlap reduction); (g) screws = 12–16 per sheet (depending on purlin spacing); (h) gutter length = total eave perimeter; (i) total weight estimate for delivery planning (GI sheet ≈ 4–6 kg/sqm depending on gauge) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 2–4 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Cross-Sell Accessories & Services**: Sales Associate recommends: (a) roof insulation (most customers skip this but it reduces indoor temperature by 3–5°C in PH tropical climate); (b) gutter system with downspouts (for rainwater collection, increasingly popular in PH); (c) fascia boards and bargeboard (weatherproofing roof edges); (d) roof installation service referral per W138; (e) waterproof roof sealant for nail/screw holes and overlaps; (f) safety equipment rental per W139 (safety harness, roofing brackets) for DIY customers; system updates quotation with all recommended accessories | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 5 | **Customer Review, Order Creation & Delivery Scheduling**: Customer reviews itemized materials list with quantities and prices; Sales Associate creates POS quotation per W542 or direct sale; for large orders (≥20 sheets or ≥200 kg total weight): (a) system schedules delivery via W5D or W980; (b) customer selects delivery date and confirms delivery address; (c) unloading assistance offered (roofing bundles require 2-person carry); (d) for orders exceeding store stock, system initiates inter-store transfer per W22 or DC replenishment per W4; quotation saved to customer's loyalty account per W894 (Project Vault) | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Roofing quantity calculator module (POS and mobile app) with pitch-correct area calculation engine
- Real-time inventory availability with GI sheet gauge/color/length matrix per store
- POS quotation module (W542) for itemized roofing material list
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)
- Delivery scheduling (W5D) for heavy/bulky roofing orders
- Inter-store transfer (W22) for stock availability across locations
- Product catalog with roofing specifications (coverage area per sheet by gauge and length)
- Installation service referral (W138) for roof installation
- Safety equipment rental (W139) for DIY roofing projects

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Measurement accuracy**: Customer roof measurements are frequently inaccurate (±10–15 cm is common); steep-pitch roofs are difficult for customers to measure; Sales Associate must recommend professional measurement per W442 for orders above PHP 50,000; satellite imagery tool in mobile app significantly improves accuracy for existing structures
- **Typhoon demand surges**: Post-typhoon demand for GI sheets, nails, and tarpaulins can spike 300–500% in affected regions within 48 hours; pre-positioned buffer stock per W960 and emergency procurement per W60 are critical; Category Manager must activate emergency pricing controls to prevent gouging perception
- **Color/finish batch matching**: Pre-painted GI sheets from different production runs may have slight color variation; for large orders requiring multiple bundles, system must prioritize same-batch allocation; for split deliveries, customer must be informed of potential shade variation
- **Weight and handling**: A 12-foot gauge 22 GI sheet weighs ~18 kg and is unwieldy in wind; store receiving, storage, and customer loading require dedicated vertical racks per W3B; vehicle loading assistance per W950 is mandatory for roofing orders
- **Safety liability**: DIY roofing is dangerous (falls from height are a leading cause of construction injuries in PH); Sales Associate must offer safety harness rental per W139 and strongly recommend professional installation per W138; BuildRight's liability is limited to product quality, not installation safety per W285


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "blueprint — Sales Associate interprets roof plan dimensions" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-update of "updates quotation with all recommended accessories" (replaces manual Step 4).
- System auto-review of "Review, Order Creation & Delivery Scheduling" (replaces manual Step 5).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must prioritize same-batch allocation; operational: vehicle loading assistance per W950 mandatory for roofing orders

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Roofing Specialist)**: Roofing calculations consume ~12–29 hours/month (~0.4–1 hour/day) at 30–40 calculations/month; absorbed by existing Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor or Sales Associate with roofing product certification per W51
- **Training**: 12-hour roofing material and calculation certification per W51; includes gauge selection, pitch calculation, and accessory cross-selling techniques
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Roof measurement & structure intake: 8–12 min
- Material selection & specification: 5–10 min
- Quantity calculation (automated): 2–4 min
- Cross-sell accessories & services: 5–8 min
- Customer review, order & delivery: 5–10 min
- **Total per calculation**: 25–44 min of staff time

## W984. Customer Water Tank & Pump System Sizing Recommendation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests water tank and/or pump sizing recommendation at store or via mobile app |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–4,000 consultations/month chain-wide (~15–20/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1 system per consultation; average 1 system |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Plumbing) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Plumbing Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Water supply reliability is a critical concern for Philippine households and businesses, particularly in provincial areas where municipal water pressure is low or supply is intermittent. Many BuildRight customers need a complete water storage and distribution system: a water tank (polyethylene or stainless steel, ranging from 200 liters to 10,000 liters), a water pump (shallow well jet pump, deep well submersible, or pressure booster pump), piping connections, float valves, and accessories. Sizing is complex — a tank too small runs dry during supply interruptions; a tank too large wastes money and space. A pump too weak cannot deliver adequate pressure to upper floors; a pump too powerful wastes electricity and shortens pipe life. The Philippine market has additional considerations: NWRB (National Water Resources Board) regulations for well pumps, LGU requirements for water tank installations in commercial buildings, and typhoon preparedness (ensuring water supply when power is interrupted). This service drives high-value sales (a complete system averages PHP 15,000–50,000) and creates long-term customer relationships through pump maintenance and replacement parts.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Water Demand Assessment**: Sales Associate collects: (a) number of household/establishment occupants; (b) number of bathrooms, kitchen sinks, laundry areas, and garden taps; (c) number of floors in the building (affects pump head pressure requirement); (d) water source — municipal supply (with pressure/volume issues), shallow well (≤25ft depth), deep well (>25ft); (e) current water supply schedule (24/7, specific hours, intermittent); (f) available tank installation space (ground level, roof deck, inside utility room); (g) budget range | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 2 | **Tank Sizing Calculation**: System calculates recommended tank capacity: (a) daily water consumption = occupants × 150 liters/person/day (PH standard residential); commercial/industrial uses higher factors; (b) desired storage days = 1–3 days for municipal supply backup, 1 day minimum for well systems; (c) tank capacity = daily consumption × storage days; (d) system recommends standard tank sizes (200L, 500L, 1000L, 2000L, 3000L, 5000L, 10000L) rounding up to nearest available size; (e) tank material recommendation — polyethylene (lightweight, UV-stabilized, economy), stainless steel (durable, hygienic, premium); (f) installation type — freestanding (ground level), elevated platform (roof deck), horizontal/vertical orientation based on available space | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Pump Sizing & Selection**: System calculates pump specification: (a) required flow rate (liters per minute) based on simultaneous fixture usage (typically 2–3 fixtures simultaneously in residential); (b) total dynamic head (TDH) = vertical lift (suction head + discharge head to highest fixture) + friction loss (pipe length × friction factor per pipe diameter); (c) pump type recommendation — shallow well jet pump (depth ≤25ft, PH most common), deep well submersible pump (depth >25ft), pressure booster pump (municipal supply with low pressure), automatic pump with pressure tank (for consistent pressure without cycling); (d) horsepower rating — 0.5HP (1-story, 1 bathroom), 1HP (2-story, 2–3 bathrooms), 1.5HP (large residential/commercial), 2HP+ (commercial/industrial); (e) power supply consideration — single-phase 220V (residential) vs. three-phase (commercial) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 4 | **Accessories & Piping Recommendation**: System generates complete bill of materials: (a) PVC pipes (PVC schedule 40) — sizes based on flow rate (typically ¾" to 1¼" for residential); (b) fittings — elbows, tees, couplings, adapters, threaded connectors; (c) float valve (mechanical or electronic) for automatic tank refill; (d) gate valves/ball valves for isolation; (e) check valve (non-return) to prevent backflow; (f) pressure tank (for automatic pump systems — eliminates pump cycling); (g) pressure gauge and pressure switch; (h) pump controller (automatic dry-run protection mandatory for well pumps); (i) electrical wiring, circuit breaker, and waterproof outdoor switch; (j) pipe sealant (Teflon tape, solvent cement); (k) flexible connector hoses; (l) water filter (sediment filter recommended for well systems) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 5 | **Customer Review, Quotation & Service Referral**: Customer reviews complete system recommendation with itemized pricing; Sales Associate: (a) presents total system cost and per-item breakdown; (b) offers installation service referral per W138 (plumber installation for complete system); (c) offers pipe cutting and threading service per W944 for custom pipe lengths; (d) creates POS quotation per W542 valid for 14 days; (e) quotation saved to customer loyalty account per W894; (f) for well pump installations: advises customer on NWRB well permit requirements and recommends licensed well driller referral | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Water system sizing calculator module (POS and mobile app) with PH-specific consumption factors
- Product catalog with tank dimensions, pump specifications, and compatibility matrix
- POS quotation module (W542) for complete system bill of materials
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)
- Installation service referral (W138) for plumber installation
- Pipe cutting service (W944) for custom pipe lengths
- Real-time inventory availability per store for bulky items (tanks, pumps)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Oversizing/undersizing risk**: Incorrect sizing wastes customer money (oversized pump/tank) or fails to deliver adequate water supply (undersized); calculator must use conservative PH residential consumption factors and account for peak simultaneous usage; Sales Associate training on PH plumbing standards per W51 mitigates
- **Power outage vulnerability**: Water pumps require electricity; during typhoons when power is frequently interrupted, pumped water systems fail; Sales Associate should recommend: (a) oversized tank for gravity-fed emergency supply; (b) generator compatibility for pump operation during outages per W913; (c) manual pump backup option for critical installations
- **Well water quality**: Shallow well water in PH may contain contaminants (bacteria, iron, manganese); Sales Associate should recommend water testing and filtration; BuildRight does not provide water testing services but can refer customers to accredited testing laboratories
- **Bulky item logistics**: Water tanks (especially 1000L+ sizes) and large pumps are bulky and heavy; store storage space is limited; delivery scheduling per W5D is mandatory for tanks above 500L; loading assistance per W950 required
- **Competitive pricing sensitivity**: Water tanks and pumps are commodity items with price-transparent online competition; BuildRight's value-add is the professional sizing service, complete system recommendation, and installation referral — not lowest price on individual items


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-schedule of "Customer Water Demand Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates recommended tank capacity" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-validate of "Accessories & Piping Recommendation" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: delivery scheduling per W5D mandatory for tanks above 500L; operational: professional sizing with complete system recommendation and installation referral — not lowest price on individual items

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Plumbing Specialist)**: Water system consultations consume ~6–14 hours/month (~0.25–0.5 hours/day) at 15–20 consultations/month; absorbed by existing Plumbing Department Sales Associate with plumbing product certification per W51
- **Training**: 12-hour water system sizing and pump selection certification per W51; includes TDH calculation, PH residential plumbing standards, and accessory cross-selling
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Water demand assessment: 8–12 min
- Tank sizing calculation: 3–5 min
- Pump sizing & selection: 5–8 min
- Accessories & piping recommendation: 5–8 min
- Customer review & quotation: 5–10 min
- **Total per consultation**: 26–43 min of staff time

## W985. Store-Level Customer Aircon Sizing & BTU/Horsepower Calculation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests aircon sizing recommendation at store appliance section or via mobile app |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–10,000 calculations/month chain-wide (~40–50/store/month); peaks March–May (PH summer) |
| **Volume** | 1–3 rooms per consultation; average 1.6 rooms |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Home Appliances) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Appliance Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Air conditioning is the highest-value appliance category for BuildRight Depot and a necessity in the Philippine tropical climate where temperatures regularly exceed 33°C with 70–90% humidity. Correct sizing is critical: an undersized aircon runs continuously without reaching target temperature (wasting electricity and shortening compressor life), while an oversized aircon cycles on/off too frequently (causing temperature swings, poor humidity removal, and wasted energy). Philippine residential rooms present unique sizing challenges: high ceilings (2.7–3.5m common in PH construction), large windows (poor insulation), concrete walls (thermal mass retention), and heat-generating appliances (kitchens). The Philippine market uses both BTU/hr (international) and HP/horsepower (local terminology) ratings interchangeably — 1HP ≈ 9,000 BTU, 1.5HP ≈ 12,000 BTU, 2HP ≈ 18,000 BTU. Inverter technology has transformed the market (30–50% energy savings over conventional units) but adds complexity to sizing recommendations. This service is a major differentiator during the March–May summer peak when aircon sales can account for 15–20% of total store revenue.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Room Assessment & Data Collection**: Sales Associate collects room parameters: (a) room dimensions — length × width × ceiling height (meters); (b) room type — bedroom, living room, kitchen, office, commercial space; (c) window area and orientation — north/south/east/west facing; window type (jalousie, sliding, awning, fixed glass); glass type (clear, tinted, reflective); presence of curtains/blinds; (d) wall material — concrete hollow block (CHB, most common in PH), wood frame, gypsum board partition; (e) insulation — none (typical PH residential), foam panel, fiberglass; (f) room occupancy — number of people typically in room; (g) heat sources — direct sunlight exposure, number of heat-generating appliances (refrigerator, oven, computer, TV); (h) floor level — ground floor, upper floor, top floor (roof heat gain); (i) existing electrical capacity — dedicated circuit available? Amperage of circuit breaker? | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 2 | **BTU/HP Calculation & Unit Recommendation**: System calculates: (a) base cooling load = room area (sqm) × 500 BTU/sqm (PH tropical baseline for typical residential); (b) adjustments — ceiling height > 2.7m (+10%), west-facing window (+10%), large window area > 40% of wall (+10%), kitchen/heat sources (+4,000 BTU), top floor under roof (+15%), poor insulation (+10%); each occupant beyond first (+600 BTU); (c) total required BTU = base load + adjustments; (d) recommended unit size — 0.5HP/5,000BTU (small bedroom ≤10sqm), 0.75HP/7,000BTU (bedroom 10–14sqm), 1HP/9,000–10,000BTU (bedroom 14–18sqm), 1.5HP/12,000BTU (master bedroom/living room 18–25sqm), 2HP/18,000BTU (living room 25–35sqm), 2.5HP/24,000BTU (large living room 35–50sqm), cassette/duct type ≥3HP (commercial); (e) unit type recommendation — window type (economy, easy installation, typical PH), split type (inverter, quieter, more efficient), portable (rental/temporary), floor-mounted (large rooms), cassette/duct (commercial); (f) inverter vs. conventional recommendation — inverter recommended for rooms used ≥8 hours/day (bedrooms, offices); conventional acceptable for intermittent use (living rooms, guest rooms) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Electrical Compatibility Check**: Sales Associate verifies: (a) required electrical load — unit wattage (typically 700W–2,500W depending on HP rating); (b) customer's circuit breaker amperage — standard PH residential circuit is 20A/30A; (c) dedicated circuit requirement — units ≥1.5HP require dedicated circuit; (d) wiring gauge — units ≥1HP require 2.0mm² AWG14 wire minimum; ≥2HP require 3.5mm² AWG12; (e) Meralco/utility provider demand — some areas have limited residential electrical capacity; (f) system flags if customer's electrical infrastructure is incompatible and recommends licensed electrician referral per W989 | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 4 | **Cross-Sell Accessories & Services**: Sales Associate recommends: (a) aircon installation service per W138 (professional installation with copper piping, drainage hose, and electrical connection); (b) wall bracket/concealed mount for split-type outdoor unit; (c) aircon cover (off-season protection during Nov–Feb when usage drops); (d) air filter replacement schedule (clean monthly, replace quarterly); (e) voltage regulator/AVR (recommended for areas with unstable voltage — common in provincial PH); (f) drain hose and condensate pump (for units without gravity drainage); (g) extended warranty registration per W935; (h) electrical supplies per W991 (wiring, circuit breaker, outlet) if customer needs electrical upgrade | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 5 | **Quotation & Order Processing**: Customer reviews recommendations; Sales Associate: (a) presents total cost including unit, accessories, and installation; (b) creates POS quotation per W542 valid for 7 days (aircon pricing is volatile during summer peak); (c) for split-type units: coordinates installation scheduling per W138 with 2–5 day lead time; (d) for window-type units: customer may self-install or request installation service; (e) processes sale or saves quotation to customer loyalty account per W894; (f) system registers unit serial number at POS for warranty activation per W33 | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Aircon sizing calculator module (POS and mobile app) with PH-specific heat load factors
- Product catalog with aircon specifications (BTU, HP, wattage, EER, dimensions, weight)
- Electrical compatibility checker module integrated with product catalog
- POS quotation module (W542) for itemized recommendation
- Installation service scheduling per W138
- Serial number registration at POS for warranty per W33 and product registration per W935
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)
- Real-time inventory availability with seasonal stock monitoring

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Summer stock-outs**: Aircon demand during March–May peak can exceed supply, especially for popular inverter models (1HP and 1.5HP); Category Manager must pre-position seasonal stock per W960; emergency procurement per W60 may be needed for unexpected demand spikes; Sales Associate must manage customer expectations on stock availability and delivery lead times
- **Electrical incompatibility**: Many PH homes (especially older construction) have insufficient electrical capacity for aircon installation; customers who purchase without checking may require costly electrical upgrades; the electrical compatibility check in Step 3 mitigates this risk
- **Installation quality**: Poor installation (undercharged refrigerant, kinked copper piping, improper drainage, inadequate sealing) causes performance issues and customer complaints; installation partner quality audit per W213 ensures installer standards
- **Price transparency**: Aircon prices are highly visible online (Lazada, Shopee, manufacturer websites); customers frequently compare prices on mobile phones while in-store; Sales Associate must emphasize BuildRight's value-add (professional sizing, installation service, warranty support, after-sales service) rather than competing purely on unit price
- **Return complexity**: Installed aircons cannot be easily returned; clear sizing verification in Steps 1–2 and customer sign-off before installation reduce return disputes; manufacturer defects handled through warranty per W33


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-collect of "Room Assessment & Data Collection" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "BTU/HP Calculation & Unit Recommendation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-validate of "Electrical Compatibility Check" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Home Appliances) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Appliance Specialist)**: Aircon consultations consume ~3–4 hours/day during summer peak (March–May), ~1 hour/day off-season; absorbed by existing Appliance Section Sales Associate with aircon product certification per W51
- **Training**: 8-hour aircon sizing and product knowledge certification per W51; includes heat load calculation, inverter technology, and PH electrical requirements
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Room assessment & data collection: 8–12 min
- BTU/HP calculation & recommendation: 3–5 min
- Electrical compatibility check: 3–5 min
- Cross-sell accessories & services: 5–8 min
- Quotation & order processing: 5–10 min
- **Total per consultation**: 24–40 min of staff time

## W987. Customer Fence & Gate Material Estimation & Design Recommendation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests fence/gate material estimation at store or via mobile app |
| **Frequency** | ~3,500–4,500 estimations/month chain-wide (~17–22/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1 fence/gate project per estimation; average perimeter 30–80 linear meters |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Steel/Hardware Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Property fencing is a near-universal requirement in the Philippines for security, boundary demarcation, and privacy. Unlike developed markets where pre-fabricated fence panels are standard, Philippine residential fencing is predominantly custom-built using hollow block (CHB) walls, steel picket fences, or a combination (CHB base with steel grill upper portion). Gate fabrication is almost entirely custom — made-to-measure steel swing gates or sliding gates that must match the fence style and opening width. The material estimation requires calculating posts (steel tubular or concrete), fencing material (CHB, steel bars, wire mesh, or pre-painted sheets), footings (concrete, rebar), gate frames, hinges, latches, and paint/finishing. Errors in estimation cause project delays (customer must return for additional materials) or over-purchasing (especially concrete and steel which are difficult to return). This service is particularly valuable for BuildRight's trade customer segment (30% of revenue) — contractors who quote fence/gate projects need accurate material lists and pricing to prepare competitive bids.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Scope & Site Intake**: Customer provides project details: (a) total fence perimeter (linear meters); (b) fence height (typical PH residential: 1.8m–2.4m); (c) fence type — CHB hollow block wall, steel picket/tubular, welded wire mesh on steel frame, pre-painted corrugated on steel frame, chain-link, or combination; (d) gate requirements — number of gates, gate opening width (pedestrian: 1.0–1.2m, vehicle: 3.0–5.0m), gate type (swing single, swing double, sliding); (e) ground conditions — flat, sloped, rocky (affects footing depth and concrete volume); (f) existing structures — retaining walls, existing posts; (g) style/aesthetic preference (decorative spear tops, flat top, arch top) | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Material Quantity Calculation**: System calculates: (a) posts — steel tubular posts (2" round or square) spaced at 2.0–2.5m centers; post count = perimeter ÷ spacing + 1; gate posts use heavier 3" or 4" tubular; post length = fence height + 0.6m footing depth; (b) horizontal rails — 2–3 runs per fence section (bottom, middle, top) in steel tubular (1" round or 1½" rectangular); total rail length = perimeter × number of runs; (c) infill material — steel pickets (quantity = section length ÷ picket spacing, typically 10–15cm); or CHB (blocks per sqm ≈ 12.5 for standard 8"×16"×4" at 10mm joint); or welded wire mesh (rolls in 25m lengths × fence height); (d) concrete for footings — post footing volume (0.3m depth × 0.2m diameter × post count) + gate post footing (0.6m depth × 0.3m diameter × gate post count); (e) rebar for footings — 2–3 pieces of 12mm rebar per post footing; (f) gate frame — steel tubular frame sized to opening, cross-braced; (g) gate hardware — heavy-duty hinges (2–3 per gate), latch/lock set, drop bolt (for double gates), gate wheel (for sliding gates), guide track and rollers (for sliding gates); (h) paint/finish — primer + 2 coats enamel paint, estimated liters based on total steel surface area; (i) miscellaneous — cement (for CHB mortar), sand, tie wire, welding rods (if customer fabricates on-site), bolts and anchors | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Gate Fabrication Recommendation**: For custom steel gates: (a) system generates gate frame cutting list — top rail, bottom rail, vertical stiles, diagonal braces; (b) if customer has welding capability: provide material list only; (c) if customer needs fabricated gate: offer BuildRight's welding/fabrication service per W988 with 3–5 day lead time; (d) gate design options — flat top, arch top, spear top (decorative), vertical bar, horizontal slat; (e) gate weight estimate for hinge/hardware selection | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 4 | **Cross-Sell Security & Automation Accessories**: Sales Associate recommends: (a) padlock or mortise lock for gate (hardware section); (b) automatic gate operator (sliding or swing) for vehicle gates; (c) solar-powered gate operator (for areas without electrical access to gate); (d) CCTV camera at gate entrance per W207; (e) outdoor lighting (solar flood lights, sensor lights) for fence perimeter; (f) anti-climb devices for high-security applications; (g) paint color matching per W981 for fence/gate aesthetic coordination | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 5 | **Quotation & Order Processing**: Customer reviews complete material list; Sales Associate: (a) presents itemized materials with quantities and pricing; (b) creates POS quotation per W542 valid for 14 days; (c) for large orders (CHB, cement, sand, steel): schedules delivery per W5D or W980 for construction site delivery; (d) offers rebar cutting/bending service per W986 for footing reinforcement; (e) offers welding/fabrication service per W988 for gate fabrication; (f) quotation saved to customer loyalty account per W894; (g) for trade customers: quotation can be converted to project quotation per W162 for bid submission | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Fence/gate estimation calculator module (POS and mobile app) with PH-standard fencing parameters
- Product catalog with fencing materials, steel tubular specifications, and gate hardware compatibility
- POS quotation module (W542) for itemized material list
- POS project quotation (W162) for trade customer bid submissions
- Delivery scheduling (W5D, W980) for heavy materials to construction site
- Rebar cutting/bending service (W986) for footing reinforcement
- Welding/fabrication service (W988) for gate fabrication
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)
- Real-time inventory availability for steel and construction materials

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Site condition variability**: Customer-described ground conditions frequently differ from actual (hardpan, water table, buried obstructions); Sales Associate must recommend +10% contingency on concrete and footing materials and advise customer to verify site conditions before purchasing full quantity
- **Gate sizing accuracy**: Gate opening measurements must be exact (±5mm) for proper gate fit; customer-provided measurements are often inaccurate; Sales Associate must emphasize precise measurement and recommend professional measurement per W442 for large/expensive gates
- **Structural adequacy**: Fence height, post spacing, and footing depth must be adequate for wind load (critical in typhoon-prone PH); Sales Associate cannot provide structural engineering advice (liability); must recommend consulting a structural engineer for fences >2.4m height or in high-wind zones per NSCP
- **Theft risk on construction site**: Steel and construction materials delivered to site are vulnerable to theft (common in PH construction); advise customer to schedule delivery close to installation date and secure materials on-site; mitigated by the Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) review and approval gate
- **Competitive pricing**: Fencing is a commodity market with many local steel suppliers offering lower prices; BuildRight's differentiation is the complete estimation service, gate fabrication, and delivery coordination


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-post of "Project Scope & Site Intake" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-capture of "Material Quantity Calculation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "generates gate frame cutting list — top rail, bottom rail, vertical stiles,…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Steel/Hardware Specialist)**: Fence estimations consume ~8–18 hours/month (~0.3–0.6 hours/day) at 17–22 consultations/month; absorbed by existing Lumber & Building Materials Sales Associate with fencing product certification per W51
- **Training**: 8-hour fence/gate material estimation and steel product knowledge certification per W51
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project scope & site intake: 10–15 min
- Material quantity calculation: 5–8 min
- Gate fabrication recommendation: 5–8 min
- Cross-sell accessories: 5–8 min
- Quotation & order processing: 5–10 min
- **Total per estimation**: 30–49 min of staff time

## W989. Customer Plumbing System Layout Design & Material Takeoff Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (homeowner, contractor, or plumber) requests plumbing layout design and material takeoff at store |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,000 consultations/month chain-wide (~10–15/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1 project per consultation; ranges from single-bathroom renovation to full-house plumbing |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Plumbing) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Plumbing Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Plumbing system design for new construction or renovation is a complex material estimation challenge that goes far beyond simple pipe length calculations. A complete residential plumbing system includes water supply lines (hot and cold), drainage/waste/vent (DWV) lines, fixture connections, water heater installation, and septic tank connection. Philippine plumbing follows the National Plumbing Code of the Philippines (NPCP) which mandates specific pipe sizes, vent configurations, and trap requirements. Customers — especially DIY homeowners and small contractors — frequently underestimate material requirements, leading to multiple store trips and project delays. A professional material takeoff service that generates a complete bill of materials based on the floor plan drives higher-value sales, reduces returns and exchanges, and positions BuildRight as a one-stop shop for plumbing projects. The service integrates with W984 (water tank/pump sizing), W944 (pipe cutting), and W138 (installation service referral) to create a complete plumbing project support ecosystem.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Scope & Floor Plan Intake**: Customer provides: (a) floor plan — hand-drawn sketch, builder's blueprint, or photo of floor plan showing fixture locations (toilet, lavatory, kitchen sink, shower, bathtub, washing machine, water heater, outdoor tap); (b) building type — single-story, two-story, three-story; (c) water source — municipal supply, water tank with pump per W984, deep well; (d) water heater type — tankless (point-of-use), storage tank (central), solar water heater; (e) project type — new construction, renovation (replacing existing plumbing), extension (adding bathroom/kitchen); (f) number of bathrooms, kitchen, laundry area, utility sink; Sales Associate marks fixture locations on system layout tool | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 12–18 min |
| 2 | **Plumbing Layout Design & Pipe Routing**: System generates: (a) water supply layout — main supply line from source (typically 1" PVC or PPR) branching to ¾" lines to bathrooms and kitchen, then ½" branches to individual fixtures; (b) drainage layout — 3" or 4" main drain line from toilets, 2" from showers and sinks, with cleanout access points at code-required intervals; (c) vent layout — main vent stack (2" minimum) through roof, with auxiliary vents per NPCP requirements (within 1.5m trap arm distance); (d) hot water supply routing (if water heater specified) — ½" PPR or CPVC with insulated runs for energy efficiency; (e) fixture connection points — exact locations for shut-off valves, angle stops, P-traps, and supply adapters | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Material Takeoff & Quantity Calculation**: System calculates complete bill of materials: (a) supply pipes — PVC schedule 40 or PPR (PN20), lengths by diameter (½", ¾", 1"); (b) DWV pipes — PVC schedule 40, lengths by diameter (1½", 2", 3", 4"); (c) fittings — elbows (90° and 45°), tees, couplings, reducers, adaptors, by pipe material and diameter; (d) valves — gate valves (main shut-off), angle stops (per fixture), check valves (if water pump per W984); (e) fixtures — toilets (close-coupled or wall-hung), lavatories, kitchen sinks, shower sets (per W997 compatibility check); (f) water heater and accessories per customer selection; (g) drain components — P-traps, sanitary tees, cleanouts, closet flanges; (h) sealants — solvent cement (PVC), Teflon tape, plumber's putty, silicone sealant; (i) support/hangers — pipe clamps, brackets, stud anchors; (j) insulation — foam pipe insulation for hot water lines; (k) septic tank connection pipe and fittings (per W998 if new construction) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Code Compliance Review & Recommendations**: Sales Associate reviews: (a) NPCP compliance — vent stack requirements, trap arm distances, pipe sizing adequacy, cleanout placement; (b) recommends upgrades — PPR over PVC for hot water lines (higher temperature rating), water hammer arrestors for washing machine/dishwasher connections, accessible shut-off valves for maintenance, backflow preventer if connected to municipal supply; (c) for multi-story buildings — pressure reducing valve may be needed for upper floors, booster pump per W984 for insufficient municipal pressure; (d) advises customer that plumbing layout is a material estimation aid, not a stamped engineering drawing; recommends licensed plumber for installation per W138 | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Quotation & Order Processing**: Customer reviews complete material list with pricing; Sales Associate: (a) presents itemized materials grouped by category (supply, DWV, fixtures, accessories); (b) offers pipe cutting service per W944 for custom-length PVC/PPR pipes; (c) offers fixture compatibility check per W997 to ensure all bathroom/kitchen fixtures work together; (d) offers water tank/pump sizing per W984 if customer needs storage/distribution system; (e) creates POS quotation per W542 valid for 14 days; (f) for large orders: schedules delivery per W5D for heavy/bulky items; (g) quotation saved to customer loyalty account per W894 | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Plumbing layout design tool (POS and mobile app) with drag-and-drop fixture placement
- Material takeoff engine with NPCP-compliant pipe sizing and fitting count logic
- Product catalog with plumbing fixture specifications and compatibility matrix
- POS quotation module (W542) for itemized bill of materials
- Pipe cutting service integration (W944) for custom pipe lengths
- Fixture compatibility checker (W997) for bathroom/kitchen bundles
- Water tank/pump sizing integration (W984) for water distribution systems
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)
- Real-time inventory availability for plumbing materials

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Design accuracy**: The system-generated layout is an estimation tool, not a replacement for a professional plumber's as-built layout; wall chases, slab penetrations, and existing pipe routing may differ from assumed paths; Sales Associate must clearly communicate that quantities are estimates and recommend purchasing 5–10% contingency on pipes and fittings
- **Code compliance disclaimer**: BuildRight is not providing professional plumbing engineering services; the material takeoff is a customer convenience and must not be represented as code-compliant design; licensed plumber sign-off is required for building permit applications per LGU requirements per W54
- **Material substitution**: If specific brands or models are out of stock, Sales Associate must recommend equivalent alternatives with matching specifications (pipe diameter, pressure rating, temperature rating); substitution without proper specification matching can cause installation failures
- **Return volume**: Plumbing projects generate higher return rates than typical merchandise due to estimation overages and design changes; clear return policy communication (30-day return for unused PVC/PPR pipes and fittings per W12) and accurate estimation reduce return friction


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "blueprint, or photo of floor plan showing fixture locations" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "Material Takeoff & Quantity Calculation" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-capture of "preventer if connected to municipal supply" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: licensed plumber sign-off required for building permit applications per LGU requirements per W54

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Plumbing Specialist)**: Plumbing consultations consume ~5–9 hours/month (~0.2–0.3 hours/day) at 10–15 consultations/month; absorbed by existing Plumbing Department Sales Associate with plumbing product certification per W51
- **Training**: 16-hour plumbing system design and NPCP basics certification per W51; includes pipe sizing, fixture specification, and material takeoff techniques
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project scope & floor plan intake: 12–18 min
- Layout design & pipe routing: 5–10 min
- Material takeoff & quantity calculation: 3–5 min
- Code compliance review: 5–10 min
- Quotation & order processing: 5–10 min
- **Total per consultation**: 30–53 min of staff time
