## W990. Customer Solar Panel System Sizing & ROI Calculator Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer inquires about solar panel system at store or via mobile app |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 consultations/month chain-wide (~7–12/store/month); growing 30–40% annually |
| **Volume** | 1 system per consultation |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Electrical) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Electrical Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

The Philippines has among the highest residential electricity rates in Southeast Asia (PHP 9–12/kWh average, higher than Thailand, Vietnam, Indonesia), making solar energy increasingly attractive for homeowners and businesses. The Net Metering program under the Renewable Energy Act (RA 9513) allows grid-tied solar systems to export excess energy back to the utility (Meralco, VECO, Davao Light, etc.) at a feed-in tariff, effectively spinning the meter backward. However, solar system sizing is complex — it requires analyzing the customer's electricity consumption, roof orientation and shading, available mounting area, local solar irradiance (averaging 4.5–5.5 kWh/m²/day in the Philippines), and financial parameters (system cost, electricity rate trajectory, payback period, ROI). BuildRight sells solar panels (mono/poly-crystalline, 400W–550W), inverters (string and micro-inverter), mounting structures, batteries (optional for hybrid systems), and electrical accessories. This consultation service drives high-value sales (residential systems average PHP 80,000–300,000) and positions BuildRight as a solar energy partner rather than just a component supplier.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Energy Consumption Assessment**: Sales Associate collects: (a) average monthly electricity bill (PHP and kWh) — from customer's utility bill; (b) peak monthly consumption (typically March–May due to aircon usage per W985); (c) electricity rate per kWh from utility; (d) roof type — metal GI sheet (most common in PH, easiest mounting), concrete slab, clay tile; (e) roof orientation — north-facing, south-facing (optimal in PH), east/west-facing, flat; (f) available roof area (sqm) — unshaded area between 9AM–3PM; (g) roof condition and structural adequacy for panel weight (~15 kg/m²); (h) building type — single-story residential, multi-story, commercial; (i) 3-phase or single-phase electrical supply | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **System Sizing Calculation**: System calculates: (a) system size (kWp) = monthly consumption (kWh) ÷ 30 days ÷ peak sun hours (4.5 for PH) ÷ system efficiency (0.75–0.80 derating factor for temperature, inverter, wiring losses); example: 500 kWh/month → 500 ÷ 30 ÷ 4.5 ÷ 0.77 ≈ 4.8 kWp system; (b) number of panels = system size ÷ panel wattage (500W panels: 4,800W ÷ 500W ≈ 10 panels); (c) required roof area = panels × panel area (2.0m² per 500W panel) = 20 sqm minimum; (d) inverter size = system size × 1.0–1.2 (slight oversizing for peak production); (e) annual energy production = system size × peak sun hours × 365 × derating = estimated kWh/year; (f) self-consumption ratio — estimated % of solar energy consumed directly vs. exported to grid; (g) battery sizing (optional hybrid system) = critical loads × desired backup hours | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Financial ROI Analysis**: System calculates: (a) total system cost = panels + inverter + mounting structure + electrical accessories + installation labor (per W138); (b) annual savings = annual production × self-consumption ratio × electricity rate + annual export × feed-in tariff; (c) simple payback period = total cost ÷ annual savings (typical PH residential: 4–6 years); (d) 25-year net savings = annual savings × 25 years × electricity rate escalation (3–5%/year assumed) − total cost − maintenance costs (inverter replacement at year 10–12); (e) internal rate of return (IRR); (f) comparison table: cash purchase vs. bank financing (partner bank per W966) with monthly amortization vs. monthly savings; (g) environmental impact — CO₂ avoided = annual production × 0.6 kg CO₂/kWh (PH grid emission factor) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Product Selection & Quotation**: Sales Associate presents: (a) system components — panels (Tier 1 brand recommendation), inverter (string inverter for simple roofs, micro-inverter for complex/shaded roofs), mounting kit, AC/DC disconnect, circuit breaker, cables and connectors; (b) optional battery bank for hybrid/off-grid capability; (c) total system cost with itemized pricing; (d) installation service referral per W138 with certified solar installer; (e) net metering application assistance — advise customer on utility net metering application process (BuildRight provides checklist and required technical documents but does not file the application); (f) LGU electrical permit requirements per W54; (g) creates POS quotation per W542 valid for 30 days (solar equipment pricing is relatively stable) | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 5 | **Documentation & Follow-Up**: For customers who proceed: (a) system generates technical summary document with system specifications, layout diagram, and ROI analysis for customer's records; (b) Sales Associate schedules site assessment per W442 (technical survey for roof condition, shading analysis, and electrical panel compatibility) before final installation commitment; (c) quotation saved to customer loyalty account per W894; (d) system registers follow-up task for Sales Associate to contact customer in 7 days if quotation not converted to sale; (e) for net metering customers: provides utility application checklist and list of required documents | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Solar system sizing calculator module (POS and mobile app) with PH-specific solar irradiance data
- Financial ROI analysis engine with configurable electricity rate and escalation parameters
- Product catalog with solar panel, inverter, and battery specifications and compatibility matrix
- POS quotation module (W542) for itemized system cost
- Site assessment scheduling per W442
- Installation service referral per W138 with certified solar installer
- Construction loan referral per W966 for financing option
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)
- LGU permit guidance per W54

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Roof structural adequacy**: Solar panels add ~15 kg/m² to the roof; older PH residential roofs (especially those with lightweight trusses) may not support the additional load without reinforcement; site assessment per W442 is mandatory before installation commitment; structural engineer referral recommended for roofs >10 years old
- **Shading losses**: Even partial shading of one panel can significantly reduce string inverter system output; micro-inverter systems mitigate but increase cost; accurate shading analysis during site assessment is critical for setting realistic production expectations
- **Utility interconnection delays**: Net metering applications with Meralco and other utilities can take 30–90 days for approval and meter installation; customers must be informed that savings only begin after interconnection is complete; BuildRight cannot control utility timelines
- **After-sales support**: Solar systems are 25-year investments; customers expect ongoing support for warranty claims per W33, inverter replacements, and system monitoring; BuildRight must maintain qualified solar service partner network per W213
- **Regulatory changes**: The Renewable Energy Act and net metering rules may evolve; ERC (Energy Regulatory Commission) and DOE regulations may affect feed-in tariffs and interconnection requirements; Category Manager must monitor regulatory environment per W114


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-collect of "Energy Consumption Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "System Sizing Calculation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-export of "export × feed-in tariff" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: before installation commitment; operational: output; operational: monitoring

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Electrical Specialist)**: Solar consultations consume ~4–10 hours/month (~0.1–0.3 hours/day) at 7–12 consultations/month; absorbed by existing Electrical Department Sales Associate with solar product certification per W51
- **Training**: 24-hour solar system design and sales certification per W51; includes solar PV fundamentals, PH net metering regulations, ROI calculation, and sales techniques
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Energy consumption assessment: 10–15 min
- System sizing calculation: 5–8 min
- Financial ROI analysis: 3–5 min
- Product selection & quotation: 8–12 min
- Documentation & follow-up: 5–8 min
- **Total per consultation**: 31–48 min of staff time

## W991. Customer Electrical Circuit Design & Residential Load Planning Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests electrical circuit planning assistance for new construction or renovation |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,000 consultations/month chain-wide (~10–15/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1 project per consultation |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Electrical) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Electrical Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Electrical wiring is a core category for BuildRight Depot, with copper wire, circuit breakers, panels, switches, and outlets representing a significant revenue segment. Philippine residential electrical design follows the Philippine Electrical Code (PEC), which mandates specific circuit configurations, wire sizing, grounding requirements, and safety devices. Typical PH residential construction requires 6–12 dedicated circuits (lighting, convenience outlets, aircon, kitchen, water heater, etc.) with proper wire sizing (THHN/THWN copper wire in EMT conduit or PVC modular). Customers — especially DIY homeowners and small contractors — frequently underestimate wire lengths, forget accessories (junction boxes, connectors, ground rods), or select incorrect wire gauge (a fire hazard). A professional circuit design and material takeoff service increases safety, drives complete-circuit sales (rather than piecemeal purchases), and reduces return trips. The service integrates with W985 (aircon sizing) for aircon circuit requirements and W990 (solar panel system) for solar interconnection.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Building Assessment & Load Inventory**: Sales Associate collects: (a) floor plan with room layout (hand-drawn or photo); (b) number of rooms and room types (bedrooms, living room, kitchen, bathrooms, garage, laundry); (c) planned appliances and equipment per room — aircon (per W985 with HP rating), water heater, electric stove/induction cooker, refrigerator, washing machine, microwave, water pump (per W984 with HP rating), lighting fixtures, number of convenience outlets per room; (d) building type — single-story, two-story; (e) service entrance — overhead or underground, existing panel amperage (typically 60A or 100A for PH residential); (f) project type — new construction, renovation, extension | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Circuit Design & Load Calculation**: System designs circuits per PEC guidelines: (a) lighting circuits — 1 circuit per 800W of lighting load, 2.0mm² THHN wire (minimum), 15A circuit breaker; (b) convenience outlet circuits — 1 circuit per 6 outlets maximum, 2.0mm² THHN wire, 20A circuit breaker, GFCI protection for wet areas (bathrooms, kitchen, outdoor); (c) dedicated appliance circuits — aircon: 1 circuit per unit, 3.5mm² THHN for 1HP, 5.5mm² THHN for 2HP, 30A circuit breaker; water heater: dedicated circuit, 3.5mm² THHN, 30A circuit breaker; electric stove: dedicated circuit, 8.0mm² THHN, 40–50A circuit breaker; water pump: dedicated circuit per W984 pump HP rating; (d) total connected load (watts) and computed load per PEC demand factors; (e) main breaker and panel size recommendation — 100A panel for standard residential, 200A for large residential with multiple aircon units; (f) service entrance wire size — based on computed load and distance from utility meter | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Material Takeoff & Quantity Calculation**: System generates complete bill of materials: (a) wire — THHN/THWN copper wire by gauge (2.0mm², 3.5mm², 5.5mm², 8.0mm²) with estimated lengths per circuit × 1.15 waste factor; (b) circuit breakers — quantity and amperage per circuit design, including main breaker; (c) panel board — load center with sufficient breaker slots; (d) conduit — PVC conduit or EMT by diameter with fittings; (e) junction boxes — quantity by type (round, square, octagonal); (f) switches — single-pole, 3-way, 4-way by quantity per room; (g) outlets/convenience outlets — standard, GFCI (wet areas), grounded, by quantity; (h) connectors and accessories — wire nuts, cable ties, ground rod, ground wire, terminal lugs, EMT connectors, PVC cement, electrical tape; (i) safety devices — surge protector, AFCI breakers (recommended for bedrooms); (j) lighting fixtures — quantity per room (if customer selects) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Code Compliance Review & Safety Recommendations**: Sales Associate reviews: (a) PEC compliance — circuit breaker sizing, wire gauge adequacy, grounding requirements (8ft ground rod mandatory per PEC), GFCI placement in wet areas; (b) recommends safety upgrades — whole-house surge protector (critical in typhoon-prone PH with frequent lightning), AFCI breakers for bedrooms (arc fault protection), smoke detectors per floor, CO detector if gas appliances present; (c) advises customer that material list is a planning aid, not a professional electrical engineering design; recommends licensed electrician for installation per W138 and PEC compliance certification; (d) for renovation projects: advises on aluminum-to-copper transition safety and knob-and-tube wiring hazards (common in older PH homes) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 5 | **Quotation & Order Processing**: Customer reviews complete material list; Sales Associate: (a) presents itemized materials grouped by circuit; (b) offers wire cutting service for custom-length wire runs; (c) offers lighting fixture selection per W997 for bathroom/kitchen fixture coordination; (d) creates POS quotation per W542 valid for 14 days; (e) for large orders (full-house rewiring): schedules delivery per W5D for heavy items (panel, conduit, wire rolls); (f) quotation saved to customer loyalty account per W894 | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Electrical circuit design tool (POS and mobile app) with PEC-compliant circuit sizing engine
- Material takeoff engine with wire length estimation and conduit routing logic
- Product catalog with electrical material specifications and compatibility matrix
- Aircon circuit requirement integration per W985
- Water pump circuit requirement integration per W984
- Solar interconnection integration per W990
- POS quotation module (W542) for itemized bill of materials
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)
- Real-time inventory availability for electrical materials

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Fire safety liability**: Incorrect wire gauge or circuit breaker sizing creates fire hazard; Sales Associate must clearly communicate that material recommendations are based on standard PEC guidelines and that a licensed electrician must verify and approve all installations; disclaimer approved by Legal per W230
- **Concealed wiring estimation**: Wire length estimates are based on straight-line routing with 15% waste factor; actual wire runs through walls, ceilings, and conduit bends may differ significantly; customer must understand that wire lengths are estimates and additional wire may be needed during installation
- **Aluminum vs. copper**: Some older PH homes have aluminum wiring; mixing aluminum and copper (without proper CO/ALR connectors) creates connection failure risk; Sales Associate must ask about existing wire material and recommend proper transition connectors
- **Utility coordination**: Service entrance upgrades (from 60A to 100A or 200A panel) require coordination with the utility provider (Meralco, VECO, etc.); customer must file a service upgrade request with the utility; BuildRight provides materials only


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-collect of "Building Assessment & Load Inventory" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-capture of "center with sufficient breaker slots" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-review of "Review & Safety Recommendations" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Electrical) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Electrical Specialist)**: Electrical consultations consume ~5–12 hours/month (~0.2–0.4 hours/day) at 10–15 consultations/month; absorbed by existing Electrical Department Sales Associate with electrical product certification per W51
- **Training**: 12-hour residential electrical design and PEC basics certification per W51; includes circuit sizing, wire gauge selection, and safety device recommendations
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Building assessment & load inventory: 10–15 min
- Circuit design & load calculation: 5–10 min
- Material takeoff & quantity calculation: 3–5 min
- Code compliance review: 5–10 min
- Quotation & order processing: 5–10 min
- **Total per consultation**: 28–50 min of staff time

## W992. Customer Garden & Landscape Design Consultation & Material Estimation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests garden/landscape design assistance at store garden section or via mobile app |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,000 consultations/month chain-wide (~10–15/store/month); peaks March–May (PH summer) |
| **Volume** | 1 project per consultation |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Garden & Outdoor) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Garden Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

The Garden & Outdoor category, while representing only 3% of active SKUs (~1,050 items), is a high-growth segment with strong cross-sell potential to higher-margin categories (pavers, outdoor lighting, irrigation, furniture, paint). Philippine residential garden projects range from simple container gardening on balconies (condominium dwellers) to full landscape design with hardscaping (pavers, retaining walls, water features) and softscaping (plants, trees, turf, ground cover). The tropical Philippine climate is ideal for year-round gardening, but also presents challenges — intense monsoon rains, typhoons, and high humidity affect plant selection and drainage design. Customers typically underestimate the scope of a landscaping project (drainage, soil preparation, irrigation) and the total material cost. A professional design consultation with material estimation drives larger, more complete purchases and differentiates BuildRight from standalone garden centers (which are rare in provincial PH).

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Scope & Site Assessment**: Customer provides: (a) garden area dimensions (length × width in meters); (b) current condition — bare soil, lawn, existing plants, concrete/paved; (c) sun exposure — full sun (6+ hours), partial shade (3–6 hours), full shade (<3 hours); (d) soil type — clay (common in PH), sandy, loam, rocky (customer observation or soil test kit); (e) drainage — standing water after rain, good drainage, sloped; (f) project type — new garden, garden renovation, container/balcony garden, vegetable garden, ornamental garden, xeriscaping (drought-resistant); (g) budget range; (h) maintenance commitment — low (weekend gardener), medium, high (daily attention); (i) existing structures — pergola, fence per W987, walkway, patio | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 2 | **Garden Design & Plant Selection**: Sales Associate designs: (a) functional zones — patio/seating area, planting beds, walkway, lawn area, vegetable patch, utility area (compost, hose storage); (b) hardscape plan — paver type (concrete pavers, natural stone, permeable pavers), walkway width (0.9–1.2m), edging material, retaining wall (if slope requires); (c) softscape plan — plant selection based on PH hardiness, sun exposure, and maintenance level: shade trees (narra, acacia, fire tree), ornamental shrubs (bougainvillea, ixora, santan), ground cover (carabao grass, Korean grass), flowering plants (gumamela, sampaguita, orchids), vegetables (tomato, chili, pechay, okra); (d) irrigation plan — drip irrigation system (water-efficient), sprinkler heads, timer/controller, rain sensor; (e) drainage plan — French drain or surface drainage to prevent standing water during monsoon | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 3 | **Material Takeoff & Quantity Calculation**: System calculates: (a) hardscape materials — pavers (area ÷ paver coverage + 5% waste), edging (linear meters), base material (gravel/sand per sqm depth), polymeric sand (for joints), retaining wall blocks (if applicable); (b) soil amendments — garden soil (cubic meters based on bed area × 0.15m depth), compost/organic matter, fertilizer (NPK ratio based on plant selection); (c) plants — quantity based on spacing requirements per species; (d) irrigation — drip line length, emitter quantity, connector fittings, timer, filter, pressure regulator; (e) landscape fabric (sqm for weed barrier under pavers and planting beds); (f) mulch (cubic meters based on bed area × 0.05m depth); (g) accessories — garden hose, hose reel, sprinkler, pruning shears, gloves, pots and planters (for container garden) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Cross-Sell Outdoor Living Products**: Sales Associate recommends: (a) outdoor lighting — solar path lights, garden spotlights, string lights; (b) outdoor furniture — garden bench, dining set, lounge chairs; (c) water feature — fountain kit, small pond liner, water pump per W984; (d) pest control — organic insecticide, snail bait, mosquito control; (e) garden tools — shovel, rake, trowel, wheelbarrow, pruning saw; (f) vertical garden system (for small spaces/condominiums); (g) shade structure — pergola kit, shade sail, umbrella | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 5 | **Quotation & Order Processing**: Customer reviews design and material list; Sales Associate: (a) presents itemized materials grouped by category (hardscape, softscape, irrigation, accessories); (b) creates POS quotation per W542 valid for 14 days; (c) for hardscape materials (pavers, gravel, sand): schedules delivery per W5D; (d) offers garden installation service referral per W138 (landscaping contractor); (e) offers fencing service per W987 if garden boundary fencing needed; (f) quotation saved to customer loyalty account per W894; (g) provides seasonal planting calendar specific to customer's PH region | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Garden design tool (POS and mobile app) with drag-and-drop zone layout
- Plant database with PH hardiness zones, sun requirements, spacing, and mature size
- Material takeoff engine with hardscape and softscape quantity calculations
- Product catalog with garden/outdoor product specifications
- POS quotation module (W542) for itemized material list
- Delivery scheduling (W5D) for heavy hardscape materials
- Installation service referral (W138) for landscaping
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)
- Seasonal planting calendar per PH region

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Plant survival guarantee**: BuildRight does not guarantee plant survival after purchase; plants are living products affected by customer care, weather, soil conditions, and pest damage; Sales Associate must provide care instructions and recommend appropriate soil amendments and fertilizers; returns only accepted within 7 days for plants that arrive damaged from transport
- **Seasonal plant availability**: Tropical plants have varying availability throughout the year; specific species may be out of stock; Sales Associate must offer equivalent alternatives and explain seasonal planting windows
- **Drainage issues**: Poor drainage is the #1 cause of garden project failure in PH (monsoon flooding kills plants and damages hardscape); drainage assessment must be thorough in Step 1; French drain or surface drainage must be included in material list if any standing water risk exists
- **Scope creep**: Garden projects tend to expand as customers see the full material list; Sales Associate must anchor to customer's stated budget and offer phased implementation (Phase 1: hardscape and irrigation; Phase 2: planting and finishing) to avoid sticker shock


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-test of "Project Scope & Site Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-post of "compost, hose storage" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "Material Takeoff & Quantity Calculation" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Garden & Outdoor) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Garden Specialist)**: Garden consultations consume ~6–14 hours/month (~0.2–0.5 hours/day) at 10–15 consultations/month; absorbed by existing Garden & Outdoor Department Sales Associate with garden product certification per W51
- **Training**: 8-hour garden design and PH plant knowledge certification per W51; includes tropical plant selection, hardscape basics, and irrigation design
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project scope & site assessment: 10–15 min
- Garden design & plant selection: 10–15 min
- Material takeoff & quantity calculation: 5–8 min
- Cross-sell outdoor living products: 5–8 min
- Quotation & order processing: 5–10 min
- **Total per consultation**: 35–56 min of staff time

## W993. Customer Construction Project Timeline & Phased Material Delivery Planner

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Trade/contractor customer requests phased material delivery planning for a construction project |
| **Frequency** | ~1,000–1,500 plans/month chain-wide (~5–7/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1 project per plan; projects range from PHP 200,000 to PHP 5,000,000+ in total material purchases |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Trade Desk), Customer (Contractor), Category Manager, Supply Chain Planner, Delivery Coordinator |

### Background

Construction projects for BuildRight's trade and corporate customers (30% of revenue) require multiple material deliveries over weeks or months, phased to match construction milestones. A typical two-story residential construction project in the Philippines follows a predictable sequence: (1) site preparation and excavation, (2) foundation and footing (rebar, cement, gravel, sand), (3) structural framing (steel, CHB, cement), (4) roofing (GI sheets, framing), (5) plumbing rough-in (pipes, fittings), (6) electrical rough-in (wire, conduit, panel), (7) masonry and finishing (tiles, plaster, paint), (8) fixtures and fittings (toilets, faucets, switches, outlets), (9) final touches (doors, cabinets, hardware). Each phase requires specific materials delivered just-in-time to avoid on-site storage issues (theft, weather damage, space constraints in urban lots). Without a phased delivery plan, contractors either: (a) purchase materials ad-hoc, making multiple trips to BuildRight stores (wasting labor hours), or (b) order everything at once, creating on-site chaos and material loss. A phased delivery planner locks in material pricing, guarantees stock availability, optimizes delivery truck utilization, and creates a multi-month revenue commitment from the customer — a significant competitive advantage over competitors who sell transactionally.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Scope & Timeline Intake**: Contractor provides: (a) project type — residential (single-story, two-story), commercial, renovation; (b) project scope — floor area (sqm), number of floors, number of rooms/bathrooms; (c) estimated project duration — start date and target completion date; (d) construction method — CHB conventional (most common in PH), pre-fab, steel frame; (e) quality level — economy (basic finishes), standard (mid-range), premium (high-end); (f) project location and site access constraints (narrow street, no parking, height restrictions); (g) existing customer account or new trade account application per W24 | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 15–20 min |
| 2 | **Construction Phase Mapping**: System maps material requirements to construction phases: (a) Phase 1 — Foundation: rebar (per W986 cutting/bending), cement, sand, gravel, anchor bolts, formwork lumber, polyethylene sheeting (vapor barrier); (b) Phase 2 — Structural: CHB hollow blocks, cement, rebar, tie wire, structural steel beams, metal deck (if applicable); (c) Phase 3 — Roofing: GI sheets (per W983), purlins, ridge cap, insulation, roofing screws, gutters; (d) Phase 4 — Plumbing rough-in: PVC/PPR pipes, fittings, fixtures (per W989 layout), water tank (per W984); (e) Phase 5 — Electrical rough-in: wire, conduit, panel, breakers, boxes (per W991 design); (f) Phase 6 — Masonry & Finishing: tiles (per W963), tile adhesive, grout, plaster, skim coat, paint (per W981 color matching); (g) Phase 7 — Fixtures & Fittings: toilets, faucets, switches, outlets, doors, locks, cabinets; (h) Phase 8 — Final: appliances (aircon per W985), lighting, hardware, accessories | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Delivery Schedule Optimization**: System generates delivery schedule: (a) assigns each phase a delivery date based on construction timeline (Phase 1: week 1, Phase 2: weeks 2–4, Phase 3: weeks 4–5, etc.); (b) groups small phases that can share a delivery truck (e.g., Phase 4 plumbing + Phase 5 electrical may overlap); (c) calculates delivery truck requirements per phase — weight, volume, and special handling (steel and cement require flatbed or 10-wheeler; tiles require covered truck); (d) identifies multi-drop opportunities for urban projects with limited on-site storage; (e) optimizes delivery route if multiple projects share delivery windows in same geographic area; (f) flags potential DC inventory constraints for imported materials (tiles, fixtures) with longer lead times | System / Supply Chain Planner | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 4 | **Pricing, Payment Terms & Contract**: Sales Associate presents: (a) total project material cost with per-phase breakdown; (b) price lock terms — pricing guaranteed for 60 days from contract signing (beyond 60 days: price adjustment clause for steel and cement which are commodity-priced); (c) payment terms — per-delivery payment (standard) or project credit facility per W24 for qualifying trade accounts; (d) deposit requirement — 20% of Phase 1 material cost to confirm project and reserve stock; (e) volume discount — if total project material cost exceeds PHP 500,000, tiered discount applied per trade pricing matrix per W163; (f) creates project quotation per W162 with phased delivery schedule as annex; (g) contractor signs delivery acceptance agreement | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 5 | **Order Execution & Ongoing Coordination**: After contract signing: (a) system creates blanket purchase order per customer with phased release triggers; (b) 3 days before each phase delivery date: system automatically sends reminder to contractor and confirms delivery; (c) contractor confirms or adjusts delivery date (±3 days flexibility); (d) system triggers DC pick/pack/ship per W106 for confirmed delivery; (e) on delivery day: driver calls contractor 30 minutes before arrival; goods receipt confirmed by contractor per W18; (f) system invoices delivered materials per phase; (g) after each phase delivery, Sales Associate follows up to confirm material adequacy and discuss next phase adjustments per W954 | Sales Associate / Supply Chain Planner / Delivery Coordinator | Department Supervisor | Ongoing (5–10 min per phase) |

### System Touchpoints
- Project timeline planner module (POS and trade desk) with construction phase templates
- Material requirement mapping engine with per-phase bill of materials
- Delivery scheduling optimization with truck capacity and route planning
- POS project quotation module (W162) with phased delivery annex
- Blanket purchase order management with phase release triggers
- DC pick/pack/ship integration per W106
- Customer goods receipt confirmation per W18
- Trade account credit management per W24
- Project pricing matrix per W163
- Delivery quality follow-up per W954
- Customer loyalty account integration for project history (W894)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Construction timeline slippage**: PH construction projects frequently run behind schedule due to weather delays (rainy season), labor availability, permit delays, and material sourcing issues; delivery dates must be adjustable with 3-day advance notice; system must handle date changes without losing price lock or stock reservation
- **Scope changes**: Contractors frequently modify project scope mid-construction (owner changes mind, site conditions differ from plan); phased delivery plan must be flexible to add/remove materials per phase; change order process per W162 handles modifications
- **On-site storage and theft**: Construction materials delivered to PH urban sites are vulnerable to theft (especially copper wire, power tools, and fixtures); advise contractor to schedule deliveries close to installation date and maintain on-site security per W71
- **Cash flow management**: Large project orders represent significant cash flow commitment from both customer and BuildRight; per-delivery billing spreads the financial burden; credit limit monitoring per W328 prevents over-exposure
- **Weather disruptions**: During rainy season (June–November), outdoor deliveries (cement, steel, CHB) may be delayed by flooding or impassable roads; system must have weather contingency logic and backup delivery dates


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "account or new trade account application per W24" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-match of "Construction Phase Mapping" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates delivery truck requirements per phase — weight, volume, and special…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must handle date changes without losing price lock or stock reservation; operational: must have weather contingency logic and backup delivery dates

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Trade Desk)**: Project planning consumes ~1–2 hours per project plan; at 5–7 plans/month per store, this averages ~10–30 min/day; absorbed by existing Department Supervisor or designated Trade Desk Sales Associate per W112
- **Supply Chain Planner**: DC-level coordination for phased deliveries adds ~2–3 hours/week of planning effort across all stores served by the DC; absorbed by existing supply planning team
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project scope & timeline intake: 15–20 min
- Construction phase mapping: 10–15 min (automated)
- Delivery schedule optimization: 10–15 min
- Pricing, payment & contract: 10–15 min
- Ongoing coordination: 5–10 min per phase
- **Total initial planning**: 45–65 min of staff time per project

## W997. Customer Bathroom/Kitchen Fixture Compatibility Checker & Bundle Builder

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer selects bathroom or kitchen fixtures and needs compatibility verification or wants to build a complete fixture bundle |
| **Frequency** | ~4,000–6,000 checks/month chain-wide (~20–30/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–3 fixtures per compatibility check; 1 complete bundle per bundle builder session |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Plumbing) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Plumbing/Fixture Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Bathroom and kitchen fixture purchases are high-value, high-anxiety transactions where compatibility errors are costly and disruptive. A customer who purchases a wall-mounted toilet only to discover during installation that their existing plumbing is floor-mounted, or buys a faucet that doesn't fit their sink's hole configuration, faces expensive rework and project delays. Philippine residential construction has widely varying plumbing standards — older homes may have galvanized iron pipes with different thread types than modern PVC/PPR systems, bathroom layouts vary significantly, and fixture brands use different mounting standards. BuildRight sells fixtures from multiple brands across a wide price range, and ensuring compatibility between toilet, lavatory, faucet, shower set, and accessories is a critical customer service. The bundle builder capability drives higher-value sales by presenting complete, compatible fixture sets with a single price, reducing the likelihood of customers purchasing individual items from competitors.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Fixture Selection & Compatibility Check**: Customer selects fixture(s) — toilet (close-coupled, wall-hung, floor-mounted, one-piece, two-piece), lavatory/basin (counter-top, under-mount, wall-hung, pedestal), faucet (single-hole, centerset, widespread, wall-mount), shower system (shower head + diverter + valve trim), kitchen sink (single bowl, double bowl, under-mount, top-mount), kitchen faucet (single-handle, pull-down, commercial style); Sales Associate enters selections into compatibility checker; system checks: (a) mounting compatibility — wall-hung basin requires wall-mount bracket and concealed carrier (if not existing); floor-mount toilet requires 10" or 12" rough-in distance matching existing waste pipe; (b) hole configuration — faucet hole spacing (single-hole, 4" centerset, 8" widespread) must match basin/sink drilling; (c) connection compatibility — faucet connections (flexible hose, rigid supply) must match customer's supply valve type (angle stop, straight stop); (d) water pressure compatibility — low-pressure systems (gravity-fed tank per W984) may not work with high-flow shower heads; (e) pipe material compatibility — connection adapters needed if existing pipes are GI (galvanized iron) and new fixtures are threaded PVC/PPR | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Compatibility Issue Resolution**: For any incompatibilities detected: (a) system identifies specific issue (e.g., "Selected faucet requires 8-inch widespread holes; selected basin has 4-inch centerset drilling"); (b) system suggests compatible alternatives from same brand or equivalent brands at similar price point; (c) system identifies required adapters/connectors (e.g., GI-to-PPR adapter, reducing coupling, extender flange); (d) Sales Associate presents options to customer with clear explanation of why incompatibility exists and how alternatives resolve it; (e) for complex compatibility issues (mixed brands, older plumbing): Sales Associate recommends bringing photos of existing plumbing or visiting with measurements for accurate assessment | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Bundle Builder — Complete Room Package**: Customer opts for complete bundle; system presents pre-configured packages or builds custom: (a) **Economy Bathroom Bundle** (~PHP 8,000–12,000): close-coupled toilet, counter-top basin, single-hole faucet, shower head with diverter, angle stops, flexible connectors, toilet seat cover; (b) **Standard Bathroom Bundle** (~PHP 15,000–25,000): two-piece toilet (dual-flush), under-mount basin on vanity top, widespread faucet, rain shower head + handheld shower with diverter, complete accessories (towel bar, toilet paper holder, soap dish, toothbrush holder); (c) **Premium Bathroom Bundle** (~PHP 35,000–60,000): one-piece toilet (soft-close seat), designer basin on furniture vanity, sensor faucet, thermostatic shower system with body jets, premium accessories, LED mirror cabinet; (d) **Kitchen Bundle** (~PHP 10,000–40,000): double-bowl sink, pull-down faucet, soap dispenser, under-sink filtration system; (e) system calculates bundle price with 5–10% discount vs. individual item pricing | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Quotation, Accessories & Service Referral**: Customer reviews bundle; Sales Associate: (a) presents itemized bundle contents with individual and bundle pricing; (b) recommends additional items — bathroom/kitchen waterproofing membrane (per W963 tile calculator), mirror, exhaust fan, towel warmer, water heater per W985 (for bathroom); (c) offers plumbing layout design per W989 for complete room renovation; (d) offers installation service referral per W138 (plumber for fixture installation); (e) offers tile/flooring calculator per W963 for floor and wall tiles; (f) creates POS quotation per W542 valid for 14 days; (g) quotation saved to customer loyalty account per W894 | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Fixture compatibility checker module (POS and mobile app) with brand/model compatibility database
- Bundle builder with pre-configured packages and custom bundle assembly
- Product catalog with fixture specifications (mounting type, hole configuration, connection type, dimensions)
- POS quotation module (W542) for bundle pricing
- Plumbing layout design integration (W989) for complete room renovation
- Tile/flooring calculator integration (W963) for coordinated material estimation
- Installation service referral (W138) for plumber installation
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Compatibility database maintenance**: Fixture manufacturers frequently update models, change specifications, or discontinue products; compatibility database must be updated at least monthly per W50 (PIM) to remain accurate; Category Manager owns database accuracy
- **Existing plumbing uncertainty**: Customers frequently cannot accurately describe their existing plumbing (pipe material, rough-in distance, valve type); compatibility check may be based on incomplete information; Sales Associate must recommend bringing photos or measurements for accurate assessment
- **Bundle discount margin**: 5–10% bundle discount reduces margin on individual items; bundle must generate incremental volume (complete room vs. single fixture) to justify discount; Category Manager monitors bundle sell-through rate and margin impact quarterly
- **Return complexity**: Bundled fixture returns create pricing complexity if customer returns individual items from bundle (how to handle bundle discount reversal); system must track bundle components and apply proportional refund logic per W12


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "centerset, widespread, wall-mount" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-detect of "Compatibility Issue Resolution" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "counter-top basin, single-hole faucet, shower head with diverter, angle stops,…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must track bundle components and apply proportional refund logic per W12

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Plumbing/Fixture Specialist)**: Compatibility checks and bundle building consume ~7–20 hours/month (~0.25–0.7 hours/day) at 20–30 interactions/month; absorbed by existing Plumbing Department Sales Associate
- **Training**: 8-hour bathroom/kitchen fixture product knowledge and compatibility certification per W51
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Fixture selection & compatibility check: 5–10 min
- Compatibility issue resolution: 5–10 min (if issues)
- Bundle building: 5–10 min
- Quotation & service referral: 5–10 min
- **Total per interaction**: 20–40 min of staff time

## W998. Customer Septic Tank & Wastewater System Sizing Recommendation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (homeowner, contractor, or developer) requests septic tank sizing for new construction or renovation |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 consultations/month chain-wide (~7–12/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1 system per consultation |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Plumbing) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Plumbing Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Proper wastewater treatment is a critical infrastructure requirement for every Philippine residential and commercial building. The majority of Philippine homes outside centralized sewerage systems (which serve only ~10% of the population, mainly Metro Manila) rely on septic tanks for wastewater treatment. the Sanitation Code (PD 856, Chapter XVII) and the National Plumbing Code mandate septic tank sizing, construction, and maintenance standards, and LGUs require septic tank plans for building permit approval per W54. Incorrect sizing — too small leads to overflow and groundwater contamination (a serious public health issue in PH); too large wastes construction cost and space — directly impacts customer safety and compliance. BuildRight sells pre-fabricated septic tanks (polyethylene, 1–10 cubic meters) and all materials for CHB-constructed septic tanks (cement, CHB, waterproofing, PVC pipes, fittings). This service integrates with W989 (plumbing layout) and W984 (water system) to provide complete water infrastructure planning.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Wastewater Load Assessment**: Sales Associate collects: (a) number of occupants/users; (b) building type — residential (single-family, multi-family), commercial, institutional; (c) number of bathrooms, kitchens, laundry areas; (d) water source — municipal supply or well pump per W984 (affects water consumption per capita); (e) soil type — clay (poor percolation), sandy (good percolation), loam; (f) available space for septic tank installation; (g) water table depth (high water table in coastal/lowland PH requires waterproof tank construction and may prevent drain field installation); (h) LGU requirements — some LGUs mandate minimum tank size or specific construction standards | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 2 | **Septic Tank Sizing Calculation**: System calculates per the Sanitation Code (PD 856, Ch. XVII) and the National Plumbing Code of the Philippines (NPCP): (a) daily wastewater volume = occupants × water consumption (150 L/person/day residential) × 80% (wastewater generation factor); (b) septic tank capacity = daily volume × 2–3 days retention time (minimum per DENR: 1.0 cubic meters effective capacity for residential); (c) recommended tank size — 1.0 cu.m. (1–3 occupants), 1.5 cu.m. (4–6 occupants), 2.0 cu.m. (7–10 occupants), 3.0 cu.m. (10–15 occupants), custom >3.0 cu.m. for commercial/institutional; (d) tank type recommendation — pre-fabricated polyethylene (fastest installation, 1-day setup, ideal for residential) or CHB-constructed (for custom sizes >3.0 cu.m. or where pre-fabricated delivery is impractical); (e) drain field sizing (if applicable) — percolation test result determines drain field area; for high water table or clay soil: sealed tank without drain field, requiring periodic desludging by LGU-accredited desludging service | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Material Takeoff**: System generates bill of materials: (a) **Pre-fabricated option**: septic tank unit (selected size), inlet/outlet PVC pipes (4" diameter), pipe fittings (elbows, tees), concrete base mix (cement + sand + gravel for pad), waterproofing membrane, access cover/riser, vent pipe, sealant; (b) **CHB-constructed option**: CHB hollow blocks (quantity based on tank dimensions × wall area × 1.05 waste), cement (bags for mortar and plaster), sand, rebar (10mm for wall reinforcement), waterproofing cement additive, inlet/outlet PVC pipes and fittings, concrete mix for base slab and cover slab, waterproofing membrane (applied to interior walls), access cover, vent pipe; (c) connection materials from building DWV to septic tank per W989 plumbing layout | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Regulatory Compliance & Installation Guidance**: Sales Associate provides: (a) DENR compliance confirmation — tank sizing meets the Sanitation Code (PD 856, Ch. XVII) minimum effective-capacity requirements (1.0 cu.m. residential, per Step 2); (b) LGU building permit guidance — customer must include septic tank plan in building permit application per W54; some LGUs require DENR-accredited installer certification; (c) maintenance recommendations — desludging every 3–5 years by LGU-accredited desludging service (BuildRight does not provide desludging services); (d) environmental advisories — no chemical/grease discharge to septic tank (kills treatment bacteria); recommend biological septic tank additive (enzyme tablets sold in store) to maintain treatment efficiency; (e) for high water table areas: advisement that sealed tank requires more frequent desludging since no drain field absorption | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 5 | **Quotation & Order Processing**: Customer reviews recommendation; Sales Associate: (a) presents complete material list with pricing; (b) offers plumbing layout design per W989 for complete DWV system design; (c) offers water tank/pump sizing per W984 for complete water system planning; (d) creates POS quotation per W542; (e) for pre-fabricated tanks: schedules delivery per W5D (tanks are bulky and heavy — 1.5 cu.m. tank weighs ~80 kg empty); (f) quotation saved to customer loyalty account per W894 | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Septic tank sizing calculator module (POS and mobile app) with Sanitation Code (PD 856, Ch. XVII) / NPCP sizing parameters
- Material takeoff engine for both pre-fabricated and CHB-constructed options
- Product catalog with septic tank specifications and plumbing material compatibility
- Plumbing layout design integration (W989) for DWV system connection
- Water tank/pump sizing integration (W984) for water system planning
- POS quotation module (W542) for material list
- Delivery scheduling (W5D) for bulky pre-fabricated tanks
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)
- LGU permit guidance per W54

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Public health liability**: Incorrect septic tank sizing or installation can cause groundwater contamination and public health hazards; BuildRight provides sizing guidance only (not installation certification); Sales Associate must recommend licensed plumber per W138 and LGU building permit per W54; disclaimer per Legal per W230
- **Soil and water table uncertainty**: Customer-reported soil type and water table depth may be inaccurate; incorrect assumptions lead to inappropriate tank selection; Sales Associate must recommend soil percolation test for properties with unknown soil conditions
- **Pre-fabricated tank delivery**: Septic tanks (especially 2.0+ cu.m.) are bulky and difficult to transport through narrow Philippine residential streets; delivery scheduling per W5D must account for vehicle access constraints; customer may need crane/boom truck for roof-deck or elevated installations
- **Maintenance education**: Customers frequently neglect septic tank maintenance; BuildRight should provide written maintenance guide with every septic tank purchase and recommend biological additive purchase as recurring revenue opportunity


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-collect of "Wastewater Load Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates per the Sanitation Code (PD 856, Ch. XVII) and the National Plumbing Code" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "generates bill of materials" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Plumbing) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Plumbing Specialist)**: Septic tank consultations consume ~3–8 hours/month (~0.1–0.3 hours/day) at 7–12 consultations/month; absorbed by existing Plumbing Department Sales Associate
- **Training**: 6-hour septic tank sizing and DENR compliance basics per W51
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Wastewater load assessment: 8–12 min
- Tank sizing calculation: 3–5 min
- Material takeoff: 3–5 min
- Regulatory compliance guidance: 5–8 min
- Quotation & order processing: 5–10 min
- **Total per consultation**: 24–40 min of staff time

## W1000. Customer Ceiling System Material Calculator & Design Recommendation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests ceiling material estimation at store or via mobile app |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–4,000 calculations/month chain-wide (~15–20/store/month) |
| **Volume** | 1–3 rooms per calculation; average 1.8 rooms |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials Specialist), Customer, Category Manager (pricing) |

### Background

Ceiling installation is a standard component of Philippine residential and commercial construction, serving both aesthetic and functional purposes (concealing structural elements, electrical wiring, plumbing pipes, and providing thermal insulation). The most common ceiling systems in the Philippines are: (1) gypsum board on metal framing (standard residential), (2) acoustic ceiling tiles on T-bar grid (commercial/offices), (3) PVC ceiling panels (economy residential, moisture-resistant for bathrooms), and (4) plywood/hardiflex on wood framing (traditional residential). Correct material estimation is essential because ceiling materials are fragile (gypsum board), bulky (difficult to transport in small vehicles), and require precise framing layout. Customers who underestimate materials face project delays; those who overestimate pay for return logistics of fragile, easily damaged sheets. The service integrates with W991 (electrical circuit design) for ceiling-mounted light fixture planning and W989 (plumbing layout) for concealed pipe routing.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Room Assessment & Ceiling Type Selection**: Customer provides: (a) room dimensions — length × width (in meters) for each room needing ceiling; (b) ceiling height (distance from floor to bottom of ceiling joists/rafters); (c) room type — bedroom, living room, kitchen, bathroom, office, commercial space; (d) ceiling type preference — gypsum board (smooth finish, paintable, standard for living rooms/bedrooms), PVC panels (waterproof, easy install, ideal for bathrooms/kitchens), acoustic tiles (sound absorption, commercial/office), plywood (traditional, paintable); (e) existing condition — open joists (new ceiling), old ceiling to replace, ceiling repair; (f) special features — recessed lighting, ceiling fan, aircon ducting, access panel for plumbing/electrical maintenance | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 2 | **Material Quantity Calculation**: System calculates: (a) ceiling area = room length × room width (per room); (b) **Gypsum board system**: gypsum board sheets (1.2m × 2.4m standard, coverage 2.88 sqm/sheet) = ceiling area ÷ 2.88 × 1.10 waste factor; metal furring channels (main runners at 1.2m spacing + cross tees at 0.4m spacing) = calculated by area and grid spacing; hanger wires (per m² of ceiling, typically 4–6 per sheet); drywall screws (30–40 per sheet); joint tape (linear meters = total joint length); joint compound (kg per sqm for 3-coat finish); corner beads (linear meters of exposed edges); (c) **PVC panel system**: PVC ceiling panels (width × length per product) = ceiling width ÷ panel width × ceiling length ÷ panel length; mounting clips/track (linear meters = perimeter + intermediate supports); screws; (d) **Acoustic tile system**: ceiling tiles (600mm × 600mm standard) = area ÷ 0.36 sqm × 1.05 waste; T-bar grid (main runners + cross tees per layout); wall molding (perimeter); hanger wires; (e) accessories — insulation (fiberglass blanket per W983 for thermal/sound), recessed light housings (per W991 electrical design), access panel, mold-resistant primer (for bathrooms/kitchens) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Cross-Sell Ceiling-Mounted Products**: Sales Associate recommends: (a) recessed LED downlights (per W991 electrical circuit planning); (b) ceiling fan (energy-efficient alternative to aircon per W985); (c) ceiling-mounted smoke detector per W476 fire safety; (d) insulation upgrade (fiberglass blanket reduces indoor temperature by 2–4°C in PH homes with GI sheet roofing); (e) decorative ceiling medallion (for chandelier/pendant light installations); (f) PVC cove molding for ceiling-wall junction finishing | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 4 | **Quotation & Order Processing**: Customer reviews material list; Sales Associate: (a) presents itemized materials grouped by system type; (b) offers delivery per W5D for bulky gypsum boards (1.2m × 2.4m sheets require covered truck transport — cannot be exposed to rain); (c) offers installation service referral per W138 (ceiling installer); (d) offers electrical layout per W991 if ceiling-mounted lighting planned; (e) creates POS quotation per W542 valid for 14 days; (f) quotation saved to customer loyalty account per W894 | Sales Associate / Customer | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Ceiling material calculator module (POS and mobile app) with PH-standard ceiling system parameters
- Material takeoff engine with framing layout and fastener count logic
- Product catalog with ceiling material specifications and compatibility
- POS quotation module (W542) for itemized material list
- Delivery scheduling (W5D) for fragile/bulky gypsum board sheets
- Installation service referral per W138
- Electrical layout integration (W991) for ceiling-mounted fixtures
- Customer loyalty account integration for quotation saving (W894)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Fragile material logistics**: Gypsum board sheets are fragile (edges chip, surfaces scratch, corners crush); transport must be in covered truck with flat load bed; loading/unloading requires 2-person carry; damage rate during transport is 3–5% per delivery; pricing must absorb average damage cost
- **Moisture sensitivity**: Gypsum board absorbs moisture and warps; PH high humidity during rainy season and typhoon flooding can damage stored and in-transit boards; PVC panels are moisture-resistant and recommended for bathrooms and kitchens; Sales Associate must recommend appropriate material per room type
- **Ceiling height safety**: Installing ceiling materials at height (2.4–3.5m) requires scaffolding or ladder work; safety harness required per W140; DIY customers must be warned about fall risk; professional installation per W138 strongly recommended
- **Hidden utilities**: Ceiling installation covers electrical wiring, plumbing pipes, and ductwork; Customer must ensure all rough-in work is complete and inspected before ceiling installation; Sales Associate should advise customer to photograph all concealed utilities before closing ceiling (for future maintenance reference)


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-calculate of "Material Quantity Calculation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-detect of "detector per W476 fire safety" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-review of "reviews material list" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: per W140

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Building Materials Specialist)**: Ceiling calculations consume ~5–12 hours/month (~0.2–0.4 hours/day) at 15–20 calculations/month; absorbed by existing Lumber & Building Materials Sales Associate
- **Training**: 6-hour ceiling system estimation and product knowledge per W51
- **No incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Room assessment & ceiling type selection: 8–12 min
- Material quantity calculation: 3–5 min
- Cross-sell products: 5–8 min
- Quotation & order processing: 5–10 min
- **Total per calculation**: 21–35 min of staff time

## W1043. Customer Paint Coverage Area Calculator & Primer/Finish Quantity Estimator Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests paint quantity recommendation at paint mixing station, pro desk, or via ecommerce |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–12,000 customer interactions/month across all stores (paint is 8% of SKU portfolio, ~2,800 SKUs) |
| **Volume** | 1 estimation per customer visit; average 3–5 surface calculations per project |
| **Owner** | Paint Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Paint Section), Paint Mixing Station Operator, Customer |

### Background

Paint & Finishes is a high-margin category (8% of active SKUs, ~2,800 items) that benefits significantly from consultative selling. Filipino homeowners and contractors frequently over- or under-purchase paint due to uncertainty about surface area coverage, primer requirements, and number of coats needed. Over-purchasing leads to customer dissatisfaction (perceived waste) and potential returns of tinted paint (which cannot be restocked). Under-purchasing leads to repeat store visits and color inconsistency between batches. The Philippine paint market has specific considerations: (a) tropical humidity affects drying time and coverage rates; (b) concrete block ("hollow block") and CHB (concrete hollow block) walls — the dominant wall material in Philippine construction — are highly porous and absorb 30–40% more paint than flat drywall; (c) new concrete requires alkali-resistant primer; (d) exterior surfaces need weather-resistant formulations; (e) the typical Filipino home has 40–80 sqm of interior wall area per room and 60–120 sqm of exterior wall per facade. This workflow provides an in-store (and ecommerce) paint quantity estimation tool that converts customer room dimensions into precise paint, primer, and supply quantities, reducing over-buying by an estimated 15–20% and increasing customer confidence in project scope. It is distinct from W168 (custom paint mixing operations) which governs the actual tinting process; this workflow addresses the upstream estimation and consultation phase.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Project Intake & Surface Area Calculation**: Sales Associate gathers project details: (a) surface type — interior wall, exterior wall, ceiling, trim/door, metal gate/railing, roof; (b) wall material — new CHB/concrete, old concrete (previously painted), drywall, plywood, wood, metal, stucco/roughcast; (c) surface condition — bare/unpainted, previously painted (good condition), previously painted (peeling/flaking), with stains/water marks; (d) room/area dimensions — length, width, height of each wall or surface; (e) deductions — window openings, door openings, built-in cabinets; (f) desired finish — flat, satin, semi-gloss, gloss; (g) system calculates net paintable surface area (gross area minus deductions) and applies surface-type absorption factor (CHB: 1.3–1.4×; smooth concrete: 1.0×; wood: 1.1×; metal: 0.9×) | Sales Associate | Paint Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Primer & Undercoat Recommendation**: System determines primer requirements: (a) new CHB/concrete surfaces: alkaline-resistant primer (mandatory, 1 coat); (b) previously painted surfaces in good condition: no primer needed unless changing from oil-based to water-based; (c) peeling/flaking surfaces: scraping + patching compound + primer (1 coat); (d) stained/water-marked surfaces: stain-blocking primer (1 coat); (e) metal surfaces: anti-rust primer (1 coat); (f) exterior surfaces: weather-resistant primer (1 coat); (g) system calculates primer volume using primer coverage rate (typically 30–40 sqm per gallon for Philippine brands) and surfaces requiring primer | Sales Associate / System | Paint Department Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Finish Coat Quantity Calculation**: System computes finish coat quantity: (a) number of coats recommended — interior walls: 2 coats; exterior walls: 2–3 coats (tropical rain exposure); ceiling: 2 coats; metal: 2 coats; (b) coverage rate per product — varies by paint line (economy: 25–30 sqm/gallon; premium: 35–45 sqm/gallon); system pulls coverage data from product master (W252) based on selected paint SKU; (c) total volume = (net surface area × absorption factor × number of coats) ÷ coverage rate per gallon; (d) system rounds up to nearest gallon/4-liter container and adds 10% contingency for touch-ups; (e) if customer has selected paint color, system checks availability in required volume per real-time inventory (W11/W533) | System / Sales Associate | Paint Department Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Ancillary Supplies Recommendation**: System generates companion product list: (a) paint rollers — 7" or 9" width based on wall area; roller covers (nap length: 3/8" smooth, 1/2"–3/4" rough surfaces); (b) paint brushes — 2" trim brush, 4" wall brush; (c) paint tray and liners; (d) masking tape (painter's tape) — 1" and 2" widths, calculated linear meters based on edge/trim length; (e) sandpaper — grit 120 (surface prep), grit 220 (between coats); (f) putty/spackling knife and patching compound for surface repair; (g) drop cloth/plastic sheeting — sized to room dimensions; (h) solvent/cleaner — water for latex, mineral spirits for oil-based; (i) system bundles these as a "project supply kit" with total estimated cost | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 5 | **Estimate Generation & Customer Handoff**: (a) system generates printable/pdf estimate with: project summary (room name, dimensions, surface types), calculated paintable area, primer recommendation (brand, product, volume, coats), finish coat recommendation (brand, product, color code, volume, coats), ancillary supply list with quantities and prices, total estimated project cost, estimated project time (drying time between coats: 2–4 hours water-based, 6–8 hours oil-based in Philippine humidity); (b) estimate saved to customer's loyalty account or as guest session; (c) customer can directly convert estimate to in-store transaction by scanning estimate QR code at POS (W542); (d) for trade account customers, estimate can be saved as project quote per W542 with 30-day validity; (e) paint mixing station operator receives color formula and volume — proceeds to W168 (custom paint mixing) if customer confirms | Sales Associate | Paint Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Paint coverage calculator engine (surface area, absorption factor, coverage rate per product)
- Product master (W252) — coverage rates, primer compatibility, paint line specifications
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- Paint mixing station integration (W168) — formula and volume handoff
- POS quotation module (W542) — estimate-to-sale conversion
- Loyalty account integration (W17) — saved estimates and purchase history
- Ecommerce product page integration — online paint calculator widget

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Surface condition misjudgment**: Customer self-reported surface condition may be inaccurate; over-the-phone or online estimates carry higher error risk; recommend in-store consultation for projects >20 sqm
- **Coverage rate variance**: Philippine paint brands (Boysen, Davies, Island Paints, Nippon Paint) have different coverage rates even within the same product line; product master must be updated per SKU per W252 governance
- **Color batch consistency**: If customer under-purchases and returns for additional paint of the same color, the second batch may have slight color variation; system should recommend rounding up and note this risk on the estimate
- **Returns of tinted paint**: Tinted paint cannot be restocked; returns policy (W12A) should clearly communicate non-returnability of custom-mixed paint; this workflow aims to reduce over-buying which in turn reduces return disputes


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-calculate of "calculates net paintable surface area" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "checks availability in required volume per real-time inventory" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-generate of "generates companion product list" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: should recommend rounding up and note this risk on the estimate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Paint Section)**: Estimation adds 5–10 minutes per consultation; at 40–60 paint consultations per store per month, this represents ~3–8 hours/month; absorbed by existing 12 Sales Associates with paint section rotation
- **No permanent incremental headcount**; paint calculator tool reduces manual estimation errors and speeds up consultation by 40%

### Time Estimate
- Customer intake & surface area calculation: 5–10 min
- Primer recommendation: 2–3 min (automated)
- Finish coat calculation: 2–3 min (automated)
- Ancillary supplies: 1–2 min (automated)
- Estimate generation & handoff: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 13–23 min (reduced from 25–40 min without calculator tool)
