## W1044. Customer Concrete Mix Ratio & Volume Calculator for Slab/Foundation/Column Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests concrete quantity estimate for a construction project (slab, footing, column, beam, driveway, post filler) |
| **Frequency** | ~4,000–6,000 customer interactions/month across all stores (cement and concrete materials are in the lumber & building materials category — 14% of SKUs) |
| **Volume** | 1 calculation per customer project |
| **Owner** | Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials Section), Customer |

### Background

Concrete is the most fundamental building material in Philippine construction. Virtually every residential and commercial building project in the Philippines involves concrete work — from simple post footings for a fence (0.05 cubic meters) to full house slabs (5–20 cubic meters) to multi-story foundation works (50+ cubic meters). The Philippine standard concrete mix uses bags of Portland cement (40 kg per bag), sand (fine aggregate), gravel (coarse aggregate), and water. Class A mix (1:2:4 cement:sand:gravel) is standard for structural elements per DPWH convention; Class B (1:2½:5) for non-structural; Class C (1:3:6) for fill and non-critical work. Filipino contractors and DIY homeowners frequently miscalculate material quantities because: (a) concrete volume depends on three-dimensional formwork dimensions, which many customers struggle to compute; (b) waste factors for concrete are 5–10% due to spillage, over-excavation, and form irregularities; (c) the number of cement bags must be derived from the mix ratio and volume, not estimated visually. BuildRight Depot sells ~PHP 3–4 billion/year in cement, sand, gravel, and related concrete materials — this consultative service drives correct purchasing, reduces under-buying (which causes project delays and repeat visits), and differentiates BuildRight from competitors who do not offer estimation services. This workflow is distinct from W964 (bulk cement, sand & aggregates delivery) which covers the logistics of delivery; this workflow covers the upstream quantity estimation.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Type & Structural Element Identification**: Sales Associate identifies the concrete application: (a) slab-on-grade (floor slab, driveway, carport); (b) footing/foundation (isolated footing, continuous footing, mat foundation); (c) column (rectangular, circular); (d) beam (tie beam, grade beam, lintel); (e) wall (CHB wall footings, retaining wall); (f) post filler (hollow block post filler, 4"/6" round column filler); (g) stairs; (h) other custom form. System displays the applicable structural standards and minimum recommended concrete class (Class A for structural, Class B for general, Class C for fill) | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 2–3 min |
| 2 | **Dimension Input & Volume Calculation**: Customer provides dimensions and system computes volume: (a) rectangular slab: length × width × thickness (in meters; default thickness: 0.10m for residential floor, 0.15m for garage/driveway); (b) L-shaped or irregular slab: decomposed into rectangles; (c) footing: length × width × depth (typical depth 0.60–1.20m for single-story); (d) column: width × depth × height; (e) circular column: π × radius² × height; (f) beam: width × depth × length; (g) retaining wall: length × height × thickness (tapers if applicable); (h) system adds waste factor: 5% for simple shapes, 10% for irregular shapes or complex formwork; (i) system outputs total concrete volume in cubic meters (m³) | Sales Associate / Customer / System | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Mix Design & Material Quantity Breakdown**: System computes material quantities based on selected mix class: (a) **Class A (1:2:4)**: ~9.0–9.5 bags cement per m³, 0.67 m³ sand, 1.33 m³ gravel; (b) **Class B (1:2½:5)**: ~7.0–7.5 bags cement per m³, 0.70 m³ sand, 1.40 m³ gravel; (c) **Class C (1:3:6)**: 5.0 bags cement per m³, 0.83 m³ sand per m³, 1.67 m³ gravel per m³; (d) system multiplies per-m³ quantities by total volume (including waste factor); (e) outputs: number of cement bags (40 kg), volume of sand (m³ or cubic meters equivalent in truck loads), volume of gravel (m³ or cubic meters equivalent in truck loads), estimated water volume (liters); (f) for projects >3 m³, system recommends ready-mixed concrete as an alternative per W964 (bulk delivery) and shows cost comparison: bagged DIY vs. ready-mix delivered | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Companion Materials Recommendation**: System generates companion product list: (a) reinforcing steel (rebar) — size and quantity per standard spacing (10mm @ 200mm O.C. for slab; 12mm or 16mm for columns and beams) per W986/W1060; (b) tie wire — 1.5 kg per 100 kg of rebar; (c) formwork materials — plywood (1/4" or 1/2" for concrete forming), 2×2 or 2×3 wood studs for form support; (d) plastic sheeting — for curing cover (kept wet for 7 days per Philippine engineering practice); (e) vibro-tamping rod or concrete vibrator rental per W139; (f) wheelbarrow and mixing tools; (g) waterproofing compound for below-grade concrete; (h) system bundles companion materials with total estimated cost | System | Sales Associate | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 5 | **Estimate Generation, Delivery Scheduling & Customer Handoff**: (a) system generates printable/PDF estimate with: project description, structural element dimensions, computed concrete volume, selected mix class with ratio, material quantity breakdown (cement bags, sand m³, gravel m³, water liters), companion materials list with prices, total estimated project cost; (b) for orders >2 m³ of sand/gravel: system triggers bulk delivery scheduling per W964 with delivery fee estimate based on distance; (c) for orders ≤2 m³: customer can load materials at store yard per W950 (vehicle loading assistance); (d) estimate saved to customer's loyalty account; (e) customer can convert estimate to transaction at POS per W542; (f) for trade account B2B customers, estimate converts to project quote per W162 with delivery window scheduling | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Concrete volume calculator engine with dimensional input for multiple structural shapes
- Mix design database (Class A/B/C ratios, per-m³ material factors)
- Product master (W252) — cement bag size, sand/gravel pricing per m³
- Real-time inventory availability (W533) for cement, sand, gravel
- Bulk delivery scheduling module (W964) integration
- POS quotation module (W542) — estimate-to-sale conversion
- Loyalty account integration (W17) — saved estimates
- B2B quotation module (W162) — trade account project quotes

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Structural engineering disclaimer**: BuildRight staff are not structural engineers; the calculator provides material quantity estimates only, not structural design; system must display disclaimer on every estimate: "This estimate is for material quantity guidance only and does not constitute structural engineering advice. Consult a licensed civil engineer for structural design requirements."
- **Soil condition variability**: Actual excavation depth and footing size may differ from customer's stated dimensions due to soil conditions; recommend 10% contingency on volume for foundation works
- **Sand/gravel volume measurement**: Philippine sand and gravel are sold by the cubic meter (truck load) with inherent ±10% measurement variance; system should note this on estimates
- **Cement shelf life**: Cement bags have a 3-month shelf life in Philippine humidity; customers ordering large quantities (>50 bags) should be advised to schedule delivery closer to pour date


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-post of "Project Type & Structural Element Identification" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-generate of "generates companion product list" (replaces manual Step 4).
- System auto-count of "Estimate Generation, Delivery Scheduling & Customer Handoff" (replaces manual Step 5).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: should note this on estimates

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Building Materials)**: Estimation adds 10–15 minutes per consultation; at 20–30 concrete consultations per store per month, this represents ~4–10 hours/month; absorbed by existing 12 Sales Associates with building materials section rotation
- **No permanent incremental headcount**; calculator tool reduces manual estimation time by ~60%

### Time Estimate
- Project type identification: 2–3 min
- Dimension input & volume calculation: 3–5 min
- Mix design & material breakdown: 1–2 min (automated)
- Companion materials: 2–3 min (automated)
- Estimate & delivery scheduling: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 11–18 min (reduced from 25–40 min without calculator tool)

## W1048. Customer Rainwater Harvesting System Design & Material Sizing Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests rainwater harvesting system design, or expresses interest in water conservation during plumbing/building materials consultation |
| **Frequency** | ~500–800 consultations/month across all stores (growing interest in Philippine water conservation, especially in water-scarce regions like Metro Manila, Cebu, and parts of Mindanao) |
| **Volume** | 1 system design per customer consultation |
| **Owner** | Plumbing Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Plumbing Section), Customer |

### Background

Rainwater harvesting is increasingly relevant in the Philippines due to: (a) water scarcity in urban areas (Metro Manila, Cebu, Davao) where municipal water supply is unreliable; (b) LGU mandates in some cities requiring rainwater collection systems for new construction (e.g., Quezon City Green Building Code, Davao City Rainwater Ordinance); (c) El Niño drought events that cause water rationing; (d) cost savings for households — a 1,000-liter rainwater tank can supply ~30% of a Filipino household's non-potable water needs (toilet flushing, laundry, garden watering, car washing). BuildRight Depot sells all the components needed for rainwater harvesting: PVC gutters and downspouts, PVC storage tanks (500L to 10,000L), first-flush diverters, leaf screens, mesh filters, PVC piping, submersible pumps, float valves, and overflow fittings. This consultative service helps customers size and select the right components for their roof area, rainfall intensity, and water demand — converting what would be a commodity parts sale into a full-system solution. This workflow is distinct from W984 (water tank & pump sizing) which focuses on pressurized water supply systems; this workflow specifically addresses rainwater collection system design with catchment-area-based sizing.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Catchment Area & Demand Assessment**: Sales Associate gathers project parameters: (a) roof type — galvanized iron (GI) sheet, clay tile, concrete flat roof, metal deck; (b) roof catchment area — length × width of roof section(s) that will drain to the collection system (in square meters); (c) roof slope and gutter location; (d) average annual rainfall for customer's location (system auto-populates from PAGASA data based on customer's city/municipality per W310 — address hierarchy); (e) water demand — intended uses (garden/lawn irrigation, toilet flushing, laundry, car washing, general cleaning); (f) available space for storage tank (ground level, elevated platform, underground); (g) existing gutter system — compatible or needs replacement; (h) budget range | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Storage Tank Sizing Calculation**: System computes recommended tank capacity: (a) monthly harvestable rainwater (liters/month) = roof catchment area (m²) × average monthly rainfall (mm) × 0.85 (runoff coefficient for GI/metal roof; 0.75 for concrete; 0.80 for tile) × 0.90 (first-flush diverter and filter losses); (b) system displays monthly harvest by month using PAGASA rainfall distribution (wet months June–November produce 70–80% of annual harvest); (c) recommended tank size = 2–3 months of dry-season demand (December–May) to bridge the dry period; (d) system recommends tank model(s) from inventory: 500L (small household, garden only), 1,000L (medium household, garden + toilet flushing), 2,000L–5,000L (large household or small commercial), 10,000L (commercial/institutional); (e) system checks real-time inventory (W533) for recommended tank at customer's store | System / Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 3 | **System Component Specification**: System generates complete bill of materials: (a) gutter system — PVC or aluminum gutter (125mm or 150mm half-round or box profile), length = roof perimeter along collection side, brackets @ 600mm O.C., end caps, joiners; (b) downspout(s) — PVC pipe diameter (75mm or 100mm), number of downspouts (1 per 50 sqm of roof area, minimum 2), elbows and brackets; (c) first-flush diverter — 1 per downspout (diverts first 20 liters of rainwater containing roof contaminants); (d) leaf screen / mesh filter — installed at gutter-to-downspout transition; (e) inlet filter — at tank entry point; (f) storage tank — per step 2 recommendation; (g) overflow pipe — PVC pipe from tank overflow to storm drain, diameter ≥ downspout diameter; (h) outlet fitting — ball valve or float valve at tank base; (i) pump (optional) — submersible pump per W984 sizing if customer needs pressurized output; (j) pipework from tank to point-of-use — PVC pipe, fittings, isolation valves; (k) system calculates total material cost using current pricing per W252 | System | Sales Associate | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate Generation & Customer Handoff**: (a) system generates printable/PDF estimate with: project summary (roof area, location, rainfall data), monthly harvest calculation with PAGASA data table, recommended tank size with rationale, complete bill of materials with quantities and prices, total estimated system cost, estimated installation time (DIY: 1–2 days; professional: 4–6 hours), maintenance requirements (gutter cleaning quarterly, tank cleaning annually, filter replacement as needed); (b) if tank requires delivery (tanks >500L): system triggers home delivery scheduling per W5D with delivery fee estimate; (c) offer professional installation referral per W138 (home installation services) and W904 (contractor referral); (d) estimate saved to customer's loyalty account; (e) customer can convert estimate to POS transaction per W542 | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Rainwater harvest calculator engine — catchment area, PAGASA rainfall data, runoff coefficients
- PAGASA rainfall database integration — monthly averages by city/municipality (W310 address hierarchy)
- Product master (W252) — tank models, gutter profiles, PVC pipe specs, pricing
- Real-time inventory availability (W533)
- Home delivery scheduling (W5D) — bulky tank delivery
- POS quotation module (W542) — estimate-to-sale conversion
- Contractor referral module (W904) — professional installation referral
- Home installation service module (W138) — installation booking
- Loyalty account integration (W17) — saved estimates

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Water quality disclaimer**: Rainwater from GI sheet roofs may contain lead, zinc, and other contaminants; system must display disclaimer: "Rainwater harvested from this system is recommended for non-potable use only. Do not use for drinking, cooking, or bathing without additional water treatment per DOH standards."
- **Structural load of elevated tanks**: A 1,000L tank weighs 1,000 kg when full; if customer plans elevated installation, recommend structural assessment by licensed engineer
- **LGU permit requirements**: Some LGUs require building permits for tank installations >2,000L; advise customer to check with municipal building official per W1056 (construction permit advisory)
- **Mosquito breeding**: Open tanks or improperly sealed systems create mosquito breeding sites (dengue risk); recommend all openings have mesh screens and tank is fully enclosed


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-collect of "Catchment Area & Demand Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "checks real-time inventory" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates total material cost using current pricing per W252" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Plumbing Section)**: Rainwater harvesting consultation adds 15–20 min per customer; at 3–4 consultations per store per month, this represents ~1–1.5 hours/month
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Catchment area & demand assessment: 5–8 min
- Tank sizing calculation: 2–3 min (automated)
- Component specification: 2–3 min (automated)
- Estimate generation & handoff: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 12–19 min (average ~15 min)

## W1050. Customer Staircase Tread, Riser & Railing Material Calculator Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests staircase material quantities for residential or small commercial construction project |
| **Frequency** | ~800–1,200 consultations/month across all stores |
| **Volume** | 1 staircase calculation per consultation |
| **Owner** | Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials Section), Customer |

### Background

Staircases are a universal element in Philippine multi-story residential construction (2–3 story homes are common in urban areas due to limited lot sizes). A typical Philippine residential staircase has 12–16 risers (floor-to-floor height ~3.0 meters) with 2–3 flights and 1–2 intermediate landings. Customers (both DIY homeowners and small contractors) frequently miscalculate staircase material quantities because: (a) tread and riser counts depend on precise floor-to-floor height and desired step dimensions (rise and run); (b) the Philippine National Building Code (PD 1096) mandates maximum riser height of 200mm and minimum tread depth of 250mm for residential stairs; (c) stringer (carriage) length must be calculated from total rise and run using the Pythagorean theorem; (d) railing balusters must comply with maximum 100mm spacing per PD 1096 (child safety). Common errors lead to under-buying (multiple store visits) or over-buying (waste and returns). This calculator service provides precise material quantities for: treads, risers, stringers, landing framing, railing posts, handrails, and balusters — converting a complex geometric calculation into a simple bill of materials that drives a complete sale.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Staircase Dimension Input**: Sales Associate gathers staircase parameters: (a) total floor-to-floor height (vertical rise) in meters; (b) total horizontal run available (floor space for staircase footprint) in meters; (c) staircase width (typical Philippine residential: 0.80m–1.00m); (d) number of flights (straight run, L-shaped with landing, U-shaped with landing); (e) desired step dimensions: target riser height (150–200mm per PD 1096), target tread depth (250–300mm per PD 1096); (f) material preference — treads: plywood (18mm), solid wood (tanguile, narra), tiles on concrete; risers: plywood (12mm), solid wood; stringers: 2×10 or 2×12 lumber; railing: wood (tanguile), steel, aluminum; (g) landing type — none, quarter-turn (L-shape), half-turn (U-shape) with landing dimensions | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Geometric Calculation & Code Compliance Check**: System computes staircase geometry: (a) number of risers = total rise ÷ target riser height (rounded up to nearest integer); (b) actual riser height = total rise ÷ number of risers; (c) system validates: riser height ≤ 200mm per PD 1096 (flags non-compliance with warning); (d) number of treads = number of risers − 1 (per flight); (e) total run = number of treads × tread depth; (f) system validates: total run ≤ available horizontal space (flags if insufficient space, recommends shorter tread or L/U configuration); (g) stringer length = √(total rise² + total run²) per flight; (h) for L/U configurations: calculates landing framing dimensions and separate stringer lengths for each flight; (i) system displays 2D side-view diagram of staircase with dimensions | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Material Quantity Calculation**: System generates bill of materials: (a) **Treads**: quantity = number of treads, width = staircase width, depth = tread depth, material = selected tread material (board footage calculated); (b) **Risers**: quantity = number of risers, width = staircase width, height = actual riser height, material = selected riser material; (c) **Stringers**: quantity = 2 (minimum) + 1 intermediate for stairs >1m wide; length per stringer calculated per step 2g; material = 2×10 or 2×12 lumber; (d) **Landing framing** (if applicable): joists, header beams, plywood decking — per landing dimensions; (e) **Railing**: newel posts (1 at bottom, 1 at top, 1 at each landing = 2–4 posts), handrail length = stringer length × number of flights, balusters = (handrail length ÷ maximum baluster spacing per PD 1096) − 1 per flight section, baluster height = riser height × number of risers per section + 0.90m (handrail height per code); (f) **Hardware**: nails, screws, construction adhesive, angle brackets for stringer-to-floor connection; (g) system calculates total board footage and converts to standard lumber lengths available in inventory | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate Generation & Customer Handoff**: (a) system generates printable/PDF estimate with: staircase side-view diagram with dimensions, step-by-step code compliance summary, complete bill of materials with quantities, unit prices, and total cost, PD 1096 compliance notes, assembly instructions overview; (b) check inventory availability for all lumber lengths and materials per W533; (c) for non-stock items (special wood species, decorative balusters): create special order per W545; (d) offer home delivery for lumber per W5D; (e) estimate saved to loyalty account and convertible to POS transaction per W542 | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Staircase geometric calculator engine — rise, run, stringer length, landing dimensions
- PD 1096 compliance validation — riser height, tread depth, baluster spacing, handrail height
- Product master (W252) — lumber dimensions, pricing, availability
- Real-time inventory availability (W533) — lumber lengths
- POS quotation module (W542) — estimate-to-sale conversion
- Special order module (W545) — non-stock decorative items
- Home delivery scheduling (W5D) — lumber delivery

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Structural engineering disclaimer**: System provides material quantities only; staircase structural design (stringer sizing, load capacity, anchoring method) requires licensed engineer for commercial buildings; mandatory disclaimer on all estimates
- **Actual vs. planned dimensions**: Customer-provided floor-to-floor height may differ from actual by 50–100mm due to finish floor materials; recommend field measurement verification per W442
- **Non-standard staircase configurations**: Curved, spiral, or winder stairs require custom engineering beyond calculator capability; recommend professional contractor referral per W904


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "Staircase Dimension Input" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "Geometric Calculation & Code Compliance Check" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "Material Quantity Calculation" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: provides material quantities only

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Building Materials)**: Consultation adds 10–15 min; at 4–6 consultations per store per month, this represents ~1–1.5 hours/month
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Dimension input: 5–8 min
- Geometric calculation: 1–2 min (automated)
- Material quantity calculation: 1–2 min (automated)
- Estimate generation & handoff: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 10–17 min (average ~13 min)

## W1052. Customer Gutter, Downspout & Flashing Sizing Calculator Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests gutter system sizing for new construction, renovation, or replacement |
| **Frequency** | ~1,200–1,800 consultations/month across all stores (gutters are part of the lumber & building materials and plumbing categories) |
| **Volume** | 1 calculation per customer project |
| **Owner** | Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials/Plumbing Section), Customer |

### Background

Gutter and downspout systems are essential in Philippine residential and commercial construction due to high annual rainfall (1,500–4,000mm/year depending on region). Proper gutter sizing prevents: (a) roof runoff damaging building foundations and walls; (b) water ingress through windows and doors; (c) soil erosion around building perimeter; (d) flooding of ground-floor areas during intense downpours. Philippine construction commonly uses: (a) GI (galvanized iron) half-round or box gutters — traditional, durable; (b) PVC gutters — lightweight, corrosion-resistant, easier installation; (c) aluminum gutters — premium, lightweight, no rust; (d) pre-painted GI gutters — aesthetic appeal. Downspouts are typically PVC (75mm or 100mm diameter) or GI (round or rectangular). Flashing materials include GI sheet, aluminum sheet, and rubberized asphalt membrane for valley flashing, chimney flashing, and roof-wall intersections. Customers frequently miscalculate gutter lengths (forgetting to account for roof pitch and overhang) and downspout quantities (underestimating for large roof areas). This service converts a complex hydraulic calculation into a complete bill of materials.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Roof & Building Parameter Input**: Sales Associate gathers project parameters: (a) roof type — gable, hip, shed, flat (with parapet), combination; (b) roof dimensions — length and width of each roof plane (or total roof footprint area); (c) roof pitch/slope angle (in degrees or rise-to-run ratio); (d) number of stories (affects downspout length); (e) gutter type preference — half-round (125mm or 150mm), box/K-style (125mm or 150mm), PVC, GI, aluminum; (f) downspout type — round PVC (75mm/100mm), rectangular GI, round GI; (g) building location (for rainfall intensity lookup per PAGASA data per W310); (h) gutter run direction — along eaves (both sides for gable), along all sides (hip roof) | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Gutter & Downspout Sizing Calculation**: System computes hydraulic requirements: (a) roof drainage area per gutter run = roof plane width × horizontal projection (length × cos(pitch)); (b) design rainfall intensity (mm/hr) per PAGASA 10-year return period data for customer's location; (c) peak runoff (liters/min) = drainage area (m²) × rainfall intensity (mm/hr) ÷ 60; (d) gutter capacity per profile: half-round 125mm = 1.2 L/s at 1:200 slope, half-round 150mm = 2.0 L/s, box 125mm = 1.5 L/s, box 150mm = 2.5 L/s; (e) if peak runoff exceeds single gutter capacity: system recommends (i) larger profile, (ii) steeper slope, or (iii) additional downspouts to reduce gutter run length; (f) downspout quantity: minimum 1 per 10m of gutter run or 1 per 50m² of drainage area (whichever is greater); (g) downspout length = roof edge height to ground + 300mm extension (splash block or direct to drainage) | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Flashing & Accessories Calculation**: System generates complete material list: (a) **Gutter sections**: total linear meters per gutter profile (standard lengths: 3m or 6m); (b) **Gutter brackets/braces**: @ 600mm O.C. for PVC, @ 900mm O.C. for GI; (c) **End caps**: 2 per gutter run (left and right); (d) **Joiners/connectors**: 1 per section joint; (e) **Corner pieces** (inside/outside): per roof plan corners; (f) **Downspout sections**: total length per downspout × number of downspouts (standard lengths: 3m); (g) **Downspout elbows**: 2 per downspout (top offset from gutter, bottom offset away from wall); (h) **Downspout brackets/wall clips**: @ 1.5m O.C.; (i) **Splash blocks or flexible downspout extensions**: 1 per downspout; (j) **Valley flashing**: length × number of valleys (for gable/hip intersections); (k) **Apron flashing**: at roof-wall intersections; (l) **Step flashing**: at chimney/penetration interfaces; (m) **Sealant and pop rivets/screws** for assembly; (n) **Leaf guard/screen**: optional, same linear meters as gutter | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate Generation & Customer Handoff**: (a) system generates printable/PDF estimate with: roof drainage diagram, hydraulic calculation summary, recommended gutter profile and downspout configuration, complete bill of materials with quantities and prices, total estimated project cost, installation instructions overview; (b) check inventory per W533; (c) for non-stock items (special gutter profiles, aluminum flashing): special order per W545; (d) offer delivery per W5D for long gutter sections; (e) offer contractor referral per W904; (f) estimate saved and convertible to sale per W542 | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Gutter sizing calculator engine — hydraulic calculation, PAGASA rainfall intensity data
- Product master (W252) — gutter profiles, downspout specifications, pricing
- Real-time inventory (W533)
- POS quotation (W542), special orders (W545), delivery (W5D)
- Contractor referral (W904)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Installation complexity**: Gutter installation requires working at roof height; recommend professional installation per W138 for multi-story buildings
- **Rainfall intensity data accuracy**: PAGASA data may not reflect local microclimate variations; add 20% capacity margin for safety


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "Roof & Building Parameter Input" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-file of "Gutter & Downspout Sizing Calculation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-validate of "check inventory per W533" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- No permanent incremental headcount; ~1 hour/week of Sales Associate time per store

### Time Estimate
- Total per consultation: 10–17 min (average ~13 min)

## W1053. Customer Bathroom & Kitchen Exhaust Fan & Ventilation Duct Sizing Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests exhaust fan recommendation for bathroom, kitchen, or other ventilation application |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,000 consultations/month across all stores (home appliances category: 5% of SKUs, ~1,750 items; exhaust fans are a high-turn sub-category) |
| **Volume** | 1 sizing calculation per room |
| **Owner** | Home Appliances Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Appliances Section), Customer |

### Background

Exhaust ventilation is critical in Philippine homes due to tropical humidity (60–90% relative humidity year-round). Without proper ventilation, bathrooms develop mold and mildew within weeks, and kitchens accumulate grease and cooking odors. Philippine building standards (PD 1096 National Building Code) require mechanical ventilation for windowless bathrooms and commercial kitchens. The Philippine exhaust fan market uses Air Changes per Hour (ACH) as the standard metric: bathrooms require 8–10 ACH, kitchens require 15–20 ACH, and laundry areas require 5–8 ACH. BuildRight Depot sells exhaust fans from PHP 500 (basic axial fan, 4" duct) to PHP 8,000 (premium centrifugal inline fan with humidity sensor, 6" duct). Customers frequently purchase under-sized fans because they select by duct diameter ("I need a 4-inch fan") rather than by airflow capacity (CMH — cubic meters per hour) matched to their room volume. This service converts room dimensions into CMH requirements and recommends the correct fan model, duct size, and accessories.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Room Parameter Input**: Sales Associate gathers room details: (a) application — bathroom, kitchen, laundry, workshop, storage room; (b) room dimensions — length, width, height (in meters); (c) room volume = length × width × height (m³); (d) existing duct/vent opening — yes/no, diameter if existing; (e) wall type — CHB (concrete hollow block, requires core drilling for new opening), wood frame, drywall; (f) ceiling type — suspended ceiling (space for inline fan above), solid concrete slab (wall-mount fan only); (g) power source — wall outlet available, or hardwired connection needed; (h) special features — humidity sensor, timer, backdraft damper, speed control | Sales Associate | Home Appliances Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **CMH Requirement & Fan Sizing**: System calculates required airflow: (a) minimum CMH = room volume × target ACH (bathroom: 8–10, kitchen: 15–20, laundry: 5–8, workshop: 6–10); (b) system recommends fan models from inventory with rated CMH ≥ minimum CMH; (c) system selects recommended duct size: 4" (100mm) for CMH ≤200, 5" (125mm) for CMH 200–400, 6" (150mm) for CMH 400–800; (d) for kitchen range hoods: additional consideration — hood width ≥ cooktop width, mounting height 600–750mm above cooktop, duct run to exterior (maximum 3m with 1 elbow, or upgrade to higher CMH for longer runs); (e) system shows top 3 fan model recommendations with: brand, model, rated CMH, duct diameter, noise level (sones), power consumption (watts), price | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Duct & Accessories Specification**: System generates complete material list: (a) exhaust fan unit (recommended model); (b) ducting — rigid aluminum duct (preferred) or flexible aluminum duct, diameter per step 2, length = distance from fan to exterior wall/roof vent + 0.5m contingency; (c) exterior wall vent/roof cap with backdraft damper (prevents outside air entry when fan is off); (d) duct tape or clamps for connections; (e) wall/ceiling mounting hardware; (f) exterior louver/grille (aesthetic cover); (g) for new wall penetration in CHB: core drilling service recommendation per W138 (installation services) — typical cost PHP 500–1,500 per hole; (h) optional upgrades: speed controller, humidity sensor, timer switch | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate Generation & Customer Handoff**: (a) system generates estimate with: room ventilation calculation (volume, ACH, CMH), recommended fan with specifications, complete duct and accessory list, total cost, estimated installation time (DIY: 1–3 hours, professional: 1–2 hours); (b) offer installation service booking per W138; (c) estimate saved and convertible per W542 | Sales Associate | Home Appliances Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Ventilation sizing calculator engine — room volume, ACH, CMH
- Product master (W252) — fan models with CMH ratings, duct specifications
- Real-time inventory (W533)
- POS quotation (W542), installation services (W138)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Duct run length impact**: Each 1m of duct and each 90° elbow reduces effective CMH by 5–10%; calculator may under-recommend for long/complex duct runs; add 20% CMH margin for runs >3m or >2 elbows


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-calculate of "calculates required airflow" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "generates complete material list" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- No permanent incremental headcount; ~3–7 min/day of Sales Associate time per store (per the Frequency × Time Estimate derivation)

### Time Estimate
- Total per consultation: 8–14 min (average ~10 min)

## W1055. Customer Water Filtration & Purification System Sizing for Residential & Commercial

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests water filtration/purification system recommendation for residential or commercial application |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 consultations/month across all stores (plumbing category: 12% of SKUs; water filtration is a growing sub-category driven by Philippine water quality concerns) |
| **Volume** | 1 system sizing per consultation |
| **Owner** | Plumbing Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Plumbing Section), Customer |

### Background

Water quality is a significant concern in the Philippines. According to the Philippine Institute for Development Studies (PIDS), ~15% of the Philippine population lacks access to safely managed drinking water. Even in urban areas served by water utilities (Manila Water, Maynilad, local water districts), tap water quality varies and many households use point-of-use filtration. The Philippine water filtration market includes: (a) sediment filters (remove particulates — essential for most Philippine water supplies); (b) carbon filters (remove chlorine, taste, odor, and some organics); (c) UV sterilizers (kill bacteria and viruses — important for areas with intermittent water supply where pipe contamination occurs); (d) reverse osmosis (RO) systems (remove dissolved solids, heavy metals, fluoride — for areas with high TDS or well water); (e) water softeners (for areas with hard water, especially in limestone regions like Cebu, Cavite, and parts of Palawan). BuildRight Depot sells complete filtration systems (under-sink, countertop, whole-house) and individual components (filter housings, cartridges, UV lamps, RO membranes, fittings). Customers frequently purchase the wrong system because they don't know their water quality parameters (TDS level, hardness, chlorine content) or flow rate requirements. This consultative service matches system specifications to customer needs.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Water Quality & Usage Assessment**: Sales Associate gathers water source and usage information: (a) water source — municipal/city water district, deep well, shallow well, rainwater, community water system; (b) known water quality issues — sediment/cloudiness, chlorine taste/smell, hardness (scale buildup on fixtures), staining (iron/manganese — brown/orange), TDS (total dissolved solids — if customer has TDS meter reading), bacterial contamination (if known); (c) household size — number of persons (determines daily water demand); (d) filtration points — point-of-use (under-sink for drinking/cooking only), point-of-entry (whole-house for all taps); (e) installation location — under kitchen sink, outside house, garage/utility room; (f) budget range; (g) if customer is uncertain about water quality, recommend water testing kit (BuildRight stocks DIY test kits for pH, TDS, hardness, iron) or local water testing laboratory referral | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **System Type Recommendation**: System recommends filtration stages based on water quality assessment: (a) **Municipal water (typical quality)**: 3-stage under-sink system — (1) 5-micron sediment filter → (2) granular activated carbon (GAC) filter → (3) 1-micron carbon block filter; adequate for most Manila Water/Maynilad supplied areas; (b) **Municipal water with intermittent supply** (pressure fluctuations cause pipe contamination): 3-stage + UV sterilizer — add UV lamp (typically 11W or 24W depending on flow rate) after carbon filtration; (c) **Well water (high TDS, possible contamination)**: 5-stage RO system — (1) sediment → (2) carbon → (3) carbon block → (4) RO membrane (50–100 GPD) → (5) post-carbon polishing filter; plus UV for bacterial safety; (d) **Hard water** (scale buildup): water softener before filtration — ion exchange resin tank sized to household daily consumption and grain hardness; (e) **Whole-house system**: point-of-entry (POE) — larger filter housings (20" big blue) with sediment + carbon stages, sized to household peak flow rate (typical: 15–30 liters/min for 3–5 person household) | System / Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Component Specification & Sizing**: System generates bill of materials for recommended system: (a) filter housings — number of stages × housing size (10" standard for under-sink, 20" big blue for POE); (b) filter cartridges per stage — type, micron rating, replacement interval (sediment: 3–6 months, carbon: 6–12 months, RO membrane: 2–3 years); (c) UV sterilizer unit — wattage (11W for under-sink, 24W–40W for whole-house), replacement lamp interval: 12 months; (d) RO system components — booster pump (if inlet pressure <40 PSI), storage tank (3.2G or 10G), faucet; (e) water softener — resin tank size (1 cu.ft. for typical household), brine tank, control valve; (f) pipework and fittings — 1/4" tubing for under-sink RO, 1/2" or 3/4" pipe for POE; (g) installation accessories — mounting bracket, ball valves (inlet/outlet isolation), pressure gauge, TDS meter (for RO systems); (h) system calculates total cost and annual maintenance cost (replacement cartridges and UV lamp) | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate Generation & Customer Handoff**: (a) system generates estimate with: water quality assessment summary, recommended system diagram, bill of materials, total cost, annual maintenance schedule with replacement costs, estimated filter life based on household consumption; (b) offer installation service per W138; (c) offer annual filter replacement subscription reminder (SMS notification when filter replacement is due based on purchase date + estimated life); (d) estimate saved per W542 | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Water filtration system recommendation engine — water quality mapping to filtration stages
- Product master (W252) — filter specifications, cartridge compatibility, pricing
- Real-time inventory (W533)
- POS quotation (W542), installation services (W138)
- Customer notification engine — annual filter replacement reminders

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Water quality variability**: Without actual water test results, recommendations are based on typical source quality; actual conditions may differ; recommend DIY test kit before system purchase for >PHP 5,000 systems
- **Maintenance compliance**: Filters that are not replaced on schedule become breeding grounds for bacteria; system should send proactive replacement reminders


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-test of "Water Quality & Usage Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-post of "post-carbon polishing filter" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates total cost and annual maintenance cost" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: recommend DIY water test before system purchase for >PHP 5,000 systems; operational: proactive filter-replacement reminders

### Staffing Implication
- No permanent incremental headcount; ~1–2 hours/week of Sales Associate time per store

### Time Estimate
- Total per consultation: 11–18 min (average ~14 min)

## W1056. Store-Level Customer Construction Permit Advisory & Municipal Building Requirements Guidance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer asks about construction permit requirements during building materials consultation |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–5,000 advisory interactions/month across all stores |
| **Volume** | 1 advisory session per customer (often in conjunction with a materials estimation service) |
| **Owner** | Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials Section), Customer |

### Background

Philippine construction is governed by the National Building Code (PD 1096), the Fire Code (RA 9514), and local government unit (LGU) ordinances that impose additional requirements. Common permits and clearances required for residential and small commercial construction include: (a) Building Permit from the LGU Office of the Building Official (OBO); (b) Electrical Permit (separate from building permit in most LGUs); (c) Plumbing Permit; (d) Zoning Clearance from the LGU Zoning Administrator; (e) Fire Safety Inspection Certificate (FSIC) from BFP per W476; (f) Environmental Compliance Certificate (ECC) or Certificate of Non-Coverage (CNC) from DENR-EMB for larger projects; (g) LGU Barangay Clearance; (h) DPWH clearance for projects near national roads; (i) Homeowners' Association (HOA) approval for subdivision lots; (j) Local water district connection permit; (k) Net-Metering application with the local distribution utility (DU)/electric cooperative under ERC net-metering rules for solar installations. Filipino homeowners — particularly DIY renovators and first-time builders — are frequently unaware of permit requirements and begin construction without permits, risking: (i) stop-work order from the OBO; (ii) fines (typically 2× the permit fee); (iii) inability to obtain occupancy permit; (iv) insurance claim denial for unpermitted structures; (v) difficulty selling the property in the future. BuildRight Depot's advisory service does not provide legal or engineering advice but offers a structured information guide that helps customers understand the permit landscape for their project type. This builds customer trust, positions BuildRight as a knowledgeable partner, and often leads to larger material purchases as customers build with proper permits and higher-quality standards.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Classification**: Sales Associate identifies the customer's project type: (a) new residential construction (single-family, 1–3 stories); (b) residential renovation/remodel (interior, exterior, extension/addition); (c) small commercial construction (store, warehouse, workshop); (d) fence/wall construction; (e) swimming pool; (f) solar panel installation; (g) electrical upgrade (panel replacement, additional circuits); (h) plumbing modification (new bathroom, septic tank); (i) other. System displays the general permit requirements for the identified project type based on the customer's LGU jurisdiction (per W310 address hierarchy and LGU database) | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 2–3 min |
| 2 | **Permit Requirements Information Guide**: System generates a printable permit requirements guide for the customer's project type and LGU: (a) **New Residential Construction**: (i) Building Permit: signed and sealed plans by licensed architect and structural engineer, barangay clearance, tax declaration, vicinity map, fire safety plan (for ≥2 stories); (ii) Electrical Permit: electrical plan signed by licensed Professional Electrical Engineer (PEE); (iii) Plumbing Permit: plumbing plan signed by licensed Master Plumber; (iv) Zoning Clearance; (v) BFP FSIC (for ≥2 stories or commercial); (vi) typical permit processing time: 10–30 business days per LGU; (vii) typical permit cost: 0.5–1% of estimated construction cost; (b) **Renovation/Remodel**: (i) Building Permit may or may not be required depending on scope — structural changes, additional floor, or change of occupancy always require permit; cosmetic changes (painting, flooring, cabinets) typically do not; (ii) Electrical/Plumbing permits for any modification to existing systems; (c) **Fence/Wall**: (i) Building Permit typically required for fences >1.80m height per PD 1096; (ii) Barangay clearance; (d) **Solar Installation**: (i) Electrical Permit from LGU; (ii) Net-Metering application with local distribution utility (DU/EC); (iii) structural clearance if roof-mounted (additional dead load and wind load); (e) **Common LGU-Specific Additions**: (i) Cebu City: Additional Green Building compliance per local ordinance; (ii) Davao City: Rainwater collection system per Davao City ordinance; (iii) Quezon City: Green Building Code compliance for ≥10,000 sqm; (iv) system notes: "Requirements shown are general guidance only. Contact your LGU Office of the Building Official (OBO) for the specific and current requirements for your project" | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated generation) |
| 3 | **Professional Referral & Guidance**: (a) system provides referral list for professional services: (i) Licensed architects and civil engineers (from BuildRight's contractor referral database per W904); (ii) Licensed electricians and plumbers; (iii) BFP-accredited fire safety service providers (for fire safety plans); (b) system prints "Next Steps" checklist: (i) Contact LGU OBO for current requirements and fees; (ii) Engage licensed architect/engineer for plans and specifications; (iii) Obtain barangay clearance; (iv) Submit permit application; (v) Upon permit approval, return to BuildRight for materials quotation per W542 or W162; (c) system offers to save the requirements guide to customer's loyalty account for future reference | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 4 | **Materials Estimation Integration**: (a) if customer has their building plan and material specifications, Sales Associate can proceed to materials estimation services: W1044 (concrete calculator), W1043 (paint calculator), W1050 (staircase calculator), W983 (roofing calculator), W989 (plumbing layout), W991 (electrical circuit design); (b) if customer has a contractor, offer to send the requirements guide to their contractor via email/SMS for coordination; (c) if customer's project is a B2B trade/corporate account, refer to pro desk per W112 for full project quotation per W162 | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 2–5 min (optional continuation) |

### System Touchpoints
- Permit requirements database — project-type-to-permit mapping with LGU-specific variations
- LGU database per W310 (address & geographic hierarchy) — jurisdiction lookup
- Contractor referral module (W904) — professional service referrals
- Loyalty account (W17) — saved permit guides
- Materials estimation service modules — W1043, W1044, W1050, W983, W989, W991
- B2B quotation module (W162) — trade account project quotes
- Pro desk scheduling (W895) — professional service appointments

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Legal liability disclaimer**: BuildRight is not a licensed engineering, architectural, or legal advisory firm; all permit information must carry prominent disclaimer: "This information is provided as general guidance only and does not constitute legal, engineering, or architectural advice. Requirements vary by LGU and may change. Always verify with your local Office of the Building Official (OBO) and licensed professionals."
- **LGU requirement variability**: Philippine LGUs have widely varying permit requirements and processing times; the system cannot guarantee accuracy for every LGU; database must be reviewed and updated annually per W657 (regulatory change management)
- **Scope-creep risk**: Customers may expect BuildRight staff to assist with permit applications, plan preparation, or LGU interactions; staff must clearly communicate that BuildRight provides information guidance only, not permit facilitation services; mitigated by CTL-267 (controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2))


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "printable permit requirements guide for the customer's project type and LGU" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-validate of "Professional Referral & Guidance" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-count of "account, refer to pro desk per W112 for full project quotation per W162" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: annual permit-requirement database review and update per W657 (regulatory change management)

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Building Materials)**: Advisory session adds 5–10 min; most sessions are embedded within existing materials consultation; incremental time burden: ~1–2 hours/week per store
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project classification: 2–3 min
- Permit requirements guide: 1–2 min (automated)
- Professional referral & guidance: 3–5 min
- Materials integration (optional): 2–5 min
- **Total per advisory session**: 8–15 min (average ~10 min)

## W1057. Customer Insulation Material Calculator (Wall, Ceiling, Roof Thermal & Acoustic)

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests insulation quantity estimate for residential or small commercial building project |
| **Frequency** | ~600–1,000 consultations/month across all stores (insulation is a growing category driven by Philippine energy efficiency awareness and Green Building Code adoption) |
| **Volume** | 1 calculation per project |
| **Owner** | Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials Section), Customer |

### Background

Thermal and acoustic insulation is an emerging category in Philippine construction, historically underutilized due to the perception that Philippine tropical climate does not require insulation. However, the growing adoption of air conditioning (penetration rate: ~60% of Philippine households, rising to ~80% in urban areas) and increasing electricity costs (PHP 10–12/kWh residential rate) have created demand for insulation that reduces cooling energy consumption by 20–40%. Additionally, acoustic insulation is increasingly requested for: (a) condominiums and townhouses (shared walls); (b) home theaters and entertainment rooms; (c) bedrooms adjacent to noisy streets; (d) office meeting rooms. BuildRight Depot carries: (a) fiberglass blanket/batt insulation (R-11 to R-30, widths 400mm and 600mm); (b) rigid foam board insulation (polystyrene EPS/XPS, polyurethane PIR, 25mm–100mm thickness); (c) reflective foil insulation (radiant barrier for roof/attic applications); (d) rock wool insulation (fire-rated and acoustic, 50mm–100mm thickness); (e) spray foam insulation kits (for gap/void filling). This calculator helps customers determine the correct insulation material, thickness/R-value, and quantity for their specific application.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Application & Climate Assessment**: Sales Associate gathers project parameters: (a) insulation application — exterior wall, interior partition wall, ceiling (below attic), attic floor, roof (under-roof/above-ceiling), floor (above crawl space), acoustic partition; (b) building type — single-family detached, townhouse/rowhouse (shared wall), condominium, commercial; (c) air-conditioned spaces — yes/no (determines thermal insulation value); (d) climate zone — based on customer's location per W310 (Philippine climate zones: Type I — two pronounced seasons, Type II — no dry season, Type III — dry season short, Type IV — rainfall evenly distributed); (e) objective — thermal (reduce cooling cost), acoustic (noise reduction), both thermal and acoustic, fire-rated (compartmentalization); (f) wall construction — CHB (hollow block) with furring, wood frame, steel frame, concrete solid; (g) available cavity depth — determines maximum insulation thickness | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **R-Value & Material Recommendation**: System recommends insulation material and thickness: (a) **Thermal — exterior wall**: recommended R-value R-13 to R-19 for Philippine climate (lower than temperate countries due to smaller indoor-outdoor temperature differential); fiberglass batt R-13 (89mm) or R-19 (152mm) for framed walls; rigid foam board 50mm–75mm for CHB furred walls; (b) **Thermal — ceiling/roof (most impactful application in Philippines)**: recommended R-30 (241mm fiberglass) or equivalent in rigid board + radiant barrier for attic/roof applications; radiant barrier alone reduces radiant heat gain by 25–40% in Philippine metal roof installations; (c) **Acoustic — interior partition**: rock wool 50mm (STC 45–50) or fiberglass batt 89mm (STC 38–43) with resilient channel; (d) **Fire-rated partition**: rock wool 50mm–100mm (fire rating 1–2 hours) per BFP requirement per W476; (e) system recommends material based on: application, available cavity depth, objective (thermal/acoustic/fire), and budget | System / Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Area & Quantity Calculation**: System calculates insulation quantities: (a) total wall/ceiling/roof area = length × height (for walls) or length × width (for ceilings/roofs), minus deductions (windows, doors, openings); (b) for batt/blanket insulation: number of rolls = total area ÷ coverage per roll (product-specific, e.g., 11.6 m² per roll for 400mm × 6m batt), rounded up; (c) for rigid board insulation: number of boards = total area ÷ area per board (standard: 1.2m × 2.4m = 2.88 m² per board), rounded up; (d) for radiant barrier (roof): linear meters = roof ridge length × 1.15 (waste factor for overlapping joints), width per roll (typically 1.2m or 1.5m); (e) companion materials: (i) vapor barrier (polyethylene sheeting, 0.15mm, for warm-humid climate — install on exterior side of insulation), (ii) insulation supports/wire rods (for ceiling applications, @ 600mm O.C.), (iii) reflective tape for radiant barrier joints, (iv) sealant/caulk for gap sealing, (v) protective equipment: gloves, long-sleeve shirt, dust mask (for fiberglass per W172 PPE requirements) | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate Generation & Customer Handoff**: (a) system generates estimate with: application summary, recommended R-value and material, area calculation, complete bill of materials, total cost, energy savings estimate (for thermal applications: "Estimated cooling cost reduction: 20–30% for ceiling insulation, 10–15% for wall insulation"), installation overview; (b) check inventory per W533; (c) offer installation referral per W138/W904; (d) estimate saved per W542 | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Insulation calculator engine — R-value recommendation, area calculation, material selection
- Product master (W252) — insulation specifications, R-values per thickness, coverage per unit
- Real-time inventory (W533)
- POS quotation (W542), installation services (W138), contractor referral (W904)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Vapor barrier positioning**: In Philippine warm-humid climate, vapor barriers must be on the warm (exterior) side of the insulation to prevent condensation within the wall cavity; incorrect placement causes mold and rot; installation instructions must clearly specify this
- **Fire safety for rigid foam**: Polystyrene (EPS/XPS) insulation is combustible and must be covered with a thermal barrier (gypsum board minimum 12mm) when used in habitable spaces per PD 1096; system must display fire safety warning


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-distribute of "Application & Climate Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "countries due to smaller indoor-outdoor temperature differential" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates insulation quantities" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must display fire safety warning

### Staffing Implication
- No permanent incremental headcount; ~3–7 min/day of Sales Associate time per store (per the Frequency × Time Estimate derivation)

### Time Estimate
- Total per consultation: 9–15 min (average ~12 min)

## W1060. Customer Rebar Stirrup, Tying Wire & Binding Material Quantity Estimator Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests rebar accessory quantities (stirrups, tying wire, spacers) for a reinforced concrete project |
| **Frequency** | ~2,500–3,500 consultations/month across all stores (rebar and reinforcing accessories are core items in the lumber & building materials category — 14% of SKUs) |
| **Volume** | 1 estimation per project |
| **Owner** | Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials Section), Customer |

### Background

Reinforced concrete construction is the dominant building method in the Philippines. Rebar (deformed steel bar) is the skeletal reinforcement inside concrete elements — columns, beams, slabs, and footings. But rebar alone is not sufficient; it must be properly configured with: (a) stirrups (rectangular ties that wrap around the longitudinal rebar in columns and beams, providing shear reinforcement and confinement — typically 10mm bar bent into rectangles at 100–200mm spacing per structural engineer's specification); (b) tying wire (annealed black wire, typically 16-gauge, used to tie intersections of longitudinal bars and stirrups — consumes 1.0–1.5 kg per 100 kg of rebar); (c) concrete spacers/cover blocks (maintain specified concrete cover between rebar and formwork surface — typically 40mm for interior exposure, 50mm for exterior exposure per Philippine structural code); (d) mechanical couplers (for splicing rebar where lap splicing is impractical — higher-value item at PHP 50–200 per coupler). Filipino contractors and foremen routinely underestimate tying wire and stirrup quantities, leading to work stoppages and mid-project store runs. This estimator service provides precise quantities for all rebar accessories based on the structural element dimensions and rebar configuration, bundled with the rebar cutting/bending service per W986.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Structural Element & Rebar Configuration Input**: Sales Associate gathers project parameters: (a) structural element type — column, beam, slab, footing, retaining wall; (b) element dimensions — (i) column: width × depth × height; (ii) beam: width × depth × length; (iii) slab: length × width × thickness; (iv) footing: length × width × depth; (c) longitudinal rebar — diameter (10mm, 12mm, 16mm, 20mm, 25mm) and number of bars per element (typical: 4–8 bars per column, 4–6 bars per beam); (d) stirrup/tie bar diameter (typically 10mm for columns, 8–10mm for beams); (e) stirrup spacing per engineer's specification (typical: 100mm at ends/ joints, 200mm at midspan — or uniform 150mm for simple residential); (f) concrete cover (typical: 40mm interior, 50mm exterior); (g) if customer has structural plan: system accepts direct input of bar schedule (diameter, number, length, bending schedule) — provides the most accurate estimation | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Stirrup Quantity & Cutting Schedule Calculation**: System computes stirrup requirements: (a) stirrup perimeter per piece: for rectangular column/beam stirrup = 2 × (width − 2 × cover) + 2 × (depth − 2 × cover) + hook length (typically 75mm × 2 hooks); (b) stirrup length per piece = perimeter (including hooks); (c) number of stirrups = element length ÷ stirrup spacing + 1 (first stirrup at 50mm from end); (d) total stirrup bar length = number of stirrups × length per piece; (e) system converts to number of 6m standard bars: total stirrup bar length ÷ 6m, rounded up; (f) system generates cutting schedule for in-store rebar cutting/bending per W986: piece number, stirrup dimensions (inside width × inside depth), bend angle (90° × 4 corners), hook length, quantity; (g) for circular columns: stirrup perimeter = π × (diameter − 2 × cover) + overlap (typically 300mm for lap splice) | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Tying Wire, Spacer & Accessory Calculation**: System computes accessory quantities: (a) **Tying wire**: (i) total rebar weight (longitudinal bars + stirrups) = number of bars × length × weight per meter (per diameter); (ii) tying wire quantity = total rebar weight × 0.015 (1.5 kg wire per 100 kg rebar); (iii) sold in 1 kg coils — round up to nearest coil; (b) **Concrete spacers**: (i) for columns: 4 spacers per stirrup × number of stirrups (to maintain cover on each face); (ii) for beams: 2–4 spacers per stirrup; (iii) for slabs: 1 spacer per m² (distributed on chairs/supports); (iv) sold in bags of 50; (c) **Rebar chairs/supports** (for slab reinforcement): 1 per m² of slab area at the bottom mat; (d) **Mechanical couplers** (if specified by engineer): per bar splice schedule, each coupler replaces 1 lap splice; (e) **Rebar caps** (safety — cover exposed rebar ends per OSHA/DOLE safety per W140): 1 per exposed bar end | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate Generation, Cutting/Bending & Customer Handoff**: (a) system generates comprehensive estimate with: structural element summary, longitudinal rebar schedule (diameter, number, length, weight, cost), stirrup cutting schedule (per piece dimensions, number of pieces, 6m bar consumption, cost), tying wire quantity (kg, cost), spacers and accessories (quantity, cost), total project cost; (b) if customer requests in-store rebar cutting/bending for stirrups: system sends cutting schedule to W986 (rebar cutting & bending service) for execution; (c) for large orders (>500 kg rebar): offer site delivery per W964 (bulk delivery); (d) for trade/corporate accounts: convert to project quote per W162; (e) estimate saved per W542 | Sales Associate | Lumber & Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Rebar accessory estimator engine — stirrup geometry, tying wire calculation, spacer quantities
- Rebar cutting/bending service module (W986) — cutting schedule generation
- Product master (W252) — rebar specifications, weight per meter, pricing
- Real-time inventory (W533) — rebar and accessory availability
- Bulk delivery scheduling (W964) — large order delivery
- POS quotation (W542), B2B project quotation (W162)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Structural engineering disclaimer**: Rebar configuration (number of bars, diameter, stirrup spacing) is a structural design decision that must be made by a licensed civil engineer; system must display disclaimer: "Rebar quantities shown are based on customer-provided specifications. BuildRight does not provide structural engineering services. Consult a licensed civil engineer for proper rebar design per NSCP (National Structural Code of the Philippines)."
- **Stirrup bending accuracy**: In-store rebar bending per W986 must match the cutting schedule precisely; tolerance: ±3mm on stirrup inside dimensions; bent stirrups that don't fit around longitudinal bars are unusable


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-schedule of "Structural Element & Rebar Configuration Input" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-generate of "generates cutting schedule for in-store rebar cutting/bending per W986" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-distribute of "distributed on chairs/supports" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must display disclaimer: "Rebar quantities shown are based on customer-provided specifications. BuildRight does not provide structural engineering services. Consult a licensed civil engineer for proper rebar design per NSCP (National Structural Code of the Philippines)."

### Staffing Implication
- No permanent incremental headcount; ~2 hours/week of Sales Associate time per store

### Time Estimate
- Input: 5–8 min
- Calculation: 1–2 min (automated)
- Accessories: 1–2 min (automated)
- Estimate & handoff: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 10–17 min (average ~13 min)

## W1061. Customer Tile Grout, Adhesive & Thin-Set Mortar Quantity Calculator Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases tiles or requests tile installation material quantities |
| **Frequency** | ~4,000–6,000 consultations/month across all stores (tiles & flooring: 12% of SKUs, ~4,200 items — this is the natural companion service to W963 tile quantity calculator) |
| **Volume** | 1 calculation per tiling project |
| **Owner** | Tiles & Flooring Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Tiles Section), Customer |

### Background

W963 (Customer Tile & Flooring Quantity Calculator) covers the tile quantity itself but does not address the installation materials — grout, adhesive/thin-set mortar, and related supplies. These installation materials represent 15–25% of the total project cost and are frequently under-purchased because: (a) grout quantity depends on tile size, joint width, and joint depth — not just floor area; (b) thin-set/adhesive quantity depends on trowel notch size (which varies by tile size and type), substrate condition, and tile back texture; (c) different tile materials (ceramic, porcelain, natural stone, mosaic) require different adhesive types (standard thin-set, modified thin-set, epoxy, mastic); (d) Filipino DIY tilers frequently purchase only tiles and a single bag of adhesive, then discover mid-project that they need grout, spacers, waterproofing membrane (for wet areas), and crack isolation membrane — necessitating multiple store visits. This workflow is the direct companion to W963; where W963 calculates tile quantities, this workflow calculates all tile installation material quantities, ensuring the customer purchases everything needed in a single store visit.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Parameter Input (Integration with W963)**: Sales Associate gathers tiling project parameters (or pulls from W963 estimate if already completed): (a) tile type — ceramic, porcelain, natural stone (granite, marble, travertine), mosaic, vinyl tile; (b) tile dimensions — length × width × thickness (mm); (c) total tiled area (m²) from W963 or manual input; (d) application — floor, wall, countertop, backsplash, shower/tub surround (wet area), outdoor; (e) substrate — concrete slab, cement screed, existing tile (re-tiling), plywood, gypsum board (wall tile only); (f) joint width — 2mm (min. for rectified tiles), 3mm (standard for ceramic/porcelain), 5–10mm (for rustic/natural stone); (g) grout type preference — cementitious (standard), epoxy (wet areas/chemical resistance), urethane (stain resistant); (h) adhesive type — pre-mixed mastic (wall tile, dry areas only), standard thin-set mortar (dry-mix, cement-based), modified thin-set (polymer-modified, for porcelain and wet areas), epoxy adhesive (for chemical-resistant/industrial) | Sales Associate | Tiles & Flooring Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Adhesive/Thin-Set Quantity Calculation**: System computes adhesive quantity: (a) trowel notch size recommended per tile type: (i) tiles ≤100mm × 100mm (mosaic): 6mm V-notch; (ii) tiles 100–300mm: 8mm square-notch; (iii) tiles 300–600mm: 10mm square-notch; (iv) tiles >600mm or natural stone: 12mm square-notch; (b) adhesive coverage rate per trowel notch: 6mm = ~5 m² per 20kg bag; 8mm = ~4 m² per 20kg bag; 10mm = ~3 m² per 20kg bag; 12mm = ~2.5 m² per 20kg bag; (c) adjust for substrate condition: rough/unflat substrate: ×1.3 (additional material to compensate for unevenness); smooth/flat substrate: ×1.0; (d) total adhesive bags = total area ÷ coverage rate × substrate factor, rounded up; (e) for wet areas (shower surround, bathroom floor): add waterproofing membrane quantity = area ÷ coverage per roll (typically 10 m² per 20kg pail of liquid membrane, or 15 m² per roll of sheet membrane) | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Grout Quantity Calculation**: System computes grout quantity: (a) grout volume formula per linear meter of joint: joint depth (mm) × joint width (mm) × 1m ÷ 1,000,000 = m³ per linear meter; (b) joint depth = tile thickness (full depth for full-bed installation; or 2/3 of tile thickness for thin-bed); (c) total joint length (linear meters): (i) for rectangular tiles: total area ÷ tile length + total area ÷ tile width = approximate total joint length; (ii) system adds perimeter joint length (perimeter of tiled area — expansion joint); (d) total grout volume = total joint length × grout volume per linear meter; (e) grout density: cementitious grout ≈ 1,600 kg/m³ (1.6 kg/L); epoxy grout ≈ 1,800 kg/m³ (1.8 kg/L); (f) total grout weight (kg) = total grout volume × density; (g) bags of grout = total weight ÷ bag size (1kg, 2kg, or 5kg), rounded up; (h) for epoxy grout: system calculates Part A (resin) and Part B (hardener) units | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Complete Installation Material List & Estimate**: System generates full bill of materials: (a) **Adhesive/thin-set**: type, number of bags, price; (b) **Grout**: type, color options, number of bags/units, price; (c) **Tile spacers**: 1 bag per 5 m² (bag of 200 pieces); size matches joint width (2mm, 3mm, 5mm); (d) **Waterproofing membrane** (if wet area): type, quantity, price; (e) **Primer** (if needed for modified thin-set on non-porous substrate): 1 liter per 4 m²; (f) **Notched trowel**: 1 per project (size per step 2a); (g) **Grout float**: 1 per project; (h) **Sponge and bucket**: 1 per project (for grout cleanup); (i) **Tile cutter rental**: per W139 (tool rental) or purchase; (j) **Rubber mallet**: 1 per project (for tapping tiles level); (k) **Level** (600mm or 1200mm): 1 per project; (l) **Crack isolation membrane** (optional): for substrates with existing cracks; (m) system calculates total project cost including all materials; (n) system generates estimate per W542 with optional installation referral per W138/W904 | System / Sales Associate | Tiles & Flooring Dept Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |

### System Touchpoints
- Tile installation material calculator engine — adhesive, grout, accessories
- Integration with W963 (tile quantity calculator) — pulls tile dimensions and area
- Product master (W252) — adhesive specifications, grout coverage data, pricing
- Real-time inventory (W533)
- POS quotation (W542), tool rental (W139), installation referral (W138/W904)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Substrate condition unknown**: Customer-reported substrate condition may be inaccurate; rough substrates consume 30% more adhesive than estimated; recommend adding 20% adhesive contingency for first-time DIY customers
- **Grout color matching**: Grout color appearance changes after drying; recommend customer purchase a small test quantity first and apply to a few tiles to verify color match before committing to full quantity
- **Epoxy grout difficulty**: Epoxy grout has a short working time (30–45 min in Philippine heat) and is difficult to clean; recommend professional installation per W138 for epoxy grout projects


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "countertop, backsplash, shower/tub surround" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "Grout Quantity Calculation" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-generate of "generates full bill of materials" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- No permanent incremental headcount; this service is naturally embedded in tile section consultations (~5 min incremental time per customer)

### Time Estimate
- Parameter input: 3–5 min
- Adhesive calculation: 1–2 min (automated)
- Grout calculation: 1–2 min (automated)
- Complete material list: 2–3 min (automated)
- **Total per consultation**: 7–12 min (average ~9 min) — typically runs in parallel with W963 tile quantity calculation
