## W1082. Store-Level Customer Construction Material Quality Verification & Grade Certification Assistance

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests material quality verification, grade certification, mill test certificate, or product compliance documentation for construction materials purchased at BuildRight (or being specified for a project) |
| **Frequency** | ~1,500–2,500 requests/month across all stores (driven by government project audit requirements, bank loan collateral verification, and quality-conscious B2B customers) |
| **Volume** | 1 request per material type per project |
| **Owner** | Building Materials Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Building Materials Section), Receiving Clerk (for stock verification), Customer |

### Background

Material quality verification is critical in Philippine construction due to the prevalence of substandard construction materials in the market. The Philippine Department of Trade and Industry (DTI) and the Department of Public Works and Highways (DPWH) have identified recurring issues with: (a) substandard rebar (undersized diameter, below-grade yield strength, uncertified origin); (b) substandard cement (adulterated with ash or limestone powder, expired product); (c) substandard GI sheets (below-specified zinc coating thickness, thinner than labeled gauge); (d) substandard electrical wire (copper content below specification, undersized conductor cross-section); (e) substandard plumbing fittings (brittle PVC, undersized wall thickness). Philippine construction standards that apply: PNS 49 (steel bars for concrete reinforcement), PNS 63 (Portland cement), PNS 67 (GI steel sheets), PNS 35 (PVC pipes), PNS 90 (electrical wires and cables). For government projects, DPWH requires mill test certificates and product testing from accredited laboratories for all critical structural materials. For bank-financed projects, lenders may require material quality documentation before releasing construction loan tranches. For private B2B projects, quality-conscious contractors require traceability and certification to ensure the materials they install meet engineering specifications. BuildRight Depot, as a reputable retailer, must be able to provide quality documentation for the materials it sells. This workflow governs how BuildRight handles customer requests for material quality verification and certification documentation.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Quality Documentation Request Intake**: Sales Associate identifies the request type: (a) product certification request: customer needs mill test certificate (MTC), certificate of conformance (CoC), or product test report for specific materials purchased or being considered for purchase; (b) product inspection request: customer wants to visually inspect and verify product specifications (e.g., rebar diameter measurement, GI sheet thickness gauge) before purchase; (c) batch/lot traceability: customer needs to trace specific material batch to manufacturer and production date; (d) product testing referral: customer needs independent third-party laboratory testing of materials for a specific project requirement; (e) material type: steel/rebar, cement, GI sheets, electrical wire, PVC pipes, timber/lumber, paint, roofing, other; (f) customer type: trade/B2B contractor, government project, bank-financed project, retail homeowner; (g) project requirement: is this for DPWH compliance, bank loan requirement, LGU building permit, or customer's own quality assurance? | Sales Associate | Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Product Certification Retrieval**: System retrieves available certification: (a) system queries product master (W252) for material specifications and available certifications; (b) for steel/rebar: retrieve mill test certificate (MTC) from supplier — includes heat number, chemical composition (carbon, manganese, phosphorus, sulfur), mechanical properties (yield strength, tensile strength, elongation), and PNS 49 compliance statement; MTCs are stored in ERP per supplier per heat number per delivery lot; (c) for cement: retrieve certificate of conformance from manufacturer — includes product type (Type I, Type IP, Type V per PNS 63), compressive strength test results (7-day and 28-day), and manufacturing date (for shelf life verification); (d) for GI sheets: retrieve mill certificate — includes zinc coating weight (AZ70, AZ150 per PNS 67), base metal thickness (gauge), and coil/lot number; (e) for electrical wire: retrieve product test report — includes conductor cross-section (mm²), insulation thickness, voltage rating, and PNS 90 compliance; (f) for PVC pipes: retrieve certificate — includes pipe class (Schedule 40, Sch 80 per PNS 35), wall thickness, pressure rating, and lot number; (g) system retrieves stored certificates from document management system per W255; if certificate not available for specific lot, system flags for supplier request | System | Sales Associate | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 3 | **In-Store Product Verification** (if applicable): For customers requesting physical verification: (a) rebar: Sales Associate and customer verify rebar diameter using caliper (10mm rebar must measure 10.0mm ± 0.3mm per PNS 49), check for mill marking/brand stamp, check surface condition (no rust, no cracks, no bends); (b) GI sheets: verify gauge thickness using sheet metal gauge tool, verify zinc coating visually (no bare spots), check corrugation profile matches specification; (c) cement: check bag integrity (no tears, no moisture damage), check manufacturing date (cement older than 3 months should not be sold per PNS 63), check PNS mark and DTI certification mark on bag; (d) electrical wire: verify conductor diameter (strip insulation and measure), verify insulation quality (no nicks, no discoloration), check PNS mark on insulation printing; (e) timber: verify species and grade marking per W976, check moisture content (if moisture meter available), check for defects (knots, splits, warping) | Sales Associate / Receiving Clerk | Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Documentation Package & Testing Referral**: (a) system generates quality documentation package: product specifications, available certificates (MTC, CoC, test reports), delivery traceability (supplier → DC → store), BuildRight's supplier quality assurance statement; (b) for government/DPWH projects: documentation package formatted per DPWH requirements for material submission; (c) for projects requiring independent testing: referral to accredited testing laboratories in the Philippines (e.g., DOST-ITDI, UP ICE Structural Engineering Lab, private labs: SGS Philippines, TÜV SÜD Philippines, Intertek) with contact information and typical testing fees (concrete cylinder test: PHP 500–1,500 per set; rebar tensile test: PHP 1,000–2,500 per sample; cement test: PHP 3,000–5,000 per sample); (d) system notes: "BuildRight Depot sources materials from DTI-certified and PNS-compliant manufacturers per supplier quality management program W110. All incoming materials undergo receiving inspection per W681. BuildRight does not perform independent laboratory testing of materials; customers requiring project-specific testing should engage an accredited third-party laboratory."; (e) documentation saved to customer's project file per W162 (trade accounts) or printed for walk-in customers | Sales Associate | Building Materials Dept Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Product master (W252) — material specifications, PNS standards compliance
- Certificate/document management system (W255) — stored MTCs, CoCs, test reports
- Oracle Quality supplier quality (W110 — fit-gap C12)
- Receiving inspection module (W681) — incoming quality records
- Product lot/traceability module (batch tracking from supplier to store)
- Material review board integration (W819) — for quality rejection cases
- POS transaction module (W5) — linking certificates to specific sales transactions
- Trade account project file (W162)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Certificate availability**: Not all products have MTCs readily available; some certificates must be requested from suppliers (lead time: 1–5 business days); system must manage customer expectations on turnaround time
- **Substandard product risk**: If BuildRight inadvertently stocks substandard materials, customer quality verification could expose the issue; this is actually a positive outcome (prevents use of substandard materials) but requires rapid escalation per W819 (material review board) and W110 (supplier CAPA)
- **Testing cost**: Independent laboratory testing costs PHP 500–5,000 per test; customers may not expect this cost; BuildRight should communicate this upfront
- **Certificate accuracy**: Mill test certificates represent the manufacturing lot's average properties, not individual piece properties; customer must understand this limitation


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-document of "Documentation Request Intake" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-schedule of "Schedule 40, Sch 80 per PNS 35" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "In-Store Product Verification" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must manage customer expectations on turnaround time

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Building Materials)**: Quality documentation request adds 5–10 minutes per request; at 8–12 requests per store per month, this represents ~1–2 hours/month; absorbed by existing staff
- **Receiving Clerk**: Physical verification inspection adds 10–15 minutes per inspection; at 3–5 inspections per store per month, this represents ~30 min–1.5 hours/month; absorbed by existing Receiving Clerks
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Request intake: 3–5 min
- Certificate retrieval: 2–3 min (automated)
- In-store verification (if applicable): 5–10 min
- Documentation & referral: 3–5 min
- **Total per request**: 13–23 min (with verification) or 8–13 min (documentation only)

## W1083. Store-Level Customer Plumbing Fixture & Fitting Compatibility Verification Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer brings a plumbing fixture, fitting, valve, or pipe sample to the store for matching, or requests help designing a plumbing layout for a residential or small commercial project |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–12,000 customer interactions/month across all stores (plumbing is 12% of SKUs, ~4,200 items) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 fixture compatibility checks per customer visit |
| **Owner** | Plumbing Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Plumbing Section), Customer |

### Background

Plumbing fixture compatibility is one of the most confusing aspects of DIY home improvement in the Philippines. The Philippine plumbing market has a mix of standards: (a) BSP (British Standard Pipe) threaded fittings — the dominant standard for galvanized iron (GI) pipes and brass fittings in the Philippines, available in ½", ¾", 1", 1¼", 1½", and 2" nominal sizes; (b) NPT (National Pipe Thread) — found on some imported fixtures, particularly from US-branded products; (c) compression fittings for PPR (polypropylene random copolymer) pipes — increasingly popular for new construction, using heat-fusion joints rather than threaded connections; (d) push-fit connectors (like SharkBite) — gaining market share for repair work; (e) PVC solvent-weld fittings — for DWV (drain-waste-vent) systems in sizes 2", 3", 4". Filipino homeowners frequently encounter mismatched threads, incompatible pipe materials (joining GI to PPR to PVC), faucet-to-valve incompatibilities (wall-mounted vs. deck-mounted, single-hole vs. widespread), and drain assembly mismatches (Philippine sinks often use different overflow and waste fitting dimensions than imported ones). The resulting return rate on plumbing fixtures is the highest of any category at BuildRight (~12–15%), largely driven by incompatibility rather than defect. A structured compatibility verification service at the plumbing counter reduces returns, increases basket size through correct companion product sales, and builds customer trust. This workflow complements W989 (plumbing layout design) which focuses on the overall pipe routing and material takeoff; this workflow specifically addresses the point-of-selection compatibility verification at the fixture and fitting level.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Project & Fixture Context Gathering**: Sales Associate identifies the customer's need: (a) replacement part matching — customer brings existing fixture/fitting for replacement; (b) new installation — customer is building or renovating and needs a complete plumbing fixture set; (c) repair — customer has a leak or break and needs the correct repair part; (d) upgrade — customer wants to replace an older fixture with a modern equivalent. Associate records: fixture location (bathroom, kitchen, laundry, outdoor), fixture type (faucet, valve, drain, toilet, shower head, water heater connection), existing pipe material visible at installation point (GI, PPR, PVC, copper, PE), and approximate pipe diameter if known | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Physical Sample Inspection & Thread/Material Identification**: For customers bringing samples: (a) Associate measures thread diameter using a thread pitch gauge (BSP threads have ½" BSP ≈ 21mm OD, ¾" BSP ≈ 26.5mm OD, 1" BSP ≈ 33.3mm OD); (b) identifies thread type: male vs. female, tapered (BSPT) vs. parallel (BSPP); (c) identifies material: GI (silver-gray, magnetic), brass (gold/yellow, non-magnetic), PPR (green or white plastic), PVC (white or gray plastic, lighter than PPR), copper (reddish-brown), PE (black flexible tubing); (d) checks seal type required: Teflon tape, pipe sealant (locktite), rubber gasket, O-ring, or solvent cement; (e) for faucets: identifies mounting type (wall-mount, deck-mount, single-hole, centerset, widespread), handle configuration (single-lever, dual-handle), and cartridge type (ceramic disc, compression, ball); (f) for drains: measures drain hole diameter (standard Philippine wash basin: 1¼" or 1½"), checks overflow presence/absence, identifies trap type (P-trap, S-trap, bottle trap) | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **System-Assisted Compatibility Lookup**: System performs compatibility matching: (a) system matches the identified thread type, size, and material against compatible products in inventory; (b) cross-reference table maintained in product attribute master (W298) links every faucet model to compatible valves, supply hoses, drain assemblies, and mounting hardware; (c) system flags incompatibilities: e.g., "Selected faucet requires ½" BSP female inlet. Customer's existing supply line is ⅜" compression. Adapter fitting needed: ⅜" compression × ½" BSP male (SKU #XXXX)"; (d) system recommends transition fittings for cross-material joins: GI-to-PPR transition union, PVC-to-GI threaded adapter, PPR-to-copper compression fitting; (e) for toilets: system verifies rough-in distance (standard Philippine: 12" or 10" from wall to closet flange center), bowl shape (round vs. elongated), flush type (gravity, pressure-assisted, dual-flush), and water supply connection type; (f) system displays compatibility status for each selected item: ✓ Compatible, ⚠ Adapter Required (with specific SKU), ✗ Not Compatible (with alternative recommendation) | System | Sales Associate | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Complete Fixture Set Assembly & Companion Products**: System generates a complete fixture package with all required accessories: (a) for a faucet installation: faucet body + supply hoses (hot/cold, correct length) + angle stop valves (matching pipe type) + Teflon tape + mounting hardware + escutcheon/deck plate; (b) for a toilet installation: toilet bowl + tank + closet flange + wax ring/sponge gasket + toilet bolts + supply valve + flexible supply hose + toilet seat cover; (c) for a shower installation: shower valve (concealed vs. exposed) + shower head + arm + diverter (if combined with tub spout) + hot/cold supply connections + rough-in valve body; (d) recommended tools: adjustable wrench, basin wrench, pipe cutter (for material type), Teflon tape, plumber's putty, silicone sealant; (e) for trade account customers, system applies contracted pricing per W163 and offers bulk quantity breaks | System / Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 5 | **Estimate Generation & Installation Advisory**: (a) system generates itemized estimate with compatibility notes for each item; (b) installation advisory printed on estimate: "Plumbing installations in the Philippines must comply with the National Plumbing Code (NPC) and local LGU plumbing permit requirements. BuildRight Depot recommends engaging a licensed Master Plumber for installations. This compatibility verification is for material selection assistance only and does not constitute a plumbing design."; (c) for customers needing installation, system offers referral to licensed plumber partners per W904 (contractor referral service); (d) estimate saved to customer loyalty account; (e) system offers to schedule a store-level plumbing consultation appointment for complex projects requiring W989 (plumbing layout design) | Sales Associate | Plumbing Department Supervisor | 2–3 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Product attribute master (W298) — fixture compatibility cross-reference database
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- POS quotation module (W542) — estimate-to-sale conversion
- Loyalty account integration (W17)
- Return rate analytics dashboard (category-level return tracking by SKU)
- Contractor referral service module (W904)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Thread standard confusion**: BSP vs. NPT mismatch is the #1 cause of plumbing returns; Associates must be trained to measure and identify thread types accurately
- **Mixed-material plumbing systems**: Many Filipino homes have a patchwork of GI, PVC, and PPR pipes from decades of incremental repairs; transition fittings must cover every combination
- **Imported fixture incompatibility**: Imported faucets and fixtures (particularly from China and the US) may use non-BSP threads or non-standard drain sizes; system must flag these and show required adapters
- **Water pressure variation**: Philippine water pressure varies dramatically (from gravity-fed systems at ~15 PSI to pressurized systems at 40–80 PSI); fixture selection (particularly shower valves) must account for local pressure conditions


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-record of "Customer Project & Fixture Context Gathering" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-capture of "Physical Sample Inspection & Thread/Material Identification" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-match of "System-Assisted Compatibility Lookup" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: imported non-BSP fixtures flagged at POS with the required adapter SKU before sale

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Plumbing Section)**: Compatibility verification adds 5–10 minutes per consultation; at ~40–60 consultations per store per month, this represents ~5–8 hours/month — absorbed by existing Plumbing Department staff (12 Sales Associates per store covering all departments)
- **Plumbing Department Supervisor**: Handles escalations for unusual compatibility questions; ~1 hour/week
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — the service reduces return processing labor (estimated 2–3 hours/week recovered per store)

### Time Estimate
- Context gathering: 3–5 min
- Sample inspection: 3–5 min
- System compatibility lookup: 2–3 min
- Fixture set assembly: 3–5 min
- Estimate & advisory: 2–3 min
- **Total per consultation**: 13–21 min

## W1084. Customer Home Security & CCTV System Design & Product Recommendation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests a home security system design, CCTV camera layout, or burglar alarm system recommendation for residential or small commercial property |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,500 customer interactions/month across all stores (growing segment driven by Philippine crime rates and affordable IP camera technology) |
| **Volume** | 1 system design per customer visit |
| **Owner** | Electrical Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Electrical Section), Customer |

### Background

Home security is a rapidly growing category for Philippine hardware retailers. BuildRight carries CCTV cameras (IP and analog), digital video recorders (DVR/NVR), burglar alarm systems, smart locks, motion sensors, door/window contact sensors, and accessories. The Philippine residential security market is driven by: (a) high urban crime rates (break-ins, theft) especially in Metro Manila and growing cities like Davao and Cebu; (b) the proliferation of affordable IP cameras and WiFi-enabled smart security devices from brands like Dahua, Hikvision, TP-Link Tapo, Imou, and EZVIZ; (c) increasing OFW (Overseas Filipino Worker) families who want remote monitoring of their Philippine homes; (d) small business owners (sari-sari stores, workshops, warehouses) who need affordable surveillance; (e) barangay and community security initiatives. The typical Filipino homeowner is not technically trained and struggles with camera selection (resolution, lens type, night vision range), storage sizing (HDD capacity for NVR), network requirements (bandwidth for remote viewing), and power supply planning (PoE vs. separate power adapters). This is distinct from W1071 (smart home system design) which covers the broader smart home ecosystem; this workflow focuses specifically on the security and surveillance function.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Property Assessment & Security Requirements**: Sales Associate gathers property and security details: (a) property type: single-detached house, townhouse, condominium, apartment, small commercial (store, workshop, office); (b) property size: lot area (sqm), number of floors, perimeter length (meters); (c) entry points: number of doors (main, back, side, garage), number of ground-floor windows, garage/carport presence; (d) security priorities: perimeter monitoring, entry point detection, indoor monitoring, vehicle monitoring, nanny/caregiver monitoring; (e) remote access requirement: customer wants to view cameras from mobile phone (yes/no), frequency of remote viewing, number of household members who need access; (f) internet connectivity: WiFi available (speed if known), wired Ethernet availability at planned camera locations, cellular signal strength (for 4G cameras in remote areas); (g) power availability: outlets near planned camera locations, willingness to run new electrical wiring; (h) budget range: entry-level (PHP 3,000–8,000), mid-range (PHP 8,000–20,000), premium (PHP 20,000–50,000+) | Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **CCTV Camera Layout & Coverage Design**: System generates camera placement plan: (a) perimeter cameras: one camera per 8–10 meters of boundary wall/fence coverage, mounted at 2.5–3m height, wide-angle lens (2.8mm for 100° FOV or 3.6mm for 85° FOV); (b) entry point cameras: dedicated camera for each main door and vehicle gate; (c) outdoor cameras: IP67 weather-rated minimum, IR night vision range ≥ 20m, true WDR (wide dynamic range) for backlit entrances; (d) indoor cameras: for monitoring main living areas, choose dome-style (less obtrusive) with 2.8mm lens; (e) camera resolution recommendation based on budget: 2MP (1080p) minimum for general monitoring, 4MP/5MP for license plate recognition at gates, 8MP (4K) for premium detail; (f) special cameras: PTZ (pan-tilt-zoom) for large open areas, doorbell camera (WiFi) for main entrance, 4G solar camera for areas without power/WiFi; (g) system outputs a camera count and a recommended placement floor plan sketch | System | Sales Associate | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Recording, Storage & Network Design**: System specifies recording infrastructure: (a) NVR (network video recorder) channel count = camera count + 20% spare capacity for future expansion; (b) storage calculation: recording days desired × cameras × bitrate × hours/day ÷ 8 ÷ 1024 = TB required (typical: 4 cameras at 2Mbps ≈ 648 GB/camera/month → ~2.6 TB total → 3TB surveillance-rated HDD minimum); (c) HDD selection: surveillance-rated HDD (WD Purple, Seagate SkyHawk) required (standard desktop HDDs fail under 24/7 write load); (d) network design: for IP cameras on existing WiFi — max 2–3 cameras per WiFi access point to avoid bandwidth congestion; for 4+ cameras — recommend PoE (Power over Ethernet) NVR with built-in PoE switch for reliability; (e) remote viewing: system confirms mobile app compatibility (iOS/Android), cloud storage options (optional subscription), and router configuration requirements (port forwarding or P2P cloud access) | System | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–4 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Alarm & Accessory Selection**: System recommends complementary security components: (a) door/window magnetic contact sensors for entry point detection; (b) PIR (passive infrared) motion sensors for indoor zones; (c) siren/strobe alarm (120dB minimum for audible deterrence); (d) smart lock for main door (fingerprint, PIN, RFID, or Bluetooth); (e) UPS (uninterruptible power supply) for NVR and network — critical in Philippines where power outages are common and burglars may cut power; (f) cable accessories: Cat6 Ethernet cable (for PoE cameras), power extension cables, cable conduits, junction boxes; (g) mounting hardware: camera brackets, pole mounts, cable clips; (h) system recommends installation tools: drill, Ethernet crimper, fish tape, cable tester | System / Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–4 min |
| 5 | **Complete System BOM, Estimate & Advisory**: (a) system generates complete BOM with itemized pricing: cameras, NVR, HDD, cables, sensors, accessories, UPS, tools; (b) for trade/B2B customers, applies contract pricing per W163; (c) estimate includes: camera count summary, storage capacity confirmation, network requirement summary, estimated installation time (DIY: 4–8 hours for 4-camera system; professional: 1–2 days); (d) advisory printed: "CCTV installation is not regulated in the Philippines for residential use. However, cameras must not be pointed at neighboring properties' private areas. Audio recording without consent may violate RA 10173 (Data Privacy Act). BuildRight Depot recommends professional installation for optimal camera positioning and cable management."; (e) system offers referral to licensed security system installers per W904; (f) estimate saved to customer loyalty account | Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Product attribute master (W298) — camera specifications, NVR compatibility, HDD compatibility matrix
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- POS quotation module (W542)
- Loyalty account integration (W17)
- Contractor referral service module (W904)
- BOM/kit assembly module (W46)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **WiFi reliability**: Many customers underestimate WiFi limitations; cameras on WiFi may experience dropouts, especially in Philippine concrete homes (hollow block/CHB walls severely attenuate WiFi signals); PoE recommendation must be emphasized
- **Power outage vulnerability**: Philippines experiences frequent power interruptions; without a UPS, the security system is offline during outages — precisely when break-ins may occur; UPS must be recommended in every system design
- **Storage technology confusion**: Customers often select standard desktop HDDs instead of surveillance-rated HDDs, leading to premature failure and loss of footage; system must enforce surveillance-HDD-only recommendation
- **Privacy considerations**: RA 10173 (Data Privacy Act) implications for audio recording and neighbor-facing cameras must be communicated


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-monitor of "monitoring, entry point detection, indoor monitoring, vehicle monitoring,…" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "count and a recommended placement floor plan sketch" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-record of "Recording, Storage & Network Design" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: UPS recommended in every system design so coverage survives outages; operational: must enforce surveillance-HDD-only recommendation

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Electrical Section)**: Security system design consultation adds 15–25 minutes per customer; at ~10–18 consultations per store per month, this represents ~3–6 hours/month — absorbed by existing Electrical Department staff
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Property assessment: 5–8 min
- Camera layout design: 3–5 min
- Storage & network design: 3–4 min
- Alarm & accessory selection: 3–4 min
- BOM & advisory: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 17–26 min

## W1089. Store-Level Customer Tile & Flooring Digital Room Visualizer Kiosk Operations

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer visits the tile & flooring gallery and requests visual preview of tile options in a room setting, or Sales Associate proactively directs customer to the visualizer kiosk during tile selection |
| **Frequency** | ~6,000–10,000 kiosk sessions/month across all stores (tiles & flooring represent 12% of SKUs, ~4,200 items, and are one of the highest-value per-unit categories) |
| **Volume** | 1 room visualization per session (customer may run 2–3 sessions per visit) |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Tiles & Flooring Section) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Tiles Section), Customer |

### Background

Tile and flooring selection is one of the most visual and emotional purchase decisions in home improvement. Customers struggle to imagine how a specific tile pattern, color, or size will look across an entire room from a small sample piece. Digital room visualizers — touchscreen kiosks or tablets that superimpose tile selections onto photographs of room templates — have become a standard tool in tile retail worldwide. BuildRight's tile & flooring gallery is a dedicated section in each store (per model company profile, Section 3.1) and represents a high-value category: average tile purchase per customer project is PHP 15,000–80,000 (a typical bathroom uses 15–25 sqm of tiles at PHP 300–800/sqm), and the category drives significant companion sales (adhesive, grout, waterproofing, tile spacers, tiling tools). The kiosk runs BuildRight's proprietary or white-label tile visualization software: (a) pre-loaded room templates (bathroom, kitchen, living room, outdoor patio, commercial space) in Filipino architectural styles (typical Filipino bathroom dimensions: 1.5m × 2.5m to 2.5m × 3.5m; living room: 3m × 5m to 5m × 7m); (b) ability for customers to upload a photo of their own room (via QR code scan from phone to kiosk); (c) real-time tile pattern application: the system maps tile dimensions (30×30cm, 30×60cm, 40×40cm, 60×60cm, 60×120cm plank-style) onto the room surface with correct grout lines; (d) color and pattern search: filter tiles by color family, material (ceramic, porcelain, vitrified, granite, marble-look), finish (glossy, matte, satin, textured, anti-slip), application (floor, wall, outdoor); (e) quantity estimation: system calculates tile quantity needed based on room dimensions plus 10% wastage factor. This workflow is distinct from W963 (tile quantity calculator) which covers the calculation service; this workflow focuses on the digital visualization experience and its integration with the sales process.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Onboarding & Room Setup**: (a) Sales Associate welcomes customer to tile visualizer kiosk; (b) customer selects room type from templates: bathroom, kitchen, living room, bedroom, outdoor patio, commercial/office, or custom (upload own photo via QR code); (c) customer enters room dimensions (length × width in meters) or selects from common Filipino room sizes; (d) customer selects surface to visualize: floor only, walls only, floor + accent wall, or floor + all walls; (e) system loads 3D room model with selected dimensions and surface areas | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Tile Selection & Real-Time Visualization**: (a) customer browses tile options on the kiosk: filter by collection, color, material, size, price range, and SRP per piece/sqm; (b) system displays only tiles currently in stock at the specific store per W533 (real-time inventory); out-of-stock tiles shown grayed out with "available at DC — 2-day delivery" option; (c) customer taps a tile to apply it to the room surface: system renders the tile pattern in real-time with correct scale, grout line width (typically 2–3mm), and tile layout pattern (straight/break-joint, diagonal, herringbone, brick-bond); (d) customer can view different tile combinations: e.g., floor tile + wall tile + accent border tile, each independently selectable; (e) system provides 360° rotation and zoom for the rendered room; (f) Associate offers design guidance: "For small bathrooms, lighter colors and larger format tiles make the space feel bigger. The 60×60cm tiles with fewer grout lines are trending for modern Filipino homes."; (g) customer may save multiple design options to compare side-by-side | Customer / System | Sales Associate | 5–10 min |
| 3 | **Companion Material Auto-Calculation**: System automatically calculates the full material list based on the selected tile design and room dimensions: (a) floor tile quantity = room area (sqm) ÷ tile coverage per piece × 1.10 (10% wastage) — rounded up to full boxes; (b) wall tile quantity = wall area (sqm) ÷ tile coverage per piece × 1.10 — minus door and window openings; (c) tile adhesive quantity = area (sqm) × 4–5 kg/sqm (depending on tile size and trowel notch); (d) grout quantity = area (sqm) × 0.3–0.5 kg/sqm (depending on grout line width and tile thickness); (e) waterproofing for bathroom walls (mandatory in Philippine construction): area × 1.5 kg/sqm × 2 coats; (f) tile spacers (2mm or 3mm): 1 bag per 5 sqm; (g) tiling tools: notched trowel, rubber grout float, tile cutter (manual or rental per W139), sponge, bucket; (h) for outdoor tiles: drainage mat and cement board underlayment if specified; (i) system calculates total estimated cost of materials; (j) for trade accounts, applies contract pricing per W163 | System | Sales Associate | 1–2 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Design Save, Estimate & Sharing**: (a) customer saves visualization to their loyalty account — accessible via mobile app or web for later review; (b) system generates a PDF estimate containing: room visualization render (before/after), itemized material list with quantities and prices, total estimated cost, tile specifications (material, size, finish, SRP per piece and per sqm), store inventory availability per line item, and estimated delivery timeline for DC-sourced items; (c) customer can share visualization via email, Viber, or Messenger (popular in the Philippines) to show family members or contractor; (d) customer can request a printed copy at the kiosk (thermal printer attached); (e) system offers conversion to project quotation per W162 for B2B/trade customers; (f) estimate is linked to customer loyalty account and tagged with "Visualizer Lead" for follow-up per W756 (post-purchase follow-up adapted for pre-purchase) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 2–3 min |
| 5 | **Kiosk Maintenance & Content Updates**: (a) daily: Sales Associate powers on kiosk at store opening, verifies touchscreen calibration, checks network connectivity, and confirms tile catalog synchronization with ERP inventory per W533; (b) weekly: Associate cleans kiosk screen and enclosure, checks printer paper and ink, and verifies that the latest tile products and pricing are loaded (sync with merchandising system per W50 PIM); (c) monthly: IT support per W265 (POS terminal hardware maintenance adapted for kiosk) performs software update, cache clearing, and diagnostic check; (d) quarterly: tile catalog content refresh — new product images loaded, discontinued products removed per W68 (product lifecycle & discontinuation), new room templates added based on Philippine design trends; (e) usage analytics: system tracks kiosk sessions per store, conversion rate (sessions → tile purchases), average time per session, most-viewed tile collections, and abandonment rate; data used by Category Manager to optimize tile assortment per W1 (merchandise planning & assortment review) | Sales Associate / IT Support | Department Supervisor | Daily: 5 min; Weekly: 15 min; Monthly: 30 min; Quarterly: 2–3 hrs |

### System Touchpoints
- Tile visualization software (kiosk application)
- Real-time inventory API (W533) — in-store stock + DC availability
- Product information management (W50 — PIM) — tile images, specifications, pricing
- POS quotation module (W542) — estimate generation
- Loyalty account integration (W17) — design save & recall
- Tile catalog content management system (merchandising integration)
- Customer notification engine — shared designs via email/Messenger/Viber
- Cycle counting module (W6) — kiosk inventory accuracy validation
- Store-level P&L (W67) — visualizer-assisted conversion tracking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Kiosk downtime**: Technical issues (touchscreen failure, network outage, software crash) render the service unavailable; backup tablet-based visualizer should be available; IT support response SLA per W48 (helpdesk)
- **Tile color accuracy on screen**: Screen rendering may not perfectly match physical tile color due to monitor calibration; Associates must remind customers that "the actual tile may vary slightly from the screen representation" and encourage viewing physical samples per W999 (tile sample loan program)
- **Customer queue during peak**: On busy weekends, the single kiosk per store may create wait times; peak-period queue management protocol needed (time limit per session, or redirect to mobile app visualizer)
- **Content synchronization lag**: New tiles or price changes in ERP must sync to kiosk within the same day; stale content leads to customer frustration when arriving at POS with outdated pricing


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "enters room dimensions" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-collect of "collection, color, material, size, price range, and SRP per piece/sqm" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "accounts, applies contract pricing per W163" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Tiles & Flooring Section) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Tiles Section)**: Kiosk assistance adds 5–10 minutes per customer session; at ~30–50 sessions per store per month, this represents ~3–8 hours/month — absorbed by existing Tiles Department staff; Associates benefit from the visualizer as a sales tool that accelerates customer decision-making
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Room setup: 3–5 min
- Tile selection & visualization: 5–10 min
- Companion calculation: 1–2 min (automated)
- Estimate & sharing: 2–3 min
- Kiosk maintenance: 5 min/day + 15 min/week
- **Total per customer session**: 11–20 min active staff time (customer may spend 15–30 min total including self-guided browsing)

## W1090. Customer HVAC Ductwork Design & Complete Material Sizing Recommendation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests a complete air conditioning system design including ductwork layout for a residential or small commercial project, or the consultation from W985 (aircon BTU calculation) identifies a ducted/central system requirement |
| **Frequency** | ~800–1,500 consultations/month across all stores (ducted systems are less common than split/window units but represent high-value projects averaging PHP 80,000–300,000) |
| **Volume** | 1 system design per consultation |
| **Owner** | Electrical Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Appliance/Electrical Section), Customer |

### Background

While W985 covers room-by-room BTU/horsepower sizing for window and split-type air conditioners, it does not address ducted/central air conditioning systems which require ductwork design, air distribution planning, and a significantly more complex material list. Ducted systems are increasingly popular in the Philippine market for: (a) upscale residential homes (6+ bedrooms, 200+ sqm floor area) in Metro Manila, Cebu, and Davao subdivisions; (b) small commercial establishments (restaurants, offices, clinics, retail shops); (c)BuildRight's B2B corporate customers undertaking commercial fit-outs. A ducted system involves: (a) a concealed ducted unit (indoor unit installed in the ceiling space) connected via insulated flexible or galvanized sheet metal ductwork to ceiling diffusers in each room; (b) a condensing unit (outdoor unit) on the roof or ground level; (c) refrigerant piping (copper) between indoor and outdoor units; (d) condensate drain piping (PVC) from indoor unit to drainage; (e) electrical supply (dedicated circuit per W1064); (f) controls (thermostat, zone dampers for multi-room control). The ductwork sizing requires: (a) airflow calculation (CFM — cubic feet per minute) per room based on cooling load; (b) duct diameter calculation based on CFM and acceptable velocity (≤ 800 FPM for branch ducts, ≤ 1,200 FPM for main trunk); (c) supply air register sizing; (d) return air grille sizing; (e) insulation specification (thermal insulation for ducts in unconditioned spaces — Philippine ceiling spaces reach 45–55°C). This workflow extends W985 to cover the complete ducted system design and material list, and integrates with W1064 for the electrical panel requirements.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Assessment & Room-by-Room Load Calculation**: (a) Sales Associate gathers project details: property type (residential or commercial), total floor area, number of rooms/zones, ceiling type (drop ceiling/T-bar with space for ducts, or concrete slab — slab limits ductwork options), outdoor unit placement options (roof access, ground-level pad, balcony), and whether the customer wants a single-zone or multi-zone system; (b) for each room/zone: room dimensions, window area and orientation (west-facing rooms have higher solar gain in the Philippines), insulation level (uninsulated Philippine hollow block walls vs. insulated), occupancy, and heat-generating equipment (computers, kitchen appliances, lighting); (c) system performs ASHRAE-based cooling load calculation per room: total load (BTU/hr) = sensible load + latent load; (d) system selects ducted indoor unit capacity: matches total cooling load across all zones; available capacities: 18,000 BTU (1.5 HP), 24,000 BTU (2 HP), 36,000 BTU (3 HP), 48,000 BTU (4 HP), 60,000 BTU (5 HP); (e) system selects condensing unit to match | Sales Associate / System | Electrical Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 2 | **Ductwork Layout & Sizing Design**: System designs the air distribution system: (a) for each room/zone: calculates required airflow (CFM) = room cooling load (BTU/hr) ÷ (1.085 × temperature difference, typically 20°F for Philippine conditions); (b) main trunk duct size: total CFM ÷ max velocity (1,200 FPM) = duct area (sq ft); select standard rectangular duct size (e.g., 20"×8", 24"×10") or round flex duct diameter; (c) branch duct to each room: room CFM ÷ max velocity (800 FPM) = branch duct area; select flex duct diameter (6", 8", 10", or 12"); (d) supply air diffuser/register per room: size based on CFM at ≤ 300 FPM face velocity (quiet operation); select from BuildRight inventory: 4-way ceiling diffuser, 2-way supply register, or linear slot diffuser; (e) return air grille: total system CFM ÷ 400 FPM = return grille area; position in central hallway or above ceiling; (f) duct insulation: for ducts in ceiling space (hot zone), specify 25mm or 50mm thick foil-faced fiberglass or elastomeric insulation (available from BuildRight per W1057 insulation calculator); (g) system generates a simple duct layout schematic showing trunk, branches, and terminal locations | System | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Complete Material List & Refrigerant Piping**: System generates full BOM: (a) Ducted indoor unit (capacity matched per step 1); (b) Condensing unit (outdoor unit, matched capacity); (c) Refrigerant piping: copper tubing — liquid line (¼" or ⅜") + suction line (½", ⅝", or ¾") based on distance between indoor and outdoor units; pre-flared connections or flare tool for on-site flaring; (d) Insulation for refrigerant piping (closed-cell elastomeric, ¾" wall thickness); (e) Flexible insulated ductwork: main trunk length + branch lengths per layout design; (f) Duct fittings: elbows, Y-branches, reducers, take-offs; (g) Ceiling diffusers/registers (per room); (h) Return air grille + filter; (i) Condensate drain: PVC pipe (½" or ¾") from indoor unit drain pan to nearest drain point + PVC insulation (to prevent condensation dripping); (j) Drain pump (if indoor unit is below the drain point); (k) Electrical: dedicated circuit breaker per W1064, electrical cable (THHN wire in EMT conduit), disconnect switch at outdoor unit; (l) Control: wall-mounted thermostat (manual or smart WiFi thermostat), zone dampers (if multi-zone); (m) Mounting hardware: indoor unit ceiling mounting frame, outdoor unit wall bracket or roof curb, vibration isolators, rubber pads; (n) Accessories: refrigerant gas (R32 or R410A, per unit specification), vacuum pump rental (per W139 tool rental), manifold gauge set; (o) installation materials: sealant, duct tape (foil tape), mechanical fasteners, copper joint compound | System | Sales Associate | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate, Installation Advisory & Professional Referral**: (a) system generates itemized estimate with duct schematic and complete BOM; (b) total estimated cost ranges: PHP 80,000–150,000 for a 2 HP ducted system (residential, 2–3 rooms), PHP 150,000–300,000 for a 5 HP ducted system (large residential or small commercial, 4–6 rooms); (c) installation advisory: "Ducted air conditioning system installation requires: (1) Licensed Mechanical Engineer for ductwork design certification (required by LGU building permit office for commercial installations); (2) TESDA-certified (Refrigeration and Air-Conditioning NC II) technician for refrigerant handling; (3) Licensed electrical engineer for electrical connection per Philippine Electrical Code. This material list is for estimation only. BuildRight Depot recommends engaging a qualified HVAC contractor for installation."; (d) system offers referral to HVAC installation partners per W904 (contractor referral); (e) for trade/B2B customers: estimate converts to project quote per W162 with staged delivery scheduling per W164 | Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- HVAC load calculation engine (ASHRAE-based, integrated with W985)
- Ductwork sizing calculator
- Product attribute master (W298) — duct specifications, diffuser specifications, copper tubing specs
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- POS quotation module (W542)
- Contractor referral service module (W904)
- Loyalty account integration (W17)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Design complexity**: Ducted system design is significantly more complex than split/window unit selection; system must prevent Associates from recommending undersized ductwork that causes poor airflow, noise, or system inefficiency
- **Ceiling space constraints**: Many Filipino homes have limited ceiling space (200–300mm between slab and drop ceiling); duct routing must account for structural beams, electrical conduits, and plumbing pipes already in the ceiling space
- **Refrigerant handling regulation**: refrigerant handling requires TESDA-certified (RAC NC II) technicians; BuildRight Associates must not advise customers to handle refrigerant themselves
- **Installation quality**: Poor ductwork installation (leaky joints, uninsulated sections, incorrect slope on condensate drain) causes performance issues that reflect poorly on BuildRight despite not being BuildRight's installation


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-match of "matches total cooling load across all zones" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates required airflow" (Step 2, already system-executed).
- System auto-generate of "generates full BOM" (Step 3, already system-executed).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor review and approval gate on ducted-system design consultations

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Electrical/Appliance Section)**: Ducted system design consultation adds 17–27 minutes per customer; at ~4–7.5 consultations per store per month (800–1,500 ÷ 200 stores), this represents ~1.5–4 hours/month — absorbed by existing staff with specialized training
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project assessment & load calculation: 8–12 min
- Ductwork design: 3–5 min (automated)
- Complete material list: 3–5 min (automated)
- Estimate & advisory: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 17–27 min

## W1093. Customer Interior Paint Color Scheme Consultation & Material Board Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer visits the paint section and requests help selecting a color scheme for a room or entire house, or Sales Associate identifies that the customer is planning a multi-room painting project during the initial paint mixing consultation per W168 |
| **Frequency** | ~5,000–8,000 consultations/month across all stores (paint & finishes represent 8% of SKUs, ~2,800 items; interior painting is one of the most common home improvement projects in the Philippines) |
| **Volume** | 1 color scheme consultation per customer visit |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Paint Section) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Paint Section), Customer |

### Background

Interior paint color selection goes far beyond choosing a single color. Filipino homeowners planning a painting project — whether a single room refresh or a full house repaint — face a series of design decisions: (a) color scheme coordination across multiple rooms (complementary vs. monochromatic vs. accent wall approach); (b) finish selection (gloss, semi-gloss, satin, eggshell, matte) which varies by room function (kitchen and bathroom require washable semi-gloss, bedrooms benefit from matte); (c) surface preparation assessment (new drywall, previously painted walls, stained walls, concrete vs. drywall — each requires different primer); (d) quantity estimation accounting for the number of coats (typically 2–3 coats for interior paint in the Philippines); (e) companion product selection (primer, putty/filler, sandpaper, painter's tape, rollers, brushes, drop cloths). BuildRight's paint section carries multiple brands (Boysen, Davies, Nippon Paint, Master, Superior, and potentially private label per W129) with each brand offering 1,000–3,000+ color options via tinting machines per W168 (custom paint mixing). The color scheme consultation service differentiates BuildRight from smaller hardware stores that sell paint without design guidance. This workflow is distinct from W168 (custom paint mixing) which covers the technical mixing process; this workflow addresses the design consultation that precedes color selection. It integrates with W981 (paint color matching from sample) for customers who want to match existing colors.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Scope & Room Assessment**: Sales Associate gathers painting project details: (a) project scope: single room, multiple rooms, full house interior, or full house interior + exterior; (b) for each room: room name (living room, bedroom, kitchen, bathroom, hallway), room dimensions (floor area in sqm, wall height — standard Philippine ceiling height: 2.4–2.7m), condition of existing paint (new surface, previously painted in good condition, previously painted with stains/damage); (c) customer's style preference: modern minimalist (neutrals, whites, grays), Scandinavian (light pastels, whites), tropical Filipino (warm earth tones, muted greens and blues), industrial (dark grays, charcoals), or traditional (cream, beige, warm whites); (d) existing fixed elements that constrain color choice: floor tile color, furniture color, kitchen cabinet color, window frames; (e) budget range for paint materials | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Color Scheme Design & Visualization**: (a) Sales Associate uses paint brand color deck/fan deck to present curated color palettes matching customer's style preference: 3–5 palette options, each with a primary wall color, secondary/accent wall color, ceiling white, and trim color; (b) system provides room visualization via tablet: apply selected palette to a room template showing wall color, accent wall, ceiling, and trim; (c) for each palette, Associate explains: (i) primary color coverage (all walls except accent wall), (ii) accent wall application (typically one wall — the wall behind the headboard in bedrooms, or the TV wall in living rooms), (iii) ceiling treatment (standard ceiling white or tinted ceiling for dramatic effect), (iv) trim and door color (white, matching wall color, or contrasting); (d) customer selects preferred palette; (e) for multi-room projects: Associate ensures color flow between rooms — adjacent rooms should share at least one color element for visual cohesion; system offers "whole house" color journey recommendations (e.g., progressing from light neutrals in entry/living areas to deeper tones in private spaces) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 3 | **Surface Preparation Assessment & Primer Selection**: System evaluates surface preparation needs based on customer's wall condition: (a) **New concrete/plaster walls** (common in new Philippine construction): require alkali-resistant primer to prevent lime/salt efflorescence — specify 1 coat of Boysen Permacoat Primer, Davies Alkali Resisting Primer, or equivalent; (b) **Previously painted walls (good condition)**: spot-putty any nail holes or cracks, light sanding, no primer needed if existing paint is sound; (c) **Previously painted walls (stained or damaged)**: stain-blocking primer (water stains from leaks — common in Philippine homes due to rainy season, smoke stains from kitchen, crayon/marker from children); (d) **Chalking old paint**: bind chalking surface with oil-based or water-based primer-sealer before repainting; (e) **Concrete patches/putty**: wall putty ( Boysen Wall Putty or equivalent) for smoothing rough areas, applied after primer, sanded smooth before topcoat; (f) system calculates putty quantity based on estimated wall area needing repair; (g) for each surface type, system adds appropriate primer and preparation materials to the BOM | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 4 | **Complete Material List & Quantity Calculation**: System generates full paint BOM: (a) for each room: (i) wall area = perimeter × ceiling height − door area (1.6 sqm per door) − window area; (ii) paint quantity = wall area ÷ coverage per liter (typically 10–12 sqm per liter per coat for interior latex) × number of coats (2 coats standard, 3 coats for dramatic color change); (iii) ceiling paint = ceiling area ÷ coverage × 2 coats; (iv) trim paint = linear meters of trim ÷ coverage per liter × 2 coats; (b) paint grouped by color across all rooms (same color in multiple rooms combined into single line item); (c) for each paint product: system selects the appropriate finish: matte or satin for living room/bedroom walls, semi-gloss or satin for kitchen/bathroom walls, gloss or semi-gloss for trim/doors; (d) companion materials: primer (type and quantity per step 3), wall putty (kg), sandpaper (sheets by grit: 80 for rough, 120 for smooth, 220 for finish), painter's tape (width: 1" or 2", number of rolls), paint rollers (9" for walls, 4" for trim, foam roller for smooth finish), paint brushes (2" for cutting in, 4" for broad areas), roller tray and refill, drop cloth/plastic sheeting (floor protection), roller extension pole (for high ceilings); (e) system calculates total estimated cost and estimated project duration (DIY: room painting takes ~1 day per coat per room; professional: 1–2 days per room) | System | Sales Associate | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 5 | **Material Board Assembly & Estimate Generation**: (a) Sales Associate assembles a physical "material board" for the customer: paint color swatches (from brand fan deck or painted sample chips per W903 sample loan program), selected primer chip, and a printed summary of the color scheme; (b) system generates PDF estimate with: color palette visual, room-by-room paint assignments, complete material list with quantities and prices, total estimated cost, and estimated project duration; (c) system links the color selections to the customer's loyalty account for future reorder: "When you need to repaint or touch up, your exact colors are saved in your BuildRight account."; (d) for customers who want to proceed immediately: Associate processes the paint mixing order per W168 — each color mixed by the tinting machine per the brand's color code; (e) for customers who want to test colors first: system offers sample-size paint cans (250ml or 500ml trial sizes, available from major brands) for at-home testing before committing to full quantities; (f) advisory: "Paint colors may appear different on your walls due to lighting conditions. BuildRight recommends testing a 1m × 1m area on your wall and viewing it at different times of day before committing to the full project."; (g) system offers referral to professional painter partners per W904 for customers who prefer not to DIY | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Paint color database & palette library (per brand, integrated with W168 tinting system)
- Room visualization tool (tablet-based)
- Product attribute master (W298) — paint coverage rates, finish specifications
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- Paint mixing system integration (W168)
- POS quotation module (W542)
- Loyalty account integration (W17) — color save & reorder
- Contractor referral service module (W904)
- Sample loan program integration (W903)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Color perception variation**: Paint colors look different under store lighting (fluorescent/LED) vs. home lighting (warm LED, natural daylight); sample testing is critical to manage expectations
- **Brand-specific color codes**: Colors are not interchangeable between brands (Boysen "Cloud White" ≠ Nippon "Cloud White"); system must lock color codes to the specific brand's mixing formula
- **Multi-coat cost shock**: Customers often underestimate the quantity (and cost) of multi-coat applications; system must clearly show cost per coat to set expectations
- **Surface preparation cost**: Primer, putty, and sandpaper can add 20–30% to the total paint bill; customers may skip preparation to save cost, leading to poor results that reflect poorly on BuildRight's paint quality


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-scan of "Project Scope & Room Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-match of "matching customer's style preference" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates putty quantity based on estimated wall area needing repair" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must lock color codes to the specific brand's mixing formula; operational: must clearly show cost per coat to set expectations

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Paint Section)**: Color consultation adds 15–25 minutes per customer; at ~25–40 consultations per store per month, this represents ~7–15 hours/month — absorbed by existing Paint Department staff (the consultation drives higher paint + companion product basket size)
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project scope & assessment: 5–8 min
- Color scheme design: 8–12 min
- Surface preparation assessment: 3–5 min
- Material list calculation: 2–3 min (automated)
- Material board & estimate: 5–8 min
- **Total per consultation**: 23–36 min

## W1095. Store-Level Customer Electrical Residential Wiring Diagram & Material List Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests a complete residential electrical wiring layout and material list for a new home construction or major renovation project, extending from the panel board design in W1064 |
| **Frequency** | ~2,500–4,000 consultations/month across all stores (electrical is 10% of SKUs, ~3,500 items; new residential construction and renovation are core customer projects) |
| **Volume** | 1 wiring diagram and material list per consultation |
| **Owner** | Electrical Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Electrical Section), Customer |

### Background

While W1064 covers panel board and circuit breaker sizing, and W982 covers electrical load calculation and wire sizing, there is no existing workflow that produces a complete residential wiring diagram showing the routing of all circuits from the panel board to every outlet, switch, light fixture, and appliance connection point. The typical Filipino residential electrical project involves: (a) service entrance from the utility pole (MERALCO, Davao Light, or electric cooperative) to the meter base to the main panel; (b) branch circuits from the panel to every point of use throughout the house; (c) each circuit consisting of: wire (THHN in EMT conduit, or NM-B cable where permitted), junction boxes, switches, outlets, light fixtures, and connections. The Philippine Electrical Code (PEC) 2017 mandates specific requirements: minimum wire sizes per circuit, maximum number of outlets per circuit, GFCI protection for wet areas, proper grounding, and separate circuits for major appliances. A complete wiring diagram with a matching material list helps the customer: (a) understand the full scope of electrical materials needed (and their cost); (b) provide their electrician or electrical engineer with a clear materials specification; (c) purchase the correct quantities of wire, conduit, boxes, switches, and outlets in a single trip; (d) avoid the common problem of under-purchasing wire ( Filipino electricians often request additional rolls mid-project, causing delays). This workflow integrates with W1064 (panel design) and W982 (load calculation) to produce a comprehensive electrical material package.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Floor Plan Input & Room Specification**: (a) Sales Associate enters the customer's floor plan into the system: number of floors, room layout (living room, dining room, kitchen, bedrooms, bathrooms, laundry area, garage/carport, outdoor area); (b) for each room, Associate specifies: room name, dimensions, and electrical point requirements: (i) ceiling light points (quantity, type: flush mount, pendant, LED panel); (ii) wall light points (quantity, if any); (iii) switch locations (quantity, type: 1-gang, 2-gang, 3-gang, position: inside or outside room); (iv) convenience outlets (quantity, type: grounded duplex, USB combo, weatherproof for outdoor); (v) special outlets (air conditioner — wall-mounted at 1.8m, electric stove — floor-level, water heater — wall-mounted, washing machine — floor-level); (vi) exhaust fan points (bathroom, kitchen); (vii) doorbell point (main entrance); (c) for outdoor: perimeter light points, gate light, garage light, outdoor outlets (GFCI-protected) | Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 2 | **Automatic Circuit Allocation & Wiring Diagram Generation**: System performs PEC-compliant circuit allocation: (a) lighting circuits: allocate points to 15A circuits with max 10 light points per circuit per PEC; separate lighting circuits per floor; (b) convenience outlet circuits: allocate to 20A circuits with max 6 outlets per circuit per PEC; separate kitchen and bathroom outlet circuits; (c) dedicated appliance circuits per W1064 (aircon, water heater, electric stove, pump); (d) system generates single-line wiring diagram: shows panel board at top, circuits branching down to room-level junction boxes, then to individual points; (e) wire routing: system estimates wire lengths based on room layout: (i) home run length from panel to each room's first junction box (estimated from floor plan dimensions); (ii) branch lengths within each room between points; (iii) standard factor: total wire per circuit = measured length × 1.3 (30% additional for routing around obstacles, drops, and connections); (f) system selects wire size per circuit per W982: 14 AWG (2.0 sqmm) for 15A lighting circuits, 12 AWG (3.5 sqmm) for 20A outlet circuits, 10 AWG (5.5 sqmm) for 30A appliance circuits, 8 AWG (8.0 sqmm) for 40A circuits, 6 AWG (14 sqmm) for 50–60A circuits | System | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Complete Electrical Material List**: System generates comprehensive BOM: (a) **Wire/Cable**: per circuit — wire size, number of conductors (2-wire with ground for 1-phase circuits, or 3-wire with ground for multi-wire branch circuits), length per circuit in meters (rounded up to standard roll lengths: 100m rolls for 14/12 AWG, 50m rolls for 10/8/6 AWG); (b) **Conduit**: EMT (electrical metallic tubing) or PVC conduit per circuit — diameter (½" for 2 wires, ¾" for 3–4 wires, 1" for 5+ wires per PEC fill ratio), length per circuit; conduit fittings: connectors, couplings, elbows, straps; (c) **Boxes**: junction boxes (4" octagonal for ceiling points, 4" square for switch/outlet combinations, handy boxes for exposed wiring), switch boxes (gang count matching switch type), outlet boxes, ceiling rose/canopy for light fixtures; (d) **Switches**: per room specification — 1-gang, 2-gang, 3-gang toggle or rocker switches, 1-way and 2-way (for hallways and stairs), dimmer switches (optional, for living room/bedroom); (e) **Outlets**: grounded duplex convenience outlets (per room count), USB combo outlets (optional upgrade), GFCI outlets (bathroom, kitchen, outdoor, garage), weatherproof covers for outdoor outlets; (f) **Circuit breakers**: per W1064 panel schedule; (g) **Service entrance**: meter base (MERALCO/Davao Light approved), service entrance wire (size per utility requirement), grounding system (ground rod, ground wire, ground clamp); (h) **Accessories**: wire nuts/solderless connectors, electrical tape (per circuit count), cable ties, nail-in clips for conduit, drywall anchors for boxes, circuit directory labels for panel; (i) **Installation tools**: wire stripper, conduit cutter, fish tape, voltage tester, screwdrivers (flat and Phillips), drill with masonry bit for concrete walls | System | Sales Associate | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate, Safety Advisory & Professional Referral**: (a) system generates itemized estimate with: single-line wiring diagram, room-by-room circuit allocation table, complete BOM with quantities and prices, total estimated cost; (b) system calculates estimated cost breakdown: wire and cable (~40% of electrical material cost), conduit and fittings (~15%), switches and outlets (~15%), boxes (~10%), circuit breakers and panel (~15%), accessories (~5%); (c) for trade account customers: applies contract pricing per W163 and converts to project quote per W162; (d) safety advisory: "All electrical installations in the Philippines must comply with the Philippine Electrical Code (PEC) 2017 and must be performed by a licensed Professional Electrical Engineer (PEE) or Registered Master Electrician (RME) under the supervision of a PEE. This wiring diagram and material list is for quantity estimation only and does not constitute a professional electrical design. A licensed PEE must review and approve the electrical plan before construction. BuildRight Depot requires customers to engage a qualified electrical professional for installation."; (e) system offers referral to licensed electrical engineers and electricians per W904; (f) estimate and diagram saved to customer loyalty account for future reference | Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Floor plan input module (simple grid-based or upload PDF/image)
- PEC 2017 circuit allocation engine
- Single-line wiring diagram generator
- Wire length estimation engine (room layout-based)
- Product attribute master (W298) — wire specifications, conduit fill ratios, box specifications
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- POS quotation module (W542)
- Loyalty account integration (W17)
- Contractor referral service module (W904)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **PEC compliance liability**: BuildRight's wiring diagram must not be represented as a professional electrical design; the disclaimer must be prominent and the customer must understand that a licensed PEE must produce or approve the actual electrical plan for building permit purposes
- **Wire length estimation accuracy**: Wire length estimates from a simple floor plan input will differ from actual site conditions (wall chase routing, detours around structural elements, vertical drops); the 1.3× factor provides a buffer but some customers may still come up short
- **Conduit fill ratio violations**: Overfilling conduit (too many wires in too-small conduit) violates PEC and creates fire risk; the system must calculate and enforce PEC conduit fill ratios
- **Grounding compliance**: Philippine electrical installations require proper grounding (earth electrode at the service entrance); some older Filipino homes lack grounding entirely; the material list must include grounding materials and the advisory must emphasize grounding importance


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "enters the customer's floor plan into the system" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-generate of "generates single-line wiring diagram" (Step 2, already system-executed).
- System auto-count of "gang count matching switch type" (Step 3, already system-executed).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must calculate and enforce PEC conduit fill ratios

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Electrical Section)**: Wiring diagram consultation adds 15–25 minutes per customer; at ~12–20 consultations per store per month, this represents ~3–8 hours/month — absorbed by existing Electrical Department staff
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Floor plan input: 8–12 min
- Circuit allocation & diagram: 3–5 min (automated)
- Material list: 2–3 min (automated)
- Estimate & advisory: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 16–25 min
