## W1096. Customer Solar Water Heater System Sizing & Complete Package Recommendation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer inquires about solar water heating options, or Sales Associate identifies solar water heater applicability during a water heater consultation or solar panel consultation per W990 |
| **Frequency** | ~600–1,200 consultations/month across all stores (growing segment driven by Philippine electricity costs and DOE renewable energy promotion) |
| **Volume** | 1 system design per consultation |
| **Owner** | Electrical Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Electrical/Appliance Section), Customer |

### Background

Solar water heating (SWH) is a mature technology well-suited to the Philippines' tropical climate with abundant sunshine (average 5–6 peak sun hours per day year-round). The Philippine Department of Energy (DOE) actively promotes solar water heating through the Renewable Energy Act (RA 9513) and the Philippine Energy Efficiency and Conservation Act (RA 11285). For BuildRight customers, solar water heaters offer: (a) electricity savings of PHP 2,000–5,000/month compared to electric water heaters (Philippine electricity rates are among the highest in Southeast Asia at PHP 9–12/kWh); (b) hot water availability even during power outages (passive thermosiphon systems require no electricity); (c) environmental benefits (reducing carbon footprint per W192). Two main SWH technologies are available: (a) **Evacuated tube collectors (ETC)**: more efficient in cloudy conditions, lighter weight, higher operating temperatures, but more fragile; (b) **Flat plate collectors (FPC)**: more durable, lower cost, good for Philippine sunny conditions. Systems can be: (i) **passive (thermosiphon)**: water circulates naturally by convection — no pump, no electricity needed, tank must be mounted above collector; (ii) **active (pumped)**: uses a circulation pump and controller — more flexible installation (tank can be at ground level) but requires electricity for the pump. This workflow is distinct from W990 (solar panel system) which covers photovoltaic (PV) solar for electricity generation; this workflow addresses solar thermal for water heating.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Hot Water Demand Assessment**: Sales Associate gathers hot water usage information: (a) household size (number of persons); (b) bathrooms with hot water: count and type (shower only, shower + bathtub); (c) kitchen hot water: yes/no (sink hot water tap); (d) laundry hot water: yes/no (washing machine hot water connection); (e) hot water usage pattern: morning peak (bathing), evening peak (bathing + kitchen), or both; (f) current water heating method: electric storage, electric tankless, LPG, or none; (g) desired water temperature (comfortable shower: 38–42°C); (h) cold water inlet temperature: Philippine municipal water ~25–28°C, deep well ~26–29°C; (i) roof type and orientation: flat concrete roof or pitched roof (G.I. sheet or tile), orientation (south-facing optimal in Philippines), available roof area for collector; (j) roof structural capacity: solar water heater + water tank = 150–300 kg — Filipino concrete flat roofs can typically support this, but lightweight G.I. sheet roofs may require structural reinforcement | Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **System Sizing & Technology Selection**: System performs sizing calculation: (a) daily hot water demand: standard Philippine household usage = 40–50 liters per person per day at 50°C delivery temperature; total = household size × 50 liters; (b) collector area: Philippine solar radiation ≈ 5 kWh/m²/day; collector area = daily hot water demand (liters) × temperature rise (°C) ÷ (solar radiation × collector efficiency × conversion factor); typical result: 1 sqm collector per 50–70 liters of daily hot water demand; (c) tank size = daily hot water demand × 1.2 (20% buffer for cloudy day storage); (d) system recommendations based on household size: (i) 2–3 persons: 150L tank + 1.5–2 sqm collector (20-tube ETC or 1.5 sqm FPC); (ii) 4–5 persons: 250L tank + 3–4 sqm collector (30-tube ETC or 2 × 2 sqm FPC); (iii) 6–8 persons: 300L tank + 4–5 sqm collector (2 × 20-tube ETC or 2 × 2.5 sqm FPC); (e) technology selection: ETC recommended for households in cloudy areas (mountainous regions, typhoon-prone areas), FPC recommended for most Philippine lowland locations (more durable, lower maintenance); (f) passive vs. active: passive (thermosiphon) recommended for single-story homes with flat concrete roof (tank + collector on roof); active (pumped) recommended for multi-story homes or roofs that cannot support tank weight (tank at ground level, collector on roof) | System | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Complete System Package & Companion Materials**: System generates full BOM: (a) solar collector: ETC (specify tube count, diameter, length) or FPC (specify dimensions, absorber coating, glazing type); (b) hot water storage tank: capacity, stainless steel or enamel-lined, insulation thickness (≥50mm polyurethane foam), integrated or separate from collector; (c) mounting frame: stainless steel or aluminum, adjustable tilt angle (optimal tilt for Philippines: 10–15° for year-round performance, adjustable for seasonal optimization); (d) circulation pump (active system only): rated for hot water, 40–60W, with controller; (e) piping: insulated hot water pipe (PPR or copper, minimum 19mm diameter, with 19mm foam insulation per W1057); (f) cold water supply connection: PPR pipe from existing cold water line to tank inlet; (g) hot water delivery connection: PPR or copper pipe from tank outlet to bathrooms and kitchen; (h) temperature & pressure relief valve (safety device per PEC/plumbing code); (i) anti-scale filter (for areas with hard water — some Philippine water supplies have high mineral content); (j) backup electric heating element: most solar water heater tanks include a 1.5–2kW electric backup element for cloudy/rainy days when solar is insufficient; (k) accessories: pipe insulation, pipe fittings, valves (gate valve, check valve), mounting hardware (bolts, anchors for concrete roof, flashing for pitched roof), sealant, and thermostatic mixing valve (prevents scalding at taps) | System | Sales Associate | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 4 | **ROI Calculation, Estimate & Installation Advisory**: (a) system calculates financial return: (i) current electric water heater energy cost: liters/day × temperature rise × specific heat of water ÷ electric heater efficiency × PHP/kWh rate = monthly cost; (ii) solar water heater energy cost: PHP 0 (sunlight is free) + backup electric heating on cloudy days (~20% of total energy) = monthly cost; (iii) monthly savings = (i) − (ii); (iv) payback period = system cost ÷ monthly savings; typical result: PHP 25,000–45,000 system cost ÷ PHP 2,000–4,000/month savings = 6–18 month payback; (b) system generates estimate with: system specification, complete BOM, total cost, ROI analysis, estimated installation time (1–2 days for passive system, 2–3 days for active system); (c) installation advisory: "Solar water heater installation involves both plumbing and structural work. Tank and collector mounting on the roof requires proper waterproofing to prevent leaks. BuildRight Depot recommends professional installation by a qualified plumber and, for active systems, a licensed electrician for the pump/controller wiring."; (d) system offers referral to accredited installation partners per W904; (e) for customers interested in larger renewable energy systems: Associate mentions W990 (solar PV system) for electricity generation | Sales Associate | Electrical Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Solar water heater sizing calculator
- ROI calculation engine
- Product attribute master (W298) — collector specifications, tank specifications
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- POS quotation module (W542)
- Loyalty account integration (W17)
- Contractor referral service module (W904)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Roof structural concerns**: A 250L tank + collector weighs ~300 kg; Filipino concrete flat roofs (typically 150mm CHB slab) can support this, but older homes or lightweight G.I. sheet roofs cannot; system must flag structural assessment requirement
- **Water quality impact**: Hard water (high calcium/magnesium content, common in some Philippine groundwater sources) causes scale buildup in evacuated tubes and tank heat exchangers, reducing efficiency over time; anti-scale filter must be recommended for affected areas
- **Typhoon vulnerability**: Solar collectors mounted on roofs are vulnerable to typhoon winds (Philippines experiences ~20 typhoons per year); mounting frame must be designed for wind speeds per NSCP (National Structural Code of the Philippines) and customers must be advised to check mounting bolts annually
- **Consumer awareness**: Solar water heating is less well-known than solar PV in the Philippines; Sales Associates need training to explain the technology and benefits clearly


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "count and type" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "System Sizing & Technology Selection" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-validate of "Complete System Package & Companion Materials" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must flag structural assessment requirement

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Electrical/Appliance Section)**: SWH consultation adds 15–20 minutes per customer; at ~3–6 consultations per store per month, this represents ~1–2 hours/month — absorbed by existing staff
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Hot water demand assessment: 5–8 min
- System sizing: 3–5 min (automated)
- Material list: 2–3 min (automated)
- ROI & estimate: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 13–21 min

## W1097. Store-Level Customer Outdoor Deck, Patio & Pergola Material Estimation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests material estimation for an outdoor deck, patio, or pergola project, or Sales Associate identifies the project type during a lumber or garden section consultation |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,500 consultations/month across all stores (outdoor living is a growing segment in the Philippine market, particularly in upscale subdivisions and resort areas) |
| **Volume** | 1 structure estimation per consultation |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Lumber Section), Customer |

### Background

Outdoor decks, patios, and pergolas are increasingly popular in Philippine residential construction, driven by: (a) the Filipino "lanai" and outdoor living culture (entertaining, family gatherings, "inihaw" grilling); (b) the growth of residential subdivisions in Metro Manila, Cebu, and Davao with larger lots that include outdoor spaces; (c) the resort and hospitality sector (hotel and resort construction is a strong segment for BuildRight's B2B customers); (d) social media influence (Pinterest, Instagram, and local home improvement pages featuring outdoor living spaces). The materials for these structures are primarily from BuildRight's lumber and building materials category (14% of SKUs, ~4,900 items): (a) **decking**: pressure-treated lumber (tanguile, yakal, or imported treated pine), composite decking (wood-plastic composite — growing in popularity despite higher cost due to low maintenance), or concrete decking tiles; (b) **structural framing**: treated lumber joists and beams, or steel framing (galvanized C-purlins for modern deck construction); (c) **pergola posts and beams**: hardwood (yakal, guijo — native Philippine hardwoods) or steel posts with wood cladding; (d) **pergola roofing**: polycarbonate sheets (for shade with natural light), G.I. corrugated sheets, or shade sail/canvas; (e) **fasteners**: deck screws (stainless steel for coastal areas, galvanized for inland), post anchors, joist hangers, beam brackets; (f) **finishing**: wood stain, sealant, or exterior paint per W1093; (g) **lighting**: outdoor LED per W1084 (security/CCTV adapted for outdoor accent lighting). This workflow provides a comprehensive material estimation for the structure, integrating with W976 (lumber grade selection) and W169 (lumber cutting services) for the lumber-specific aspects.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Structure Design & Dimension Input**: Sales Associate gathers project specifications: (a) structure type: deck only, pergola only, deck + pergola, patio (concrete slab); (b) deck dimensions: length × width × height above ground (ground-level: 0–0.3m, raised: 0.3–1.0m, elevated: 1.0m+); (c) deck shape: rectangular, L-shaped, or multi-level; (d) deck surface material preference: natural wood (specify: tanguile, yakal, or treated pine), composite decking (WPC), or concrete tile; (e) pergola dimensions: length × width × post height (standard: 2.4m post height for 2.1m clearance); (f) pergola roof type: open-rafter (decorative only, no rain protection), polycarbonate roof (rain protection + natural light), G.I. sheet roof (full rain protection), or retractable canvas/shade sail; (g) site conditions: ground type (concrete pad, compacted soil, or grass), drainage consideration (slope away from house), attachment to existing structure (ledger board against house wall or freestanding); (h) load requirements: residential light-duty (furniture + people, ~200 kg/sqm) or heavy-duty (hot tub, outdoor kitchen, ~400 kg/sqm) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |
| 2 | **Structural Design & Material Calculation**: System designs the structure and calculates materials: **For Deck**: (a) footing: concrete footing at each post location — footing diameter × depth based on deck height and soil type (Philippine standard: 0.3m diameter × 0.6m depth for ground-level, 0.4m × 0.8m for raised); footing count = post grid spacing (typically 1.5–2.0m centers); (b) posts: 4×4 (100mm × 100mm) or 6×6 (150mm × 150mm) treated lumber or steel — quantity = footing count, length = deck height + footing projection + beam clearance; (c) beams: double 2×8 or 2×10 (50mm × 200mm or 50mm × 250mm) per span table — spans ≤ 3.0m for 2×8, ≤ 4.0m for 2×10; (d) joists: 2×6 or 2×8 at 400mm or 600mm spacing — quantity = deck width ÷ joist spacing + 1, length = deck depth; (e) decking boards: quantity = deck area ÷ board coverage per piece × 1.10 (10% wastage for cutting); (f) fasteners: deck screws (stainless steel 304 for coastal, 316 for saltwater proximity), joist hangers, post anchors, beam brackets, carriage bolts for post-to-beam connections. **For Pergola**: (g) posts: 4×4 or 6×6 — 4 posts minimum (2 per side), length = post height + footing embedment; (h) beams: double 2×8 spanning across posts; (i) rafters: 2×4 or 2×6 spanning between beams at 400–600mm spacing — quantity = pergola length ÷ rafter spacing; (j) roofing: polycarbonate sheets (6mm or 10mm twin-wall, length matching rafter span × width needed) or G.I. sheets per W983 (roofing calculator); (k) post bases: galvanized steel post anchors set in concrete footings; (l) hardware: lag bolts, through bolts, angle brackets, rafter ties | System | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Companion Products & Finishing Materials**: System adds: (a) **Surface preparation**: gravel base under deck (for ground-level, to prevent weed growth and improve drainage) — volume in cubic meters; weed barrier fabric; (b) **Concrete for footings**: cement bags + sand + gravel per footing count × footing volume; concrete mix ratio per W1044; (c) **Wood treatment**: if using natural lumber (not pre-treated): wood preservative (solignum or equivalent), application brush, 2–3 coats recommended for ground-contact posts; (d) **Deck finish**: wood stain or exterior sealer for decking surface (quantity based on deck area × 2 coats); application by brush or sprayer; (e) **Waterproofing**: ledger board flashing (aluminum or galvanized) where deck meets house wall — prevents water infiltration; (f) **Outdoor lighting**: deck post solar lights, step lights (LED), pergola string lights ("fairy lights" — very popular in Philippine outdoor spaces), and under-deck LED strip lighting; per W1084 adapted for decorative outdoor lighting; (g) **Railing** (for decks > 0.6m above ground per Philippine building code): posts, top rail, bottom rail, balusters (wood, steel, or cable railing), and hardware; (h) **Stairs** (for raised decks): stringers, treads, risers, handrail per stair calculator; (i) **Tools**: circular saw per W169 (lumber cutting service available in-store), drill, level, measuring tape, post level, concrete mixer rental per W1094 | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–4 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Estimate, Lumber Advisory & Service Coordination**: (a) system generates itemized estimate with: structural diagram (simplified isometric sketch), complete BOM organized by structural component, quantities and prices, total estimated cost; (b) estimated cost ranges: ground-level wood deck (tanguile): PHP 2,500–4,000/sqm; raised wood deck: PHP 3,500–5,500/sqm; composite deck: PHP 5,000–8,000/sqm; pergola (wood, open-rafter): PHP 3,000–5,000/sqm; pergola (polycarbonate roof): PHP 4,500–7,000/sqm; (c) lumber advisory: (i) wood grade: per W976 (lumber grade selection) — Associate explains the difference between construction grade (knots allowed, for structural framing) and select grade (minimal knots, for visible decking and pergola beams); (ii) cutting service: per W169 — BuildRight can cut lumber to specified lengths, reducing customer's on-site cutting; (iii) treatment: untreated lumber must be treated before installation, especially posts in contact with ground; (iv) moisture content: lumber should be kiln-dried or air-dried to ≤ 18% moisture content to prevent warping; (d) system offers installation referral per W904 for customers who need professional deck/pergola construction; (e) estimate saved to customer loyalty account | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Deck/pergola structural calculator (span tables, material quantity formulas)
- Product attribute master (W298) — lumber dimensions, grades, composite decking specs
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- Lumber cutting service integration (W169)
- POS quotation module (W542)
- Loyalty account integration (W17)
- Contractor referral service module (W904)
- Concrete mix calculator integration (W1044)
- Roofing calculator integration (W983)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Wood quality variability**: Philippine lumber (tanguile, yakal) quality varies significantly by batch and supplier; visible defects (knots, splits, warping) may only be apparent after purchase; quality inspection at delivery per W109 is essential
- **Termite risk**: Philippine tropical climate is highly conducive to termites; all ground-contact wood must be pressure-treated or treated with solignum; failure to treat leads to structural failure within 2–5 years; mitigated by standard operating procedures and system-enforced validation rules
- **Typhoon wind resistance**: Pergolas with polycarbonate or G.I. roofs must be designed for typhoon wind loads per NSCP; under-designed pergolas become dangerous flying debris during typhoons
- **Cost sensitivity**: Many customers underestimate deck/pergola costs; the estimate must be clear and transparent, with material cost breakdown, to avoid sticker shock at POS


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-post of "Structure Design & Dimension Input" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-capture of "Structural Design & Material Calculation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "count × footing volume" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Lumber Section)**: Deck/pergola estimation adds 15–20 minutes per consultation; at ~10–18 consultations per store per month, this represents ~3–6 hours/month — absorbed by existing Lumber Department staff
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Structure design & input: 5–8 min
- Structural calculation: 3–5 min (automated)
- Companion products: 3–4 min (automated)
- Estimate & advisory: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 14–22 min

## W1098. Customer Emergency Home Repair Quick-Fix Bundle Curation & Package Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer arrives at store with an urgent home repair need (leaking pipe, broken window, electrical issue, roof leak, clogged drain, etc.), or customer calls/uses the app requesting an emergency repair material bundle |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–12,000 emergency repair interactions/month across all stores (home repair emergencies are a consistent high-frequency retail traffic driver) |
| **Volume** | 1 bundle per emergency |
| **Owner** | Customer Service Rep |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer Service Rep, Customer |

### Background

Emergency home repairs are one of the most common reasons Filipino homeowners visit a hardware store. The Philippine climate and housing stock create recurring repair needs: (a) **roof leaks** during rainy season (June–November) — G.I. sheet roofing develops rust holes, flashing deteriorates, ridge cap gaps open; (b) **plumbing leaks** — pipe corrosion (especially in older GI pipe systems), faucet washer failure, toilet valve failure; (c) **electrical issues** — tripped breakers during brownouts, faulty outlets, damaged wiring after flooding; (d) **window/door damage** — broken glass from typhoon debris, swollen wooden doors from humidity, broken hinges; (e) **clogged drains** — common in Philippine homes due to insufficient drain pipe diameter and tree root intrusion; (f) **wall cracks and leaks** — concrete hollow block walls develop cracks from settling and seismic activity (Philippines is earthquake-prone). These customers share common characteristics: (a) they are stressed and time-pressured ("I need to fix this NOW before the next rain"); (b) they may not know exactly what materials they need ("My roof is leaking — what do I buy?"); (c) they need everything in one trip (making multiple trips during an emergency is highly undesirable); (d) they often need basic tools they may not own (putty knife, pipe wrench, wire nuts). Pre-curated "Quick-Fix Bundles" for the top 10–15 emergency repair scenarios address these needs: each bundle contains all materials and tools needed for the repair, packaged together with a simple instruction card. This is distinct from W910 (product bundle assembly & pre-packaged solution kit management) which covers planned merchandise bundles; this workflow focuses on emergency repair scenarios with real-time bundle assembly. It integrates with W1093 (paint color consultation) for cosmetic repair matching.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Emergency Diagnosis & Bundle Selection**: (a) Customer Service Rep or Sales Associate quickly diagnoses the emergency: "What's the problem? Where is it? How urgent?"; (b) Associate maps the problem to a Quick-Fix Bundle: (i) **Roof Leak Bundle**: G.I. sheet patch (matching existing profile), roofing screws with rubber washers, butyl tape (for flashing repair), wire brush (for rust removal), rust converter, roofing sealant, and a set of basic hand tools if needed; (ii) **Pipe Leak Bundle**: pipe repair clamp or self-fusing silicone tape, pipe cutter (for GI pipe), replacement pipe section (GI or PPR), fittings, Teflon tape, pipe wrench, and a bucket (for residual water); (iii) **Faucet Leak Bundle**: replacement washer set (universal rubber washers), faucet cartridge (if known brand), adjustable wrench, Teflon tape, plumber's putty; (iv) **Clogged Drain Bundle**: drain snake (hand-crank, 6m), chemical drain cleaner (caustic soda or enzymatic), plunger, rubber gloves, bucket; (v) **Electrical Tripped Breaker**: circuit tester, replacement breaker (if customer knows panel type — Schneider, ABB, etc.), wire nuts, electrical tape; (vi) **Broken Window Bundle**: glass cutter (if cutting own glass), replacement glass pane (standard sizes pre-cut per W943 glass cutting service), glazing points, window putty/glazing compound, putty knife; (vii) **Wall Crack / Leak Bundle**: hydraulic cement (for active leaks), crack injection polyurethane foam, wire brush, trowel, bonding agent, and wall patch compound; (viii) **Flood Cleanup Bundle**: wet/dry shop vacuum rental per W139, squeegee, heavy-duty mop, chlorine bleach for disinfection, rubber boots, nitrile gloves, N95 mask; (ix) **Door/Hinge Repair Bundle**: replacement hinges (brass or stainless), wood screws, wood filler (for stripped screw holes), screwdriver set, door stopper; (x) **Toilet Repair Bundle**: toilet fill valve replacement kit, flush valve/flapper, toilet wax ring, supply hose, adjustable wrench, sponge (for bowl water removal) | Sales Associate / Customer Service Rep | Customer Service Rep | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Bundle Assembly & Customization**: (a) system retrieves the Quick-Fix Bundle template from product master: pre-defined SKU list per emergency type with standard quantities; (b) Associate customizes based on customer's specific situation: (i) for roof leak: "What's your roof profile? (corrugated, rib-type, tile?) What color?" — system substitutes matching G.I. sheet; (ii) for pipe leak: "What pipe material? (GI, PPR, PVC, copper?) What diameter?" — system substitutes matching pipe and fittings; (iii) for electrical: "What breaker brand? How many amps?" — system substitutes matching breaker; (c) system checks real-time inventory for all bundle items at the store per W533; if any item is out of stock, system suggests nearest alternative or offers to order for DC delivery per W19; (d) Associate adds items not in the standard bundle that the customer may need based on conversation (e.g., safety glasses for roof work, a longer pipe section than standard, additional roofing screws) | Sales Associate / System | Customer Service Rep | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Quick-Fix Instruction Card & Advisory**: (a) system prints a Quick-Fix Instruction Card specific to the emergency type: single-page, illustrated, step-by-step guide in English and Filipino/Tagalog; (b) example — Roof Leak Quick-Fix Card: "Step 1: Safety first — do not climb on the roof during rain. Wait for dry weather. Step 2: Locate the leak source from inside the attic or ceiling. Step 3: Wire-brush the rust/corrosion area. Step 4: Apply rust converter and wait 30 minutes. Step 5: Cut butyl tape to cover the hole plus 5cm overlap on all sides. Step 6: Press firmly and smooth from center outward. Step 7: If replacing a G.I. sheet section, remove screws, position new sheet with 1.5 corrugation overlap, secure with roofing screws. Step 8: Apply sealant to screw heads and overlap edges. WARNING: For roofs higher than 3 meters, BuildRight recommends hiring a professional roofer. Safety harness required."; (c) safety warnings printed in bold on every card: appropriate PPE, fall hazard warnings for roof/height work, electrical shock warnings for electrical repairs ("Turn off the main breaker before touching any wiring"), and chemical hazard warnings for drain cleaner and solvents; (d) system prints "When to Call a Professional" guidance: conditions where the repair exceeds DIY capability (e.g., "If the pipe leak is at a joint inside the wall, call a licensed plumber. Do not break open walls unless you can identify the exact leak location.") | System / Sales Associate | Customer Service Rep | 2–3 min |
| 4 | **Fast-Track Checkout & Loyalty Tracking**: (a) bundle is processed at POS as a single transaction with all items pre-grouped for fast scanning (pre-printed bundle barcode or quick-scan list on cashier terminal); (b) emergency repair bundles qualify for BuildRight's "Rush Repair" priority checkout lane (no queuing during emergencies); (c) for loyalty members: system automatically saves the repair details and materials purchased to the customer's account — useful for future maintenance tracking (e.g., "You repaired your roof with butyl tape on [date]. Roof patches typically last 1–2 years. Consider a professional roof inspection."); (d) system tracks emergency repair bundle sales data: bundle type, frequency by store, seasonal spikes (roof leak bundles spike during rainy season, flood cleanup bundles spike after typhoons), and average bundle value | Sales Associate / Cashier | Customer Service Rep | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Quick-Fix Bundle template library (product master with pre-defined SKU lists per emergency type)
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- Bundle customization engine (substitution logic based on customer specifications)
- Quick-Fix Instruction Card printer (template-based, bilingual English/Filipino)
- POS fast-scan module (bundle barcode or quick-scan list)
- Loyalty account integration (W17) — repair history tracking
- Seasonal demand analytics dashboard (bundle sales by type and season)
- Product substitution rules engine (W279)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Customer safety liability**: Emergency repairs often involve hazardous situations (roof work, electrical work, chemical handling); the instruction card must include prominent safety warnings, and Associates must verbally reinforce "If you're not comfortable doing this repair yourself, BuildRight can refer a professional."
- **Incomplete diagnosis**: The customer may describe the symptom incorrectly (e.g., "my roof is leaking" could be a plumbing leak from an overhead pipe, not the roof); the bundle may not address the actual problem; the instruction card must include a "diagnostic check" step before proceeding with the repair
- **Tool knowledge gap**: Many DIY customers don't know how to use the tools in the bundle; the instruction card must include basic tool usage guidance
- **Seasonal stockouts**: Roof leak bundles during rainy season and flood cleanup bundles after typhoons experience demand spikes; pre-positioning per W960 (seasonal forward stock pre-positioning) is critical


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-log of "Clogged Drain Bundle" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "checks real-time inventory for all bundle items at the store per W533" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-capture of "Quick-Fix Instruction Card & Advisory" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate / Customer Service Rep**: Emergency bundle assembly adds 5–10 minutes per customer; at ~40–60 emergency customers per store per month, this represents ~4–8 hours/month — absorbed by existing floor staff; the fast-track process is designed to reduce checkout time to offset the consultation time
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Emergency diagnosis & bundle selection: 3–5 min
- Bundle assembly & customization: 3–5 min
- Instruction card & advisory: 2–3 min
- Fast-track checkout: 3–5 min
- **Total per emergency**: 11–18 min

## W1102. Customer Retaining Wall & Foundation Formwork Material Estimation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests material estimation for a retaining wall or foundation formwork project, or Sales Associate identifies the need during a construction material consultation for a sloped lot or multi-level project |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,000 consultations/month across all stores (retaining walls are common in Philippine hillside and terraced construction; foundation formwork is needed for most new construction projects) |
| **Volume** | 1 retaining wall or formwork estimation per consultation |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Lumber/Building Materials Section), Customer |

### Background

Retaining walls and foundation formwork are critical structural elements in Philippine construction. The Philippines' mountainous and hilly terrain (especially in areas like Baguio, Cebu, Davao's hillside subdivisions, and Rizal province) creates widespread need for retaining walls: (a) **garden/landscape retaining walls**: low walls (0.3–1.0m height) for terraced gardens and landscaping, typically built with hollow blocks (CHB) or stone; (b) **structural retaining walls**: medium walls (1.0–3.0m height) for hillside lot leveling, built with reinforced concrete (RC) cantilever or gravity walls; (c) **major retaining walls**: high walls (>3.0m) for commercial and subdivision development, requiring structural engineering design. Foundation formwork is needed for all new construction projects that involve reinforced concrete: footings, columns, beams, and slabs. The formwork (also called "shuttering") is the temporary mold into which concrete is poured — typically made from lumber (plywood sheets and timber studs/wales) or steel formwork panels. This workflow addresses the material estimation for: (a) CHB/concrete block retaining walls; (b) reinforced concrete retaining walls; (c) foundation formwork (footings, columns, beams). This integrates with W976 (lumber grade selection) for formwork lumber, W1044 (concrete mix calculator) for concrete quantities, and W986 (rebar cutting and bending) for reinforcement steel.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Specification & Structural Input**: Sales Associate gathers project details: (a) **For retaining walls**: (i) wall height (meters) and total wall length (meters); (ii) retained soil height (may differ from wall height if wall is partially embedded); (iii) soil type: clay (high lateral pressure), sandy (moderate), gravel (low); (iv) surcharge load: is there a structure, driveway, or slope above the wall? (affects wall design); (v) wall type preference: CHB (hollow block — for walls ≤ 1.5m), reinforced concrete cantilever (for walls 1.0–3.0m), or gravity wall (stone/mass concrete — for walls ≤ 2.0m); (vi) drainage provision: weep holes (required for all retaining walls — prevents hydrostatic pressure buildup behind the wall); (b) **For foundation formwork**: (i) foundation type: isolated footing, continuous footing, mat foundation, or pile cap; (ii) number and dimensions of footings (length × width × depth); (iii) column dimensions and heights (from footing to beam level); (iv) beam dimensions (width × depth) and total beam length; (v) slab area and thickness (if slab formwork needed); (vi) formwork material preference: lumber (plywood + timber) — most common in the Philippines, or steel panels — for large projects; (c) **Site conditions**: (i) accessibility for material delivery; (ii) water table level (affects concrete mix and waterproofing needs); (iii) seismic zone per the NSCP zone map (Zone 1–4; Zone 3–4 areas drive reinforcement requirements) | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 8–12 min |
| 2 | **Retaining Wall Material Calculation**: System calculates retaining wall materials based on wall type: **For CHB wall (≤ 1.5m height)**: (a) CHB quantity = wall area (length × height) ÷ CHB face area (0.2m × 0.4m = 0.08 sqm face area for both 4" and 6" CHB — the nominal size is thickness, not face) × 1.05 (5% wastage); (b) cement mortar: 1 bag cement per 70 pieces of 4" CHB or 1 bag per 50 pieces of 6" CHB; sand: 0.08 cubic meters per bag of cement; (c) reinforcement: for CHB walls > 0.6m, vertical rebar (10mm or 12mm) at 0.40m centers grouted into CHB cells; horizontal rebar (10mm) at every 2 courses; (d) foundation: concrete strip footing below wall — width = wall height × 0.5, depth = 0.30m minimum; concrete volume per W1044; (e) drainage: weep holes (50mm PVC pipe) at 1.5m centers along wall base; gravel drain (¾" crushed stone) behind the wall with filter fabric; **For RC cantilever wall (1.0–3.0m)**: (f) concrete volume = (base slab + stem wall + toe + heel) based on standard cantilever wall proportions; system calculates per wall height; (g) reinforcement steel: main vertical bars (12mm or 16mm), horizontal distribution bars (10mm or 12mm), base slab reinforcement (12mm or 16mm both ways); rebar weight calculated per kg per W986; (h) formwork: plywood (12mm thick, 4'×8' sheets) for wall faces; timber studs (2"×3" at 400mm centers); timber wales (2"×4" at 600mm vertical spacing); form ties (snap ties or threaded rod with wing nuts); (i) concrete volume per W1044 — mix ratio 1:2:4 (Class A) for structural retaining walls; (j) drainage: perforated PVC drain pipe (110mm diameter) along wall base with gravel surround; weep holes at 1.5m centers | System | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 3 | **Foundation Formwork Material Calculation**: System calculates formwork materials: (a) **Footing formwork**: (i) plywood area = 2 × (footing length + footing width) × footing depth per footing × number of footings; (ii) studs: 2"×3" at 400mm centers along plywood; (iii) braces: 2"×4" diagonal braces to prevent form bulging under concrete pressure; (iv) form ties: snap ties per lineal meter of formwork; (b) **Column formwork**: (i) plywood area = column perimeter × column height × number of columns; (ii) yokes/clamps: at 400mm vertical spacing (made from 2"×4" lumber or steel column clamps); (iii) kicker: bottom form to prevent grout leakage; (c) **Beam formwork**: (i) plywood area = 2 × beam depth × beam length + beam width × beam length (for soffit); (ii) soffit supports: 2"×4" joists at 400mm centers; (iii) shores/posts: 4"×4" lumber at 1.0m centers to support beam soffit; (iv) side forms: 2"×3" studs at 400mm centers with 2"×4" wales; (d) **Slab formwork**: (i) plywood area = slab area (sqm); (ii) joists: 2"×4" at 400mm centers; (iii) stringers: 2"×6" at 1.0m centers perpendicular to joists; (iv) shores: 4"×4" at 1.0m × 1.0m grid; (e) **Common formwork materials**: (i) form release agent (oil or emulsion — prevents concrete from sticking to plywood); (ii) nails (assorted: 2" for plywood-to-stud, 3" for stud-to-wale, 4" for braces); (iii) tie wire (16-gauge black annealed wire for reinforcement tying); (iv) spacers: concrete cover spacers (25mm or 50mm) to maintain rebar cover; (f) lumber reuse factor: plywood = 4–5 uses (for typical construction quality), timber = 6–8 uses; system estimates new lumber purchase vs. potential reuse for customers with multiple pours | System | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min (automated) |
| 4 | **Companion Materials & Complete BOM**: System adds companion materials: (a) **Concrete materials** per W1044: cement, sand, gravel — quantities based on total concrete volume from steps 2/3; (b) **Reinforcement steel** per W986: rebar cutting and bending schedule; tie wire quantity; rebar chairs/spacers; (c) **Waterproofing**: for retaining walls — apply waterproofing membrane (cementitious or bituminous) to the back face (soil side) before backfilling; quantity based on wall area; (d) **Backfill and drainage**: gravel (¾" crushed stone) for drainage layer behind wall; filter fabric (non-woven geotextile) to prevent soil clogging the drainage; perforated PVC drain pipe (110mm); (e) **Compaction**: gravel base compaction — manual tamper for small projects, plate compactor rental per W1094 for larger projects; (f) **Tools**: carpentry tools for formwork (hammer, saw, level, plumb bob, measuring tape, string line), concrete tools (wheelbarrow, shovel, trowel, vibrator rental per W1094), and rebar tools (bolt cutter or hacksaw, bending tool, tie wire twister) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 2–3 min (automated) |
| 5 | **Estimate, Safety Advisory & Professional Referral**: (a) system generates itemized estimate with: structural sketch (simplified section view of retaining wall or formwork layout), complete BOM organized by category (structural, concrete, reinforcement, formwork, drainage, tools), quantities and prices, total estimated cost; (b) safety and engineering advisory: "Retaining walls exceeding 1.0m height and all structural foundations must be designed by a licensed Civil Engineer. The National Structural Code of the Philippines (NSCP) 2015 governs structural design in Seismic Zone 4. This material estimation is for quantity purposes only and does not constitute a structural design. BuildRight Depot strongly recommends engaging a licensed Civil Engineer to design and supervise the construction of retaining walls and foundations."; (c) for retaining walls > 3.0m: system requires a disclaimer that the estimation is for preliminary budgeting only and must not be used for construction without a professional engineer's design; (d) system offers referral to licensed civil engineers and contractors per W904; (e) estimate saved to customer loyalty account | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Retaining wall material calculator (CHB and RC wall types)
- Formwork material calculator (footing, column, beam, slab types)
- Concrete mix calculator integration (W1044)
- Rebar calculator integration (W986)
- Product attribute master (W298) — lumber dimensions, CHB specifications, rebar weights
- Real-time inventory availability per store (W533)
- POS quotation module (W542)
- Loyalty account integration (W17)
- Contractor referral service module (W904)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Structural safety liability**: Retaining wall failures can cause property damage, injury, and death; the disclaimer must be prominent and the Associate must verbally emphasize the need for professional engineering design for walls > 1.0m
- **Seismic design complexity**: Philippine Seismic Zone 4 requires specific reinforcement and design considerations that cannot be addressed by a simple material calculator; the system must not produce designs that appear to substitute for professional engineering
- **Concrete quality sensitivity**: Retaining wall concrete must achieve specified strength (typically 3,000–4,000 PSI / 20–28 MPa for structural walls); improper concrete mix ratio or inadequate curing leads to structural weakness; concrete mix instructions per W1044 must be precise
- **Formwork failure risk**: Formwork collapse during concrete pouring is a leading cause of construction accidents in the Philippines; the formwork material estimation must include adequate bracing and the instruction card must emphasize formwork inspection before pouring; mitigated by CTL-267 (controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2))


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "centers grouted into CHB cells" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates formwork materials" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-log of "clogging the drainage" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must not produce designs that appear to substitute for professional engineering

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Lumber/Building Materials Section)**: Estimation consultation adds 15–25 minutes per customer; at ~10–15 consultations per store per month, this represents ~3–6 hours/month — absorbed by existing Lumber Department staff
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Project specification: 8–12 min
- Retaining wall calculation: 3–5 min (automated)
- Formwork calculation: 3–5 min (automated)
- Companion materials: 2–3 min (automated)
- Estimate & advisory: 3–5 min
- **Total per consultation**: 19–30 min

## W1124. Customer Construction Material Compatibility Cross-Reference & Substitution Advisory

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer inquiry about product compatibility, or system identifies out-of-stock item requiring substitution recommendation |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–12,000 compatibility queries/month across all stores (peak during summer renovation season Mar–May) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 product pairs per query |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (Department-specific) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Department Supervisor, Customer |

### Background

Home improvement and construction projects require materials that are technically compatible: (a) tile adhesive must match tile type (ceramic, porcelain, natural stone, large format); (b) paint primer must be compatible with surface material (concrete, wood, metal, previously painted); (c) electrical wire gauge must match circuit breaker amperage; (d) plumbing pipe material must match fitting type (PVC, PPR, copper, galvanized); (e) lumber span tables must match structural load requirements. Customers — especially DIYers (55% of revenue) — frequently purchase incompatible combinations that lead to project failures, product returns, and customer dissatisfaction. This workflow provides a structured compatibility advisory service at the store level, supported by an ERP-based cross-reference matrix and trained staff. It complements W279 (product substitution rules) by extending it to customer-facing advisory, and W971 (power tool battery compatibility checker) by broadening it to all product categories.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Need Identification**: (a) customer approaches sales associate with project description or product list; (b) sales associate identifies the project type: bathroom renovation, electrical installation, plumbing repair, roofing, painting, etc.; (c) determines customer skill level: professional contractor, experienced DIYer, or first-time DIYer; (d) captures key project parameters: room size, surface material, load requirements, environmental conditions (indoor/outdoor, wet/dry area) | Sales Associate | Sales Associate | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Compatibility Cross-Reference Lookup**: (a) sales associate scans or searches primary product in ERP mobile app or in-store kiosk; (b) system displays compatibility matrix: required complementary products (adhesive, primer, connector, fastener), compatible brands/models, and incompatibility warnings; (c) system highlights common mismatch errors (e.g., "Using mastic adhesive with large-format porcelain tile — recommend modified thin-set mortar instead"); (d) for electrical and plumbing: system applies Philippine building code requirements (National Structural Code of the Philippines, Philippine Electrical Code) to validate specifications; (e) if primary product is out-of-stock, system suggests verified substitutes per W279 with compatibility confirmation | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Customer Advisory & Bundle Recommendation**: (a) sales associate presents compatibility-verified product selection to customer; (b) explains why certain combinations are required (e.g., "This waterproofing membrane requires this specific primer for proper adhesion — using a different primer will void the manufacturer warranty"); (c) offers complete bundle: all required complementary materials (adhesive, grout, spacers, waterproofing, tools) with bundle pricing per W910; (d) for complex projects (full bathroom renovation, electrical panel upgrade), recommends consultation with in-store design consultant (W148) or pro desk specialist (W112); (e) provides printed or emailed project material list with compatibility notes for future reference | Sales Associate | Sales Associate | 5–8 min |
| 4 | **Post-Sale Follow-Up & Issue Tracking**: (a) system logs compatibility advisory as part of customer's purchase history; (b) if customer returns with compatibility-related product failure (e.g., tile delamination due to wrong adhesive), the advisory record is referenced to determine if correct guidance was provided; (c) compatibility issues reported to merchandising team for product data improvement (W50 PIM); (d) recurring compatibility confusion flagged for better product labeling, shelf signage, or package bundling | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 2–3 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Product compatibility cross-reference matrix (ERP master data module, linked to W298 product attribute templates)
- Product substitution rules engine (W279)
- Mobile ERP app / in-store kiosk — customer-facing compatibility lookup
- Bundle pricing engine (W910)
- PIM system (W50) — product relationship and attribute data
- Customer purchase history module — advisory logging
- Returns module (W12) — compatibility failure analysis

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Data accuracy**: Compatibility matrix must be maintained by merchandising team with vendor input; outdated data can lead to incorrect recommendations
- **Liability**: Providing compatibility advice creates implied warranty risk; disclaimers must be clear that recommendations are advisory and customers should verify with licensed professionals for structural, electrical, and plumbing work
- **Training depth**: Sales associates need periodic training on new products and compatibility rules; high turnover (~15–20%) means continuous training investment
- **Edge cases**: Unusual substrate conditions (e.g., painting over previously oil-painted surfaces, tiling over existing tiles) require expert judgment beyond system recommendations


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "captures key project parameters" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-scan of "scans or searches primary product in ERP mobile app or in-store kiosk" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "printed or emailed project material list with compatibility notes for future…" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: recommendations

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: Compatibility queries add ~10–15 min per interaction; at ~40–60 queries/store/month, this represents ~7–15 hours/month — distributed across 12 sales associates per store, ~1 hour/month each
- **Department Supervisor**: Complex escalation queries add ~2–3 hours/week per supervisor
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — improved attach rate (estimated 15–25% increase in complementary product sales with active advisory) justifies staff time

### Time Estimate
- Need identification: 3–5 min
- Cross-reference lookup: 3–5 min
- Advisory & bundle: 5–8 min
- Post-sale logging: 2–3 min
- **Total per query**: 13–21 min

## W1129. Customer Interior Design Material Board Curation & Mood Board Assembly Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests design consultation for renovation project, or walks in with design inspiration (magazine photo, Pinterest board, contractor plan) |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–5,000 material board requests/month across all stores (peak during summer renovation season) |
| **Volume** | 1 material board per request (typically covers 1–2 rooms) |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (Design-trained) / Design Consultant (W148) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate / Design Consultant, Customer, Department Supervisors (relevant departments) |

### Background

Home renovation customers often struggle to visualize how different materials (tiles, paint, fixtures, countertops, cabinets, hardware) will look together in their finished space. Professional interior designers create "material boards" (also called mood boards or design boards) — physical assemblies of material samples showing the proposed combination of finishes, colors, and textures for a room. BuildRight Depot's extensive product range across tiles, paint, plumbing fixtures, lighting, hardware, and countertops makes it uniquely positioned to offer this service. The material board serves as: (a) a visualization tool for the customer to approve the design direction before purchasing; (b) a shopping list of coordinated products that ensures aesthetic consistency; (c) a specification reference for contractors executing the renovation; (d) an upselling vehicle — customers who see a complete coordinated design buy more complementary products than those purchasing individual items. This workflow complements W148 (design consultancy), W211 (3D rendering), W999 (tile sample loan), and W903 (material sample loan) by focusing on the physical material board curation process.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Design Consultation & Style Profiling**: (a) customer describes renovation project: room type (bathroom, kitchen, living room, bedroom), design style preference (modern minimalist, industrial, tropical Filipino, Mediterranean, Scandinavian), budget range, and existing elements to keep; (b) design-trained sales associate or design consultant reviews customer's inspiration materials (photos, magazine clippings, phone images); (c) associate uses in-store design kiosk or tablet to browse BuildRight's product catalog filtered by customer's style, budget, and room type; (d) initial color palette established: 2–3 primary colors, 1–2 accent colors | Sales Associate / Design Consultant | Department Supervisor | 15–25 min |
| 2 | **Material Selection & Sample Gathering**: (a) associate guides customer through relevant departments to select physical samples: tiles (floor and wall) — 2–3 options per surface; paint — color swatches or mixed sample pots; countertop material (granite, quartz, laminate) — sample chips; cabinet hardware (handles, knobs) — physical samples; plumbing fixtures (faucet, shower head) — display models or catalog photos; lighting fixtures — catalog or display samples; (b) for each material, system shows: price per unit/sqm, availability, lead time, and complementary product suggestions; (c) customer selects preferred option for each material category; (d) associate gathers physical samples (tile chips, paint swatches, material chips) | Sales Associate / Design Consultant | Sales Associate | 20–40 min |
| 3 | **Material Board Assembly & Documentation**: (a) associate assembles physical material board on store-provided foam board (A3 size): tiles arranged as they would appear (floor tile at bottom, wall tile above, accent tile as border), paint swatches showing wall color and trim, countertop sample, hardware samples placed on countertop, fixture photos pinned alongside; (b) associate photographs completed material board for digital record; (c) system generates product list linked to material board: each item with SKU, quantity needed (based on room dimensions), unit price, and estimated total; (d) digital material board saved to customer's account for future access and sharing with contractor | Sales Associate / Design Consultant | Sales Associate | 15–20 min |
| 4 | **Quotation Generation & Sales Conversion**: (a) system calculates total project cost based on room dimensions and selected materials; (b) quotation generated per W542 with itemized material list and room-specific quantities; (c) bundle discount offered if customer purchases complete material board package: 5–10% off total vs. individual item pricing per W910; (d) if customer approves: sales order created with scheduled delivery or will-call per W773; (e) if customer takes material board home to decide: associate schedules 48-hour follow-up call per W744; (f) material board retained by customer as reference; physical samples returned per W903 loan policy if not purchased | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Product catalog module — filtered browse by style, room, budget
- Room dimension calculator — quantity estimation based on room measurements
- Bundle pricing engine (W910) — complete package discount
- Quotation generation module (W542) — project quotation
- Customer account module — saved material boards and project lists
- CRM module — design consultation history
- Inventory module — real-time stock and lead time for selected items

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Sample inventory**: Physical tile chips, paint swatches, and material samples are consumable; replenishment must be managed per W903
- **Time investment**: 45–100 minutes per material board session requires dedicated staff time; may not be feasible during peak weekend hours without scheduling
- **Non-conversion risk**: Customer may use the material board as a reference to purchase from competitors or online; offering bundle discount and same-day availability mitigates this
- **Design liability**: Material board is advisory, not a professional interior design service; disclaimers must be clear that color accuracy depends on lighting conditions and actual product may vary from samples
- **Space requirements**: In-store design consultation area needs dedicated space with good lighting, sample storage, and work surface — competing with selling floor space


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-scan of "Customer Design Consultation & Style Profiling" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-log of "Material Selection & Sample Gathering" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-record of "Material Board Assembly & Documentation" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Sales Associate (Design-trained) / Design Consultant (W148) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Design-trained)**: 1–2 associates per store trained in basic design consultation; ~15–25 material board sessions/month during peak season = ~15–25 hours/month — covered by design-trained associates during assigned consultation hours
- **Design Consultant (HQ)**: Complex projects escalated to 2 HQ-based consultants who visit stores by appointment — existing roles under W148
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- Style profiling: 15–25 min
- Material selection: 20–40 min
- Board assembly: 15–20 min
- Quotation & conversion: 10–15 min
- **Total per session**: 60–100 min

## W1138. Customer Construction Worker Safety Gear Bundle Recommendation & Compliance Package

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases construction materials (especially for new projects), or inquires about PPE/safety equipment, or store identifies contractor/trade customer |
| **Frequency** | ~6,000–8,000 safety gear recommendations/month across all stores |
| **Volume** | 1 bundle recommendation per customer interaction |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (Safety & PPE Department) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer (Contractor/Worker), Department Supervisor |

### Background

BuildRight Depot carries a dedicated Safety & PPE category (~700 SKUs, 2% of active assortment) including hard hats, safety glasses, gloves, dust masks, ear protection, safety harnesses, steel-toed boots, high-visibility vests, and first aid kits. Philippine DOLE regulations (DO 13 — Construction Safety) mandate specific PPE for construction workers, and contractors face penalties for non-compliance. However, many small contractors and individual workers either: (a) don't know which PPE is legally required; (b) purchase the cheapest available option regardless of quality/certification; (c) skip PPE purchases to save money, especially for short-term projects. By packaging safety gear into role-specific bundles (e.g., "Mason's Safety Kit," "Electrician's Safety Kit," "General Construction Worker Compliance Pack"), BuildRight can: increase PPE category attachment rate, ensure customers are compliant with DOLE regulations, differentiate on safety leadership, and create a high-margin bundle that complements construction material purchases. This complements W910 (product bundle management) and W140 (OHS incident management) by focusing on proactive PPE sales.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer Identification & Needs Assessment**: (a) when customer purchases construction materials (cement, rebar, electrical supplies, plumbing materials), Sales Associate asks about project type and worker count; (b) for contractor/trade customers: "How many workers will be on site? Do they have complete PPE?"; (c) for DIY customers: "Are you doing the work yourself? Here's what you'll need to stay safe"; (d) identify worker roles: masonry, carpentry, electrical, plumbing, painting, welding, general labor, scaffolding; (e) assess current PPE condition: "Do your workers already have hard hats and safety boots that are in good condition?" | Sales Associate | Sales Associate | 3–5 min |
| 2 | **Bundle Recommendation**: (a) Sales Associate recommends pre-configured safety bundle from ERP based on worker role: **General Construction Pack** (hard hat, safety glasses, dust mask N95, leather gloves, high-vis vest, steel-toed boots) — PHP 2,500–3,500; **Mason's Pack** (add knee pads, rubber gloves, long rubber boots); **Electrician's Pack** (add insulated gloves, voltage tester, arc-flash safety glasses); **Welder's Pack** (add welding helmet, leather apron, welding gloves, ear plugs); **Scaffolding/Heights Pack** (add full-body safety harness, lanyard, safety helmet with chin strap); (b) system displays bundle with itemized contents and individual vs. bundle pricing (bundle discount: 10–15% vs. individual items); (c) if customer has partial PPE already: system recommends only missing items à la carte | Sales Associate / System | Sales Associate | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Compliance Education & Documentation**: (a) Sales Associate provides brief DOLE compliance overview: "Under DOLE Department Order No. 13, all construction workers must wear hard hats, safety shoes, and appropriate PPE on site. Employers are responsible for providing PPE at no cost to workers"; (b) offers DOLE compliance checklist printed or emailed from ERP; (c) for contractors: offers to generate a "Safety Compliance Certificate" — a BuildRight-branded document listing all PPE items purchased, which the contractor can present to DOLE inspectors or project owners as evidence of compliance; (d) certificates stored in customer's account for annual renewal tracking | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 4 | **Sale Processing & PPE Replenishment**: (a) PPE bundle added to customer's transaction at POS with bundle pricing applied; (b) loyalty points earned on PPE purchases (standard 1 point per PHP 100); (c) system tracks PPE bundle sales by store and customer segment for inventory replenishment; (d) PPE items that are part of bundles monitored for stockout risk — safety items should not go out of stock (safety stock maintained at higher levels); (e) seasonal demand spikes: PPE demand increases during summer construction peak (Mar–May) and post-typhoon reconstruction (Jul–Dec) | Sales Associate / POS System | Department Supervisor | 5–8 min |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP product module — pre-configured safety bundle definitions and pricing
- Bundle pricing engine (W910) — bundle discount calculation
- POS module — bundle scanning and pricing
- Customer account module — safety compliance certificate storage
- Inventory module — PPE stock monitoring and replenishment alerts
- CRM module — PPE purchase history for renewal reminders
- Print/email module — DOLE compliance checklist and safety certificate generation

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Price sensitivity**: Small contractors may resist PPE purchases due to cost; bundle discount and compliance framing are essential to overcome price resistance
- **Cheaper alternatives**: Hardware stores (rito/rito, neighborhood hardware) sell unbranded, uncertified PPE at lower prices; BuildRight must differentiate on quality, certification, and compliance documentation
- **Bundle composition**: Pre-set bundles may not perfectly match every worker's needs; flexibility to swap individual items within a bundle is important
- **Seasonal demand fluctuation**: PPE demand fluctuates significantly with construction season and post-disaster reconstruction; planning must account for surge demand


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "Customer Identification & Needs Assessment" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-test of "tester, arc-flash safety glasses" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "printed or emailed from ERP" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: PPE recommendation adds ~3–5 min per relevant customer interaction; with ~1–2 relevant interactions/store/day involving construction materials (30–40/store/month per the Frequency), this is ~1.5–3 hours/month distributed across associates — absorbed by existing team
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — PPE category margin (~40–50%) justifies the sales effort

### Time Estimate
- Customer identification: 3–5 min
- Bundle recommendation: 3–5 min
- Compliance education: 3–5 min
- Sale processing: 5–8 min
- **Total per interaction**: 14–23 min (only when PPE recommendation is relevant)

## W1152. Store-Level Customer Project Material Quantity Verification & Completeness Check

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer presents project material list for purchase, or customer requests verification that they have all required materials for their project |
| **Frequency** | ~3,000–5,000 completeness checks/month across all stores (peak during summer renovation season) |
| **Volume** | 1 project material list per check (typically 15–60 line items) |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (Department-specific) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Department Supervisor, Customer |

### Background

One of the most common pain points for DIY customers (55% of revenue) and even professional contractors is arriving at a construction site and discovering they're missing a critical material: the wrong size PVC fitting, no silicone sealant for the bathroom installation, insufficient tile adhesive, or missing electrical connectors. This results in: (a) project delays (return trip to store); (b) impulse purchases from nearest competitor; (c) improvised substitutions that compromise quality; (d) customer frustration attributed to BuildRight for not advising them properly. Professional contractors who experience repeated material shortages switch suppliers. This workflow provides a systematic completeness check at the point of sale: the sales associate reviews the customer's project material list against a completeness checklist stored in the ERP, cross-referenced with the project type (bathroom renovation, electrical installation, roofing, etc.), and proactively identifies missing items, incorrect quantities, and complementary materials needed. It complements W1124 (material compatibility advisory), W823 (material calculator), and W967 (material takeoff estimation) by adding the completeness verification layer at the point of purchase.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Type Identification & Checklist Selection**: (a) customer presents material list (paper, phone photo, or BuildRight project list from W894 Project Vault); (b) sales associate identifies project type from list or customer description: bathroom renovation, kitchen renovation, electrical installation, plumbing repair, roofing, fencing, painting, flooring, or general construction; (c) system loads corresponding completeness checklist: e.g., "Bathroom Renovation" checklist includes 47 required items across 8 categories (structural, waterproofing, plumbing, electrical, tiles, fixtures, hardware, consumables); (d) if project type unclear, associate uses guided questionnaire: "What room are you working on?", "Is it a new installation or renovation?", "What's the approximate area?" | Sales Associate | Sales Associate | 2–3 min |
| 2 | **Material List Cross-Reference & Gap Identification**: (a) associate scans or enters customer's material list into ERP completeness checker; (b) system compares customer's list against completeness checklist: matched items (customer has it), missing required items (customer needs it), missing recommended items (customer should consider it), quantity check (is the quantity sufficient for the project scope based on area/dimensions?); (c) system flags gaps: "You're tiling a 15 sqm bathroom floor — your list has 14 sqm of tiles. We recommend purchasing 10% extra for cuts and breakage. Add 2 sqm?"; "You have PVC pipes and fittings but no PVC solvent cement. Required for installation."; "You selected a shower faucet but no shower head. Do you need one?"; (d) for compatibility: system also runs compatibility check per W1124 to verify selected items work together | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 3–5 min |
| 3 | **Customer Review & Gap Resolution**: (a) associate presents completeness check results on tablet or printout: ✓ items already on list, ⚠ missing required items, 💡 recommended additions; (b) for missing required items: associate explains why the item is needed and offers to add to purchase; (c) for quantity shortfalls: associate explains the calculation (e.g., "Based on your 20 sqm floor area with 10% waste factor, you need 22 sqm of tiles. Your list has 18 sqm — that's 4 sqm short."); (d) customer decides which gaps to fill; (e) associate adds approved items to the sales order; (f) system generates updated material list with all items (original + additions) and total estimated project cost | Sales Associate | Sales Associate | 5–8 min |
| 4 | **Post-Purchase Project List Archival**: (a) completed material list saved to customer's account as a "Verified Project List" per W894 (Project Vault); (b) customer can share verified list with contractor or reference for future similar projects; (c) system generates project receipt: items purchased, quantities, prices, and BuildRight store location(s); (d) if customer returns within 30 days for missing items from the same project: associate can pull up verified list and quickly identify and add missing items; (e) completeness check data feeds analytics: most commonly missed items by project type → used to improve checklist and staff training | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 1–2 min |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP completeness checker module — project checklist database, item matching, gap identification
- Product compatibility matrix (W1124) — cross-reference for compatibility verification
- Material calculator (W823) — quantity verification based on project dimensions
- Customer account module / Project Vault (W894) — verified project list archival
- POS module — adding gap items to sales order
- Analytics module — commonly missed items, completeness check conversion rate, project list reuse

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Checklist maintenance**: Completeness checklists must be updated with new products, discontinued items, and evolving building practices; requires merchandising team ownership
- **Customer perception**: Some customers may view completeness check as upselling; associate must frame as service ("We want to save you a return trip")
- **Time at checkout**: Completeness check adds 5–10 min at the point of purchase; best performed at department level before proceeding to checkout, not at the cashier
- **Project complexity**: Custom or unusual projects may not fit standard checklists; associate judgment needed for non-standard items
- **Accuracy of customer-provided dimensions**: Quantity calculations depend on accurate project dimensions; customer estimates may be wrong


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-validate of "Project Type Identification & Checklist Selection" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-capture of "enters customer's material list into ERP completeness checker" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "Customer Review & Gap Resolution" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Sales Associate (Department-specific) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: Completeness check adds 10–18 min per interaction; at ~15–25 checks/store/month across 12 associates, ~2–4 hours/month per associate during peak season — absorbed by existing role with improved conversion rate
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — estimated 10–15% increase in average transaction value for checked projects justifies time investment

### Time Estimate
- Project type identification: 2–3 min
- Cross-reference & gap ID: 3–5 min
- Customer review: 5–8 min
- Archival: 1–2 min
- **Total per check**: 11–18 min
