## W1154. Store-Level Customer Paint VOC & Indoor Air Quality Compliance Advisory

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer purchases paint or coating products, or customer inquires about indoor air quality, or customer specifies enclosed-space application |
| **Frequency** | ~8,000–12,000 VOC advisory interactions/month across all stores (paint is 8% of active SKUs, ~2,800 SKUs) |
| **Volume** | 1–5 paint/coating products per interaction |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate (Paint & Finishes Department) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer |

### Background

Paint and coating products emit volatile organic compounds (VOCs) that affect indoor air quality and human health. Philippine regulations and global standards increasingly restrict VOC content in paints: (a) DENR Administrative Order (DAO) sets VOC limits for architectural paints; (b) Green building standards (BERDE, the Philippine green building rating system per W800) require low-VOC paints for certified buildings; (c) enclosed spaces (bathrooms, kitchens, small rooms) amplify VOC exposure risks; (d) vulnerable populations (children, elderly, pregnant women, those with respiratory conditions) face heightened health risks from VOC exposure; (e) occupational safety standards require proper ventilation during and after painting. BuildRight's paint category (2,800 SKUs, 8% of active assortment) includes products with varying VOC levels: zero-VOC (water-based acrylics), low-VOC (most latex paints, <50g/L VOC), medium-VOC (some enamels, 50–150g/L), and high-VOC (solvent-based paints, lacquers, industrial coatings, >150g/L). This workflow provides point-of-sale advisory to help customers select appropriate paint products based on application environment, ventilation conditions, and health considerations. It complements W168 (paint mixing), W1130 (tinting machine calibration), and W1093 (paint color scheme consultation) by adding the health and regulatory compliance dimension.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Application Environment Assessment**: (a) customer approaches paint department or system identifies paint purchase at POS; (b) sales associate asks application questions: "Where will this paint be used?" (bedroom, bathroom, kitchen, living room, exterior, commercial space), "How large is the space?", "Is the space well-ventilated?", "Are there children, elderly, pregnant women, or anyone with respiratory conditions in the household?", "Is this for a BERDE/LEED-certified building project?"; (c) associate enters responses into ERP paint advisory module | Sales Associate | Sales Associate | 1–2 min |
| 2 | **VOC Level Matching & Product Recommendation**: (a) system displays paint options filtered by application: enclosed rooms with vulnerable occupants → recommend zero-VOC or low-VOC only; well-ventilated spaces without vulnerable occupants → low or medium VOC acceptable; exterior applications → no VOC restriction (outdoor ventilation sufficient); commercial/industrial with PPE → all options available; BERDE/LEED projects → zero or low-VOC with compliance documentation; (b) system shows VOC content (g/L) for each paint option alongside standard performance specs (coverage, durability, finish); (c) associate explains trade-offs: zero/low-VOC paints may have slightly fewer color options or longer drying time but are safer for indoor use; (d) for high-VOC products selected for enclosed spaces: system displays advisory warning and requires customer acknowledgment per W520-style prompt | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 2–3 min |
| 3 | **Ventilation & Safety Guidance**: (a) for any indoor paint purchase, associate provides ventilation guidance: "Open windows and use fans during and after painting", "Wait at least 24 hours before reoccupying the room for low-VOC; 48–72 hours for medium/high-VOC", "Wear a paint mask (N95 or VOC-rated) when applying solvent-based paints"; (b) system suggests complementary safety products: paint mask (N95), gloves, drop cloths, painter's tape, ventilation fan; (c) for large indoor projects: recommend purchasing or renting a portable ventilation fan per W139 (tool rental); (d) guidance printed on receipt alongside paint mixing instructions | Sales Associate | Sales Associate | 1–2 min |
| 4 | **Compliance Documentation (BERDE/LEED)**: (a) for customers specifying green building certification: system generates VOC compliance certificate for each paint product showing: product name, manufacturer, VOC content (g/L), applicable standard (DENR DAO, Green Seal, or equivalent), and batch number; (b) certificate available as PDF for customer's LEED/BERDE documentation submission; (c) VOC compliance data maintained in product master and linked to SDS (W698) | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 2–3 min (if applicable) |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP paint advisory module — application questionnaire, VOC filtering, product recommendation
- Product master (W252) — VOC content per SKU, certification data
- SDS module (W698) — safety data sheet access, VOC documentation
- POS module — advisory prompts, receipt printing of safety guidance
- Item compatibility module — complementary product suggestions
- Compliance certificate generator — BERDE/LEED documentation
- Analytics module — VOC advisory completion rate, low-VOC product sales mix, customer compliance tracking

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Product availability**: Low/zero-VOC paint options may be limited in the Philippine market, especially for specialty finishes; customer may need to accept higher-VOC alternative
- **Price premium**: Zero/low-VOC paints typically cost 15–30% more than standard formulations; customer may resist the premium without clear health benefit explanation
- **Advisory fatigue**: Adding VOC advisory to every paint transaction may slow checkout; automate where possible and limit detailed advisory to high-risk applications (enclosed spaces, vulnerable occupants)
- **Regulatory evolution**: VOC regulations continue to tighten; product data must be updated as new standards take effect
- **Customer skepticism**: Some customers may view VOC advisory as upselling premium products; factual, health-focused framing essential


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "enters responses into ERP paint advisory module" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-document of "VOC Level Matching & Product Recommendation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-generate of "printed on receipt alongside paint mixing instructions" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: standard operating procedures; system-enforced validation rules

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: VOC advisory adds ~2–5 min per paint transaction; at ~40–60 paint transactions/store/month, ~1.5–5 hours/month total across paint department associates — absorbed by existing role
- **No permanent incremental headcount** — low-VOC products typically carry higher margins, offsetting advisory time

### Time Estimate
- Application assessment: 1–2 min
- VOC matching: 2–3 min
- Safety guidance: 1–2 min
- Compliance documentation: 2–3 min (if needed)
- **Total per interaction**: 4–10 min

## W1158. Store-Level Customer Project Material List Sharing & Contractor Collaborative Shopping

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer saves a project material list in Project Vault (W894) and shares it with their contractor, or contractor requests access to customer's material list, or customer presents shared material list for collaborative purchase |
| **Frequency** | ~2,000–3,000 shared lists/month across all stores |
| **Volume** | 1 shared list per project; 1–3 collaborators per list |
| **Owner** | Sales Associate |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate, Customer (Homeowner), Contractor/Builder, BuildRight Loyalty System |

### Background

Home renovation projects in the Philippines typically involve a homeowner (BuildRight's B2C customer) and a contractor (BuildRight's B2B trade customer). Effective collaboration between these two parties on material selection and purchasing is critical but often fragmented: (a) homeowner selects tiles at BuildRight; contractor visits separately to confirm quantities; neither knows what the other ordered; (b) contractor recommends specific brands; homeowner purchases different brands; installation fails due to incompatibility; (c) homeowner pays for materials; contractor picks up from store — payment and pickup authorization is unclear; (d) contractor needs to verify homeowner's material selections before starting work but has no easy way to see the list. This workflow enables homeowners and contractors to collaborate on a shared project material list through BuildRight's Project Vault (W894): (a) homeowner creates material list and shares it with contractor via unique link; (b) contractor reviews list, adds/corrects quantities, confirms compatibility; (c) either party can purchase from the shared list (with proper authorization); (d) system tracks purchases against the list for project completion monitoring. This increases customer loyalty (both B2C and B2B), improves project success rate, and drives incremental sales. It complements W894 (Project Vault), W904 (contractor referral), and W112 (pro desk) by enabling digital collaboration between homeowner and contractor.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project Material List Creation & Sharing**: (a) homeowner creates project material list in BuildRight app or in-store kiosk (linked to W894 Project Vault); (b) list includes: room/area, selected materials (tiles, paint, fixtures), quantities (based on calculator W823), preferred brands, budget range; (c) homeowner shares list with contractor via: unique share link sent via SMS/email, or contractor's loyalty card number lookup in system; (d) contractor receives notification with shared list access; (e) contractor reviews list on their mobile device or in-store kiosk | Customer (Homeowner) | Customer | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Contractor Review & List Modification**: (a) contractor opens shared list and reviews each item: confirms product selection appropriate for project, adjusts quantities based on professional experience (e.g., "Homeowner calculated 22 sqm tiles but I know we need 25 sqm for the layout pattern"), suggests alternative products ("This waterproofing membrane is better for Philippine tropical conditions"), flags missing items ("We need waterproofing tape for the corners — not on the list"); (b) contractor saves modifications with comments visible to homeowner; (c) system notifies homeowner of contractor's changes for approval; (d) homeowner approves or discusses changes in-app or in-store | Customer (Contractor) | Customer | 10–20 min |
| 3 | **Collaborative Purchase Authorization**: (a) once list is finalized, either party can initiate purchase; (b) system supports flexible payment and pickup options: homeowner pays online → contractor picks up in-store (with authorization code); homeowner pays in-store → contractor picks up later (with authorization code); contractor pays on trade account → homeowner reimburses separately; split payment: homeowner pays for materials, contractor pays for installation accessories; (c) purchase authorization linked to shared list: each line item marked as "purchased" when bought by either party; (d) system prevents double-purchase: if contractor buys tiles from shared list, tiles marked as purchased and homeowner sees update | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Project Completion Tracking & Post-Project Review**: (a) system tracks purchase completion: items purchased / total items on shared list; (b) both parties receive progress notifications: "15 of 18 items purchased. Remaining: 10 sqm wall tiles, 2 boxes grout, 1 tube silicone sealant."; (c) when all items purchased: system generates project completion summary with total cost, breakdown by party, and purchase dates; (d) both parties invited to leave project review / rating; (e) if homeowner satisfied with contractor: contractor referral promoted per W904 (contractor referral service); (f) completed project list archived in both parties' Project Vault for future reference or reorder | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | Automated |

### System Touchpoints
- Project Vault module (W894) — shared list creation, modification, and access control
- Loyalty/customer module — homeowner and contractor account linkage
- Mobile app / in-store kiosk — list sharing, modification, and purchase interface
- POS module — shared list purchase authorization, split payment, purchase marking
- Notification engine — sharing notifications, modification alerts, purchase updates
- Contractor referral module (W904) — post-project referral processing
- Analytics module — shared list adoption rate, project completion rate, average list value

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Privacy**: Homeowner may not want to share personal project details; privacy controls and consent essential
- **Dispute risk**: If contractor modifies list and homeowner disagrees, or if materials purchased don't match project needs, BuildRight may be drawn into disputes; clear disclaimer that BuildRight facilitates sharing but does not guarantee project outcomes; mitigated by CTL-267 (controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2))
- **Contractor adoption**: Getting contractors to use a digital tool requires training and incentive; loyalty points or trade account discounts for using shared lists
- **Payment complexity**: Split payments and authorized pickups add POS complexity; system must handle authorization codes securely
- **Offline contractors**: Many Filipino contractors are not tech-savvy; in-store assisted sharing option essential


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-review of "reviews list on their mobile device or in-store kiosk" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "calculated 22 sqm tiles but I know we need 25 sqm for the layout pattern" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "account → homeowner reimburses separately" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must handle authorization codes securely

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate**: Assisted sharing and purchase adds ~10–15 min per collaborative project — absorbed by existing role
- **No permanent incremental headcount**

### Time Estimate
- List creation & sharing: 5–10 min (customer self-service)
- Contractor review: 10–20 min (contractor self-service)
- Purchase authorization: 5–10 min
- **Total store-assisted effort per project**: 10–15 min (most interactions are self-service)

## W1227. Store-Level Customer Kitchen & Bathroom Complete Renovation Material Calculator & Project Quote

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests a complete material list for a kitchen or bathroom renovation project |
| **Frequency** | ~15–25 renovation material calculations per store per month; ~3,000–5,000/month chain-wide |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~20 calculations/month = ~4,000/month |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Tiles & Flooring / Plumbing) |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Sales Associate, Customer |

### Background

Kitchen and bathroom renovations are BuildRight Depot's highest-value residential projects. A typical bathroom renovation involves: tiles (floor + wall), toilet, faucet, sink, shower set, plumbing fittings (elbows, tees, adapters), waterproofing, grout, adhesive, and optionally a vanity mirror and accessories. A kitchen renovation adds: cabinets, countertop, sink, faucet, backsplash tiles, electrical outlets, and appliances. Homeowners and small contractors frequently come to BuildRight with room dimensions but no idea what materials or quantities they need. Providing a professional material calculator — a detailed breakdown of every item needed with quantities based on room dimensions — is a high-value advisory service that differentiates BuildRight from competitors and drives large basket sales. This is especially relevant for BuildRight where tiles & flooring (12% of SKUs) and plumbing (12% of SKUs) are core categories.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Room dimension capture**: Sales Associate captures room dimensions with customer: (a) bathroom: length × width × height, location of door and window; (b) kitchen: length × width, countertop run length, sink location; (c) note special requirements (walk-in shower, double vanity, island) | Sales Associate | — | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Material calculation**: Using ERP project calculator tool: (a) floor tiles: room area + 10% wastage = tiles needed (converted to boxes by tiles/box); (b) wall tiles: wall area (less door/window) + 10% wastage; (c) adhesive: kg per sqm per manufacturer spec; (d) grout: kg per sqm per joint width; (e) plumbing fittings: calculated from layout ( elbows, tees, adapters per standard bathroom/kitchen configuration); (f) fixtures: 1 toilet, 1 faucet, 1 sink per standard bathroom; (g) waterproofing: area × coats per manufacturer | System / Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 10–15 min |
| 3 | **Product selection**: Customer selects specific products: (a) tile design and size (affects quantity — larger tiles = fewer pieces); (b) fixture brands and models (price range); (c) color/finish preferences; (d) system updates quantities based on selected product specifications | Sales Associate / Customer | — | 15–30 min |
| 4 | **Project quote generation**: System generates complete project quote: (a) line-by-line material list with quantities, unit prices, and line totals; (b) categorized by trade (tiles, plumbing, electrical, fixtures); (c) project total; (d) estimated installation materials (not labor — labor is customer's responsibility); (e) optional: trade account pricing if customer is registered contractor per W58 | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 5 min |
| 5 | **Quote review & adjustment**: (a) customer reviews quote; (b) Sales Associate adjusts quantities, swaps products, or removes items per customer preference; (c) system recalculates totals; (d) save quote to customer's loyalty account for future retrieval | Sales Associate / Customer | — | 10–15 min |
| 6 | **Conversion to sale**: If customer proceeds: (a) convert quote to sales order per W58 quotation-to-order; (b) if trade account: apply trade pricing per W112; (c) arrange delivery per W19 if needed for bulky items; (d) if not ready to buy: save quote for future (valid for 30 days with price protection) | Sales Associate | Store Manager | 10 min |

### System Touchpoints

- W58 quotation-to-order conversion for quote-to-sale processing
- W112 trade counter operations for B2B pricing
- W168 custom paint mixing for wall color matching
- W19 home delivery for bulky item delivery
- W252 item master for product specifications and pricing
- W17 loyalty program for quote storage and customer history

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Measurement accuracy** — customer-provided room dimensions are often approximate; even 10cm error affects tile quantity significantly; recommend customer verify dimensions or offer home measurement per W1210
- **Scope creep** — customer starts with bathroom renovation but adds "while we're at it" items (lighting, exhaust fan, shower enclosure); calculator must handle add-ons gracefully
- **Tile size impact** — switching from 30×60cm to 60×60cm tiles changes quantity and wastage calculation; system must recalculate dynamically
- **Quote expiration** — prices change, especially for volatile construction materials per W1231; 30-day price protection may create margin risk if costs increase


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-count of "countertop run length, sink location" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "calculated from layout" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-update of "updates quantities based on selected product specifications" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: must recalculate dynamically

### Staffing Implication

- Sales Associate: ~45–80 min per renovation calculator session. Monthly per store: ~15 hours. Absorbed by existing Sales Associate roles in tiles/plumbing sections. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

- Per session: ~45–80 minutes. Monthly chain-wide: ~3,000 hours. Annual: ~36,000 hours.

## W1238. Customer Building Plan Review, Material Take-Off & Professional Estimation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (typically contractor, builder, or homeowner with building permit) presents building plan/blueprint for material estimation |
| **Frequency** | ~5–10 plan reviews per store per month; ~1,000–2,000/month chain-wide |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~7 plans/month = ~1,400/month |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Sales Associate, Category Manager, Customer |

### Background

Professional material take-off (MTO) is the process of analyzing a building plan/blueprint and calculating every material needed with precise quantities. For BuildRight's trade customer segment (30% of revenue — contractors and builders), this is a critical pre-construction step. A typical residential building plan in the Philippines (2-storey, 100 sqm footprint) requires: hollow blocks (~3,000–4,000), cement (~300–400 bags), rebar (~2–3 tons), lumber for formwork (~200 board feet), plywood, sand and gravel, electrical wire, PVC pipe, roofing sheets, nails, and more. An accurate MTO saves the contractor from: (a) under-ordering (work stoppage, additional delivery cost), (b) over-ordering (tied-up capital, storage issues, returns), and (c) missing items (emergency procurement per W60 at premium cost). BuildRight's Department Supervisors, particularly in Lumber & Building Materials, have the expertise to review plans and generate material lists. This is a value-added service that builds customer loyalty and positions BuildRight as a partner, not just a supplier.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Plan intake & scope assessment**: Customer presents building plan (blueprint, PDF, or sketch): (a) Department Supervisor reviews plan for scope (single-storey, multi-storey, residential, commercial); (b) identify which trades are covered (structural, architectural, plumbing, electrical); (c) confirm which materials customer wants from BuildRight vs. other sources | Department Supervisor | — | 15–20 min |
| 2 | **Structural material take-off**: For structural components: (a) foundation: estimate concrete volume (length × width × depth) → cement bags, sand, gravel; (b) hollow block walls: calculate wall area (less openings) ÷ block face area = blocks needed + wastage; (c) rebar: from structural plan — bar sizes, lengths, quantities per schedule; (d) formwork lumber: from floor area and number of pours | Department Supervisor | — | 30–60 min |
| 3 | **Architectural material take-off**: (a) roofing: roof area from plan → roofing sheets + ridge caps + fasteners; (b) tiles (if in plan): floor area per room per W1227; (c) doors and windows: count from plan, sizes from schedule; (d) paint: wall/ceiling area × coats ÷ coverage per gallon | Department Supervisor | — | 20–30 min |
| 4 | **Plumbing & electrical estimation**: (a) plumbing: pipe lengths from layout + fittings count + fixtures per W1227; (b) electrical: wire lengths from layout + switches/outlets count + panel/breakers from load schedule | Department Supervisor / Sales Associate | — | 20–30 min |
| 5 | **Consolidated material list & quote**: (a) compile all materials into single list organized by trade; (b) assign BuildRight SKUs to each material; (c) apply quantities with wastage factors (cement +5%, tiles +10%, lumber +15%); (d) generate quote with trade pricing per W112 if contractor has trade account; (e) calculate total project material cost | Department Supervisor / System | Category Manager | 15–20 min |
| 6 | **Customer review & order conversion**: (a) review material list with customer; (b) adjust quantities or swap products; (c) discuss phased delivery schedule (foundation first, then structural, then architectural); (d) convert to project order per W58 with staged call-off per W164; (e) set up project billing per W165 if applicable | Department Supervisor / Customer | Store Manager | 20–30 min |

### System Touchpoints

- W58 corporate/project account management for project setup
- W162 project quotation & bid management for professional quoting
- W164 staged project delivery & call-off orders for phased delivery
- W165 project retention & milestone billing for project billing
- W112 trade counter operations for trade pricing
- W252 item master for SKU matching and specifications

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Plan quality variation** — customer plans range from professional engineering blueprints to hand-drawn sketches on graphing paper; take-off accuracy depends heavily on plan detail and clarity
- **Expertise dependency** — only experienced Department Supervisors can perform accurate take-offs; turnover in these roles creates capability gaps; training and reference guides are essential
- **Time investment** — a full material take-off takes 1–3 hours; during peak hours, this takes the Department Supervisor away from the sales floor, impacting other customer service
- **Liability concern** — material estimates are advisory only; BuildRight must clearly communicate that estimates are not engineering specifications and customer should verify with their engineer/architect


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "blueprint, PDF, or sketch" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "calculate wall area" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-count of "count from plan, sizes from schedule" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication

- Department Supervisor: ~2–3 hours per full take-off. Monthly per store: ~14–21 hours. Absorbed by existing Department Supervisor role. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

- Per take-off: 1.5–3 hours depending on project complexity. Monthly chain-wide: ~2,800–4,200 hours. Annual: ~34,000–50,000 hours.

## W1246. Store-Level Customer Roofing & Structural Material Load Calculation & Estimation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests roofing or structural material estimation for a construction project |
| **Frequency** | ~10–20 roofing estimations per store per month; ~2,000–4,000/month chain-wide |
| **Volume** | 200 stores × ~15 estimations/month = ~3,000/month |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Sales Associate, Customer |

### Background

Roofing is a specialized subset of construction material estimation that BuildRight Depot frequently handles. Philippine residential roofing typically uses either: (a) corrugated GI (galvanized iron) sheets — most common, or (b) clay/concrete roof tiles — premium segment. Structural materials include: steel trusses (C-purlins, angle bars, tubular sections), lumber trusses (for simpler structures), and fasteners (roofing nails, tek screws, tie wire). Accurate roofing estimation requires: roof pitch angle, building footprint dimensions, overhang width, and ridge/hip/gable configuration. Unlike flat surface calculations, roof area is larger than footprint due to pitch. An error in estimation means either: (a) too few roofing sheets (incomplete roof, emergency procurement), or (b) too many sheets (waste, returns). BuildRight's Lumber & Building Materials Department Supervisors are trained to perform these calculations, providing a differentiated service that competitors without knowledgeable staff cannot match.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Project parameters capture**: Sales Associate captures roofing parameters from customer: (a) building footprint (length × width); (b) roof type (gable, hip, combination); (c) roof pitch/rise (Philippine standard: 30–45 degrees for typhoon country); (d) overhang width (typically 0.6–1.0m); (e) preferred roofing material (GI sheet gauge, roof tile type) | Sales Associate | — | 5–10 min |
| 2 | **Roof area calculation**: Department Supervisor calculates: (a) roof slope length: (rise² + run²)^0.5 per pitch; (b) roof plane area: slope length × building length (+ overhang on both sides); (c) total roof area: sum of all planes (2 for gable, 4 for hip); (d) applicable waste factor: +5% for simple gable, +10–15% for hip/complex with valleys | Department Supervisor | — | 10 min |
| 3 | **Material quantity calculation**: (a) roofing sheets: total roof area ÷ effective coverage per sheet (net after overlap); (b) ridge caps: ridge length ÷ coverage per ridge cap piece; (c) C-purlins (for steel frame): roof slope length ÷ purlin spacing (typically 0.6–0.8m) × number of planes; (d) truss material: calculated from span and spacing per structural design; (e) fasteners: roofing nails/tek screws per sheet × number of sheets; (f) insulation (if applicable): same area as roofing | Department Supervisor | — | 15 min |
| 4 | **Quote generation**: (a) assign BuildRight SKUs to each material item; (b) apply quantities with wastage; (c) calculate line totals at trade pricing per W112 if applicable; (d) generate project quote with delivery estimate for bulky items | Department Supervisor / System | — | 10 min |
| 5 | **Customer review & sale**: (a) review material list with customer; (b) discuss delivery options (roofing sheets and C-purlins are too large for customer vehicle); (c) convert to sale or save quote; (d) arrange delivery per W19 or W6 | Department Supervisor / Customer | Store Manager | 10–15 min |

### System Touchpoints

- W112 trade counter for trade pricing
- W58 quotation management for quote generation
- W6 logistics for delivery of bulky roofing materials
- W252 item master for roofing material specifications
- W19 home delivery for customer delivery scheduling

### Pain Points / Risks

- **Structural safety** — roofing structural calculations (truss sizing, purlin spacing) have safety implications; BuildRight must provide material estimates only, not structural engineering advice; customer must verify with licensed engineer
- **Typhoon compliance** — Philippine building codes require specific roofing gauge and fastener specifications for typhoon resistance; Department Supervisor should recommend appropriate gauge (typically 26–28 gauge GI) per location
- **Delivery logistics** — roofing sheets (8–12 feet long) and C-purlins require specialized delivery vehicles; not all 3PL partners can handle these; coordination per W6 is essential
- **Color availability** — pre-painted GI roofing sheets come in limited colors; customer color preference may not be in stock, requiring special order per W38 with longer lead time


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-generate of "Project parameters capture" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-calculate of "Roof area calculation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-assign of "assign BuildRight SKUs to each material item" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Lumber & Building Materials) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication

- Department Supervisor: ~40–60 min per roofing estimation. Monthly per store: ~10–15 hours. Absorbed by existing role. No incremental headcount.

### Time Estimate

- Per estimation: ~40–60 minutes. Monthly chain-wide: ~2,000–3,000 hours. Annual: ~24,000–36,000 hours.

## W1284. Customer Bulk Material Quantity Takeoff & Quotation Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer (typically contractor, builder, or homeowner) requests material estimate for a construction project based on building plans or verbal description |
| **Frequency** | ~40–60 quantity takeoff requests per store per month; ~8,000–12,000 chain-wide |
| **Volume** | Average takeoff covers ~15–40 material line items; ~5–10% conversion to full project order per W112 |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor |
| **Participants** | Department Supervisor, Sales Associate, Customer, Category Manager (for large projects) |

### Background

Professional contractors and serious DIY homeowners frequently bring building plans, sketches, or verbal descriptions to BuildRight Depot requesting a complete material list with quantities and pricing. This "quantity takeoff" service translates construction plans into a shopping list of materials: cement (bags), rebar (pieces by diameter), sand and gravel (cubic meters), hollow blocks (pieces), plumbing fittings, electrical wire (meters), tiles (square meters), paint (liters), and hardware. The service is a key differentiator that builds loyalty with the trade customer segment (30% of revenue) and drives large basket sizes. Effective takeoff service requires product knowledge, construction experience, and ability to read basic plans — skills concentrated in Department Supervisors.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer intake & project scope**: Department Supervisor receives customer's request: (a) review building plans, sketches, or photos customer provides, (b) if no plans: conduct structured interview — what are you building? (house extension, bathroom renovation, fence, etc.), dimensions, number of floors, materials preference, (c) determine project type: new construction, renovation, repair; (d) estimate project size to allocate appropriate time (small: <30 min, medium: 30–60 min, large: schedule appointment) | Department Supervisor | — | 10 min |
| 2 | **Material quantity calculation**: Department Supervisor performs quantity takeoff based on project scope: (a) **structural**: cement bags = floor area × 0.4 bags/sqm (typical); hollow blocks = wall area ÷ 0.08 sqm/block (= area × ~12.5 blocks/sqm, standard 8"×16"); rebar = based on structural requirement (customer to verify with engineer); sand/gravel = cement bags × 0.05 cu.m. per bag ratio; (b) **finishes**: tiles = floor/wall area × 1.10 (10% wastage); paint = area ÷ 25 sqm per gallon (2 coats) × 1.15 wastage; (c) **plumbing**: pipe length from plan, fittings estimated per connection type; (d) **electrical**: wire length from plan + 15%, switches/outlets counted from plan; (e) **hardware**: nails, screws, cement nails, adhesive based on project type | Department Supervisor | — | 20–45 min |
| 3 | **SKU matching & availability check**: Department Supervisor maps calculated quantities to BuildRight SKUs: (a) select appropriate product grade/brand based on customer budget preference (economy vs. premium), (b) system checks real-time availability at store and nearest DC for each item, (c) identify out-of-stock items and propose alternatives, (d) note lead times for items requiring special order per W38 | Department Supervisor / System | — | 10–15 min |
| 4 | **Quotation generation**: (a) apply appropriate pricing: SRP for walk-in, trade price per W112 for registered trade accounts, corporate pricing for registered B2B per W24, (b) calculate delivery logistics: bulk items (cement, sand, gravel) require delivery scheduling per W1283 or W6, (c) generate project quotation in system with: item list, quantities, unit prices, line totals, delivery estimate, quotation validity (30 days), (d) print or email quotation to customer | Department Supervisor / System | — | 10 min |
| 5 | **Quotation review with customer**: Department Supervisor reviews quotation with customer: (a) walk through each material category, explain quantities and assumptions, (b) highlight items that may need adjustment based on actual site conditions, (c) discuss delivery options and logistics for bulky materials, (d) discuss optional items not in takeoff (tools, PPE, adhesives, finishing materials), (e) note any substitutions customer requests | Department Supervisor | — | 15 min |
| 6 | **Follow-up & conversion**: (a) for trade/corporate accounts: Sales Associate follows up within 7 days per CRM workflow, (b) system tracks quotation status (draft → sent → viewed → accepted → expired), (c) if customer accepts: convert quotation to sales order (full project) or partial order (phased delivery), (d) for large projects (>PHP 500K): escalate to Category Manager for project pricing negotiation and dedicated logistics coordination per W112 | Sales Associate / Department Supervisor | Store Manager | 10 min/follow-up |
| 7 | **Post-project feedback**: For completed projects, Department Supervisor follows up: (a) were material quantities accurate? (too much = waste for customer; too little = return trip = poor experience), (b) capture actual quantities used vs. estimated for takeoff accuracy improvement, (c) request customer review/testimonial for marketing per W14, (d) identify follow-on project opportunities (extension, finish-out, landscaping) | Department Supervisor | — | 10 min |

### System Touchpoints
- Quotation management module with project templates by construction type (W1284.4)
- Real-time inventory availability check at store and DC level (W1284.3)
- Trade pricing engine (W112) and corporate pricing (W24)
- CRM quotation tracking with conversion funnel analytics (W1284.6)
- Delivery scheduling integration (W1283, W6)
- Takeoff accuracy tracking: estimated vs. actual quantities (W1284.7)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Liability for structural estimates** — quantity takeoffs for structural elements (rebar, cement mix ratio, column dimensions) have safety implications; Department Supervisors must clearly state estimates are for material planning only and structural engineering is the customer's responsibility
- **Estimation accuracy** — construction waste factors, site conditions, and plan variations cause actual quantities to differ from estimates; 10–15% variance is typical; managing customer expectations is critical
- **Time-intensive for Department Supervisors** — a medium takeoff takes 45–60 minutes; with ~1–2 requests per day, this consumes 15–20% of Department Supervisor time, diverting from floor management and customer service
- **Unconverted effort** — many takeoffs result in price shopping rather than purchase; customer takes BuildRight's detailed quotation to a competitor for price matching; mitigated by requiring trade account registration for detailed takeoffs or charging a refundable consultation fee for non-members


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-review of "review building plans, sketches, or photos customer provides" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "verify with engineer" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculated quantities to BuildRight SKUs" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: requiring trade account registration for detailed takeoffs or charging a refundable consultation fee for non-members

### Staffing Implication
- **Department Supervisors**: ~1–2 hours/day on quantity takeoffs. Absorbed by existing role (4 Supervisors sharing load).
- **Sales Associates**: ~15 min/day on quotation follow-up. Absorbed.
- **No incremental headcount**, but peak construction season (Jan–May) may require temporary staffing support in high-volume stores.

### Time Estimate
- Small takeoff: ~30 min; medium: ~45–60 min; large: ~90–120 min
- Monthly per store: ~50 requests × 50 min avg = ~42 hours
- Chain-wide monthly: ~200 stores × 42 hours = ~8,400 hours
- Follow-up: ~10 min × 50% conversion rate = ~4 hours/store/month

## W1314. Customer Project Material List (Bill of Materials) Creation, Management & Reorder Tracking

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests a comprehensive material list for a home construction or renovation project; or contractor/building professional requests project BOM for bidding |
| **Frequency** | ~30–50 project BOM requests per store per month; ~6,000–10,000/month chain-wide |
| **Volume** | ~200 stores × ~40 BOMs/month = ~8,000 BOMs/month; average BOM: 15–40 line items covering 3–8 product categories |
| **Owner** | Department Supervisor (Trade Counter / Pro Desk) |
| **Participants** | Sales Associate (Trade Counter), Department Supervisor, Customer, Category Manager (for pricing), Supply Planning Analyst (for availability) |

### Background

Filipino homeowners, contractors, and builders frequently approach BuildRight Depot for comprehensive material lists (BOMs — Bills of Materials) for construction and renovation projects such as: (a) building a house (from foundation to finishing — the most common large BOM), (b) bathroom renovation (tiles, plumbing fixtures, paint, accessories), (c) kitchen renovation (countertop, cabinets, tiles, plumbing, electrical), (d) perimeter fence construction (per W1091 — cement, hollow blocks, steel bars, GI sheets), (e) room extension or second-floor addition, (f) commercial fit-out (for trade/business customers). A well-prepared BOM lists every material needed with quantities, specifications, unit of measure, unit price, and total cost. For BuildRight, the BOM is a powerful sales tool: (i) a complete BOM locks in the customer's entire material spend with BuildRight (vs. buying piecemeal from multiple suppliers), (ii) the BOM enables BuildRight to offer project-level pricing (bundle discount), (iii) the BOM enables delivery scheduling coordination, (iv) the BOM creates a reorder relationship — the customer returns for additional materials as the project progresses. This workflow manages the creation, storage, pricing, availability checking, and reorder tracking of customer project BOMs.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer project scoping**: (a) Sales Associate interviews customer about the project: (i) project type (new construction, renovation, extension, fit-out), (ii) project scope (square meters, number of rooms/floors, specific rooms), (iii) budget range, (iv) timeline (when does construction start? target completion?), (v) customer type: DIY homeowner, contractor with crew, or builder/developer; (b) based on project scope, Sales Associate selects appropriate BOM template from system: (i) standard house construction BOM (1-story, 50–100 sqm), (ii) bathroom renovation BOM (5–10 sqm), (iii) kitchen renovation BOM (8–15 sqm), (iv) fence construction BOM (per linear meter), (v) custom BOM (no template — built from scratch); (c) Sales Associate uses the BOM template as a starting point and customizes per customer's specific requirements: product preferences (brand, grade, color), specification changes, additions, and omissions | Sales Associate | Department Supervisor | 15–30 min |
| 2 | **BOM creation with quantity calculation**: (a) Sales Associate builds the BOM line by line in the ERP BOM module: (i) select product category → select specific item → enter quantity, (ii) system calculates quantity based on project dimensions: e.g., for a 10 sqm bathroom floor tile at 60cm × 60cm tile size: 10 sqm ÷ 0.36 sqm/tile = 28 tiles + 10% wastage = 31 tiles; e.g., for a 50 sqm house floor screed: cement bags = area × 0.12 bags/sqm (thin screed; a full slab uses ~0.4 bags/sqm per W1284) = 6 bags + 15% wastage = 7 bags; (iii) for each line item, system displays: product description, SKU, specification, unit of measure, unit price (SRP), quantity, and line total; (b) system suggests complementary items: (i) if customer selects tiles: suggest tile adhesive, grout, spacers, and tile cutter rental (W139), (ii) if customer selects paint: suggest primer, rollers, brushes, drop cloths, and paint mixing (W168), (iii) if customer selects plumbing fixtures: suggest pipes, fittings, sealant, and tools; (c) Sales Associate reviews with customer and adjusts as needed; (d) system calculates BOM total: subtotal (SRP), project discount (if applicable — typically 5–15% for full BOM purchase), VAT (12%), and grand total | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | 20–45 min |
| 3 | **BOM availability check & pricing confirmation**: (a) system checks real-time inventory availability for all BOM items at the customer's preferred store: (i) items in stock: confirmed available, (ii) items low stock (< 5 units): flagged — may sell out before customer purchase, recommend reservation per W546, (iii) items out of stock: system checks nearest store or DC availability — offer transfer (W219) or special order (W38), (iv) items not carried (special specifications): offer special order per W38 with lead time estimate; (b) for trade account customers: system applies trade pricing per W8 (5–15% discount based on account tier); (c) for project-level pricing (BOM total > PHP 50,000): Department Supervisor reviews and may offer additional project discount: (i) PHP 50K–100K: additional 3–5% project discount, (ii) PHP 100K–500K: additional 5–8% project discount, (iii) > PHP 500K: VP Merchandising approval required for pricing exception; (d) finalized BOM saved in customer's profile with project name and reference number | Sales Associate / System / Department Supervisor | Department Supervisor | 10–20 min |
| 4 | **BOM delivery scheduling & fulfillment coordination**: (a) for customers purchasing the full BOM: (i) Sales Associate coordinates delivery scheduling: large projects may require 3–5 scheduled deliveries (foundation materials first, then framing, then finishing), (ii) system creates delivery schedule with dates, items, and quantities per delivery, (iii) for trade accounts: schedule billed deliveries per credit terms; (b) for BOMs exceeding PHP 100K: Department Supervisor assigns the BOM a dedicated project coordinator (rotated among Sales Associates) for follow-up and reorder management; (c) system generates project BOM summary for customer: itemized list with pricing, delivery schedule, and trade account terms (if applicable) | Sales Associate / Department Supervisor | Department Supervisor | 15–30 min |
| 5 | **BOM reorder tracking & project completion**: (a) as the customer's project progresses: (i) customer returns to BuildRight for additional materials not in original BOM (e.g., changed design, additional scope), (ii) Sales Associate retrieves original BOM from customer profile, adds additional items, tracks project spend against original BOM, (iii) system tracks BOM fulfillment rate: % of BOM items purchased vs. planned — identifies customers who started but haven't completed their BOM purchase (follow-up opportunity); (b) monthly: Department Supervisor reviews open BOMs: (i) BOMs with no purchase activity in 30 days: Sales Associate contacts customer for follow-up — is the project still proceeding? does the customer need any modifications?, (ii) BOMs with partial fulfillment: encourage customer to complete remaining items with targeted promotion or delivery incentive; (c) upon project completion: Sales Associate marks BOM as completed, records total actual purchase vs. original BOM estimate (variance analysis feeds demand planning accuracy for W31) | Sales Associate / System | Department Supervisor | Monthly review: 2–3 hours/store |

### System Touchpoints
- ERP BOM module — project BOM creation, template management, quantity calculators
- Customer profile module — BOM storage per customer, purchase history tracking
- Inventory availability module — real-time stock check per store
- Pricing module — project-level discount calculation, trade pricing
- Delivery scheduling module — phased delivery planning per W19/W668
- Special order module (W38) — out-of-stock and non-catalog item ordering
- Sales analytics — BOM conversion rate, average BOM value, category penetration per project type
- Demantra demand planning (W31 — fit-gap C5) — BOM variance data for forecast refinement

### Pain Points / Risks
- **BOM accuracy** — quantity calculations based on standard formulas may not account for site-specific conditions (irregular room shapes, wall openings, complex tile patterns); Sales Associates must adjust standard calculations for non-standard projects
- **Price volatility** — BOM prices at quotation time may differ from actual purchase time (especially for import items affected by FX); trade customers expect price holds on BOMs, which is difficult for BuildRight to offer on FX-sensitive items
- **Customer cherry-picking** — customers may use BuildRight's BOM as a shopping list but purchase items from cheaper competitors; offering project-level discounts, delivery coordination, and trade credit creates switching costs
- **Project cancellation** — customers may cancel projects after BOM creation; if BuildRight ordered special items for the BOM, those items become excess inventory per W588


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "BOM creation with quantity calculation" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-review of "reviews and may offer additional project discount" (replaces manual Step 3).
- System auto-count of "BOM delivery scheduling & fulfillment coordination" (replaces manual Step 4).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Department Supervisor (Trade Counter / Pro Desk) review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- **Sales Associate (Trade Counter)**: ~30–45 min per BOM creation × ~40 BOMs/month = ~20–30 hours/month. Absorbed by existing Trade Counter staff.
- **Department Supervisor**: ~2–3 hours/month on BOM review and follow-up. Absorbed.
- **No incremental headcount.**

### Time Estimate
- Per BOM creation: 30–60 min (scoping 15 min + creation 20 min + availability 10 min + delivery 15 min)
- Monthly per store: ~40 BOMs × 45 min = ~30 hours
- Monthly follow-up: ~3 hours
- **Monthly chain-wide: ~200 stores × 33 hours = ~6,600 hours**
- **Annual chain-wide: ~79,200 hours**

## W1491. Customer Kitchen & Bathroom Design Consultation, 3D Rendering & Material Take-Off Generation

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests kitchen or bathroom renovation design consultation at store service counter or via online booking |
| **Frequency** | ~8–15 consultations per store per month; higher in stores near residential development areas |
| **Volume** | ~1,600–3,000 consultations/month chain-wide; ~40–60% conversion rate to material purchase |
| **Owner** | Project Estimator |
| **Participants** | Customer, Project Estimator, Department Supervisor (Plumbing & Electrical), Installation Coordinator |

### Background

Kitchen and bathroom renovations are among the highest-value residential projects for BuildRight customers, with average project values of PHP 80,000–300,000 (kitchen) and PHP 40,000–150,000 (bathroom). Unlike ad-hoc material purchases, these projects benefit from a structured design consultation where the Project Estimator helps the customer select fixtures, tiles, cabinets, countertops, plumbing fittings, and electrical accessories, generates a 3D visualization of the proposed design, and produces a complete material take-off (MTO) — a bill of materials listing every item needed with quantities. This consultative selling approach increases average transaction value by 40–60% compared to unassisted shopping, and positions BuildRight as a project partner rather than just a materials supplier. The service is offered free of charge, funded by the higher margins on project-scale purchases.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Consultation intake & measurement gathering**: (a) Project Estimator meets customer at the design consultation station (dedicated area with large screen, product samples, and tile/fixture catalogs); (b) captures project scope: kitchen or bathroom, room dimensions (customer provides measurements or schedules a site visit per PA-12.1), budget range, style preferences (modern, minimalist, industrial, Filipino-Spanish), and timeline; (c) if customer has photos of current space: uploads to system for reference; (d) if no measurements: schedules site measurement visit (separate workflow per W1491 extension) | Project Estimator | — | 15–20 min |
| 2 | **Product selection & design layout**: (a) using the room dimensions and customer preferences, Project Estimator guides customer through product selection on the 3D design tool: (i) floor and wall tiles: selection from BuildRight's tile catalog with quantity calculator based on floor/wall area + 10% waste factor; (ii) fixtures: toilet, faucet, shower system, sink from display catalog; (iii) cabinetry: modular cabinet system dimensions and finishes; (iv) countertops: granite, quartz, or laminate options with pricing per square meter; (v) plumbing fittings: visible and concealed components; (vi) electrical: lighting fixtures, outlets, switches; (b) Project Estimator enters selections into 3D design tool and generates real-time cost estimate; (c) customer adjusts selections to fit budget; (d) design iterations until customer approves layout | Project Estimator | Department Supervisor | 45–90 min |
| 3 | **3D rendering generation**: (a) system generates photorealistic 3D rendering of the designed space using selected products and materials; (b) multiple views: overhead plan, perspective views from 2–3 angles, close-up detail views; (c) rendering includes realistic lighting, textures, and product-specific appearances (e.g., exact tile pattern from catalog); (d) customer reviews rendering on large screen; (e) minor adjustments made if needed; (f) final rendering exported as PDF and emailed to customer along with a shareable web link valid for 30 days | System / Project Estimator | — | 10–15 min (automated rendering) |
| 4 | **Material take-off (MTO) generation**: (a) system auto-generates complete MTO from the approved design: (i) all selected products with exact SKU, quantity, and unit price; (ii) computed quantities for tiles (area-based), grout, adhesive, and waterproofing; (iii) plumbing materials: pipes, fittings, valves, connectors based on room layout; (iv) electrical materials: wire length estimate, conduit, junction boxes based on fixture placement; (v) consumables: sealant, screws, anchors, cement; (b) MTO totals displayed with: material cost subtotal, estimated installation labor cost (if BuildRight installation selected per PA-12.1), delivery fee estimate per W1488; (c) Project Estimator reviews MTO for completeness and adjusts quantities for waste factors | System / Project Estimator | — | 10 min |
| 5 | **Quotation generation & customer follow-up**: (a) system generates formal quotation document with: 3D renderings, itemized MTO, pricing, validity period (30 days), terms and conditions; (b) quotation saved in customer's profile in CRM; (c) Project Estimator schedules follow-up call/email at 7 days and 14 days if customer does not proceed; (d) if customer proceeds: quotation converted to sales order per PA-11.2 project sales workflow; (e) installation scheduled per PA-12.1 if BuildRight installation selected; (f) material delivery coordinated per W1488 or customer pickup | Project Estimator | Department Supervisor | 10 min + follow-up |
| 6 | **Design library & reuse**: (a) completed designs saved to store design library (anonymized if customer declines data storage); (b) popular designs tagged by category and price range for future customer inspiration; (c) monthly: Department Supervisor reviews design conversion rates and popular product combinations to inform assortment decisions per PA-01.1 | Project Estimator / Department Supervisor | VP Merchandising | 1 hour/month |

### System Touchpoints
- 3D kitchen/bathroom design tool with BuildRight product catalog integration (W1491.2–3)
- Automated material take-off engine computing quantities from room dimensions and product selections (W1491.4)
- Product catalog with real-time pricing and stock availability (W1491.2)
- Quotation generation module with PDF export and CRM integration (W1491.5)
- Customer follow-up task management with automated reminders (W1491.5)
- Design library with anonymization, tagging, and search for design reuse (W1491.6)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Design tool learning curve**: the 3D design tool requires training; new Project Estimators need 2–4 weeks of practice before they can efficiently guide customers; design quality varies by estimator skill
- **Customer measurement accuracy**: if customer-provided measurements are off by even 5 cm, tile and cabinet quantities may be wrong; site measurement visit (additional cost/time) is recommended but customers resist the extra step
- **3D rendering expectations**: customers sometimes expect magazine-quality renderings; the tool produces good but not photorealistic output; managing expectations is important
- **Conversion rate sensitivity**: at 40–60% conversion, each non-converting consultation represents ~60–90 minutes of unpaid estimator time; maintaining conversion rates requires skilled estimators and competitive pricing
- **Design tool licensing cost**: per-store 3D design software licenses cost PHP 30,000–60,000/year per station; ROI depends on conversion volume


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-log of "Consultation intake & measurement gathering" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-capture of "enters selections into 3D design tool and generates real-time cost estimate" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-review of "reviews rendering on large screen" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: Project Estimator review and approval gate

### Staffing Implication
- Project Estimator: ~60–90 min per consultation × ~12/month = ~12–18 hours/store/month. With stores averaging 1–2 dedicated estimators, this is a primary job function.
- Department Supervisor: ~1 hour/month on design library review. Absorbed.
- No incremental headcount beyond existing Project Estimator roles.

### Time Estimate
- Per consultation (intake through quotation): 90–135 min
- Follow-up per non-converting consultation: 15–30 min
- Monthly design library: 1 hour
- **Chain-wide: ~1,600–3,000 consultations × 2 hours avg = 3,200–6,000 hours/month**

## W1517. Customer Structural Steel & Rebar Estimation, Span Calculation & Engineering Advisory Service

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer requests structural steel/rebar estimation for residential or small commercial construction (2–5 story building, floor area 50–500 sqm) |
| **Frequency** | ~80–120 consultations/month across 200 stores |
| **Volume** | Estimated material value: PHP 50K–500K per consultation |
| **Owner** | Project Estimator (Structural) |
| **Participants** | Project Estimator, Customer, Category Manager (Steel), licensed Structural Engineer (external consultant on retainer) |

### Background

Structural steel and rebar estimation is one of the highest-value and highest-liability advisory services BuildRight offers. Unlike finish materials (tiles, paint) where estimation errors lead to extra trips or waste, errors in structural steel/rebar estimation can compromise building safety. Philippine residential construction commonly uses reinforced concrete frames (columns, beams, slabs) with deformed rebar (10mm–25mm diameter) and structural steel I-beams for larger spans. The National Structural Code of the Philippines (NSCP) governs minimum reinforcement ratios. While BuildRight is not an engineering firm and does not provide structural design, the advisory service helps customers understand approximate quantities and costs — and critically, identifies when a licensed Structural Engineer is required. This workflow bridges the gap between a customer walking in with a floor plan and purchasing the right amount of steel and rebar, while managing BuildRight's liability exposure.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer intake**: (a) customer presents floor plan, building permit drawings, or rough sketch of structure; (b) Project Estimator captures: number of floors, floor area per floor, column spacing, beam spans, slab thickness, and intended use (residential, commercial); (c) if customer has structural plans from a licensed engineer: proceed to material takeoff (Step 4); if no structural plans: advise customer that structural design requires a licensed engineer per the Civil Engineering Law (RA 544) and PD 1096/NSCP | Project Estimator | — | 15–20 min |
| 2 | **Span and load assessment** (advisory only, not structural design): (a) using the store's structural estimation reference tool (pre-programmed with NSCP-derived rules of thumb for typical Philippine residential construction): (i) estimate approximate column rebar requirements (number and diameter of main bars + ties based on column cross-section and floor count), (ii) estimate approximate beam rebar (main bars + stirrups based on span length), (iii) estimate slab reinforcement (welded wire mesh or rebar grid based on slab area and thickness), (iv) estimate structural steel members if applicable (I-beam/W-section sizes for spans > 6m); (b) system generates approximate material list with quantities | Project Estimator | — | 20–30 min |
| 3 | **Engineering referral** (if required): (a) if project exceeds advisory scope (building > 3 stories, commercial occupancy, unusual spans, seismic zone 4 per PHIVOLCS map): (i) Project Estimator refers customer to licensed Structural Engineer from BuildRight's referral directory (per W1521 Contractor Referral); (ii) provides customer with BuildRight's structural materials catalog and pricing for engineer reference; (b) if project is within scope (typical 1–2 story residential, standard spans): proceed to material takeoff | Project Estimator | — | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Material takeoff and quotation**: (a) system generates detailed material list: (i) rebar by diameter (10mm, 12mm, 16mm, 20mm, 25mm) × length (6m or 9m standard), quantities in pieces, computed weight in kg, (ii) tie wire (kg), (iii) welded wire mesh (sheets), (iv) structural steel sections (I-beam, C-purlin, angle bar) if applicable, (v) accessories (rebar chairs, spacers, chairs); (b) system checks current pricing from steel master and applies current market rate (steel prices fluctuate weekly in Philippines); (c) system checks stock availability at store and nearest DC | Project Estimator / System | — | 15–20 min |
| 5 | **Customer consultation and order**: (a) Project Estimator presents material list, cost estimate, and availability to customer; (b) advises on: (i) order timing — steel prices may change if order is delayed, (ii) delivery logistics — rebar and steel are heavy/bulky items requiring truck delivery per W1509, (iii) cut-to-size options — store can cut rebar to specified lengths per W169 (Lumber & Board Cutting adapted for steel); (c) customer decides: (i) purchase full quantity now, (ii) phased purchase aligned with construction schedule per W993 (Phased Material Delivery), (iii) take quotation for later | Project Estimator | — | 15–20 min |
| 6 | **Post-sale follow-up** (for phased purchases): (a) system creates material schedule linked to customer project; (b) Project Estimator calls customer at scheduled phase milestones to confirm next delivery; (c) any design changes (customer's engineer modifies structural plan) trigger re-estimation | Project Estimator / System | Category Manager (Steel) | 5–10 min/follow-up |

### System Touchpoints
- Structural estimation reference tool: NSCP-derived rules of thumb for typical Philippine residential concrete frame construction with rebar and steel section calculators (W1517.2)
- Material takeoff engine: rebar diameter × length → weight calculation, structural steel section weight tables, accessory estimation (W1517.4)
- Real-time steel pricing integration: weekly market rate update from Category Manager applied to estimation tool (W1517.4)
- Stock availability check: store and DC inventory for steel and rebar SKUs (W1517.4)
- Project material schedule: phased purchase tracking linked to customer project and construction timeline (W1517.6)
- Integration with W169 (Cutting Services for steel cutting), W1509 (Bulk Delivery), W993 (Phased Delivery), W1521 (Contractor Referral for engineer referral)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Liability exposure**: despite clear disclaimers that BuildRight provides estimation advisory and not structural engineering, customers may rely on advisory quantities for construction; if quantities are insufficient and structural failure occurs, BuildRight faces reputational damage and potential litigation — mitigated by mandatory engineering referral for projects exceeding advisory scope
- **Steel price volatility**: Philippine steel rebar prices fluctuate PHP 2,000–5,000/MT weekly due to global iron ore prices, PHP/USD exchange rate, and local supply disruptions; a quotation valid for 7 days may be 5–10% off by the time the customer orders, causing friction
- **Knowledge intensity**: structural estimation requires significantly more technical knowledge than finish material estimation; finding and training Project Estimators with structural knowledge for all 200 stores is challenging — may need regional specialist coverage model with 1 structural estimator covering 10–15 stores
- **Rebar cutting logistics**: store-level rebar cutting requires dedicated equipment (rebar cutter/bender) and trained operator — not all 200 stores may have this capability, requiring DC-side cutting or vendor cutting service coordination


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-capture of "captures" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-count of "Span and load assessment" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-log of "catalog and pricing for engineer reference" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: mandatory engineering referral for projects exceeding advisory scope

### Time Estimate
- Per consultation: 60–100 min (intake 15–20 min + assessment 20–30 min + referral/decision 5–10 min + takeoff 15–20 min + consultation 15–20 min)
- Follow-up calls: 5–10 min each
- **Chain-wide: ~80–120 consultations/month × 80 min average = ~6,400–9,600 minutes ≈ ~107–160 staff hours/month**

### Staffing Implication
- Project Estimator (Structural): ~80–120 consultations/month ÷ 200 stores = ~0.4–0.6 consultations/store/month; regional specialist model with 1 structural estimator covering 10–15 stores = ~15–20 specialists chain-wide
- Category Manager (Steel): ~2–3 hours/week on steel pricing updates to estimation tool; absorbed
- No incremental headcount beyond existing Project Estimator pool; structural knowledge training investment needed

---

## W1518. Customer Solar PV System Sizing, ROI Modeling, Net Metering Application & Installation Package Advisory

| Field | Detail |
|---|---|
| **Trigger** | Customer inquires about solar panel system for residential or small commercial property |
| **Frequency** | ~60–100 consultations/month across 200 stores (growing 30–40% year-over-year as solar adoption accelerates in Philippines) |
| **Volume** | Average system: 3–10 kWp; material value PHP 80K–400K; average ticket PHP 150K |
| **Owner** | Project Estimator (Electrical) |
| **Participants** | Project Estimator, Customer, Category Manager (Electrical), Solar Installation Partner (external) |

### Background

The Philippines' high solar irradiation (4.5–5.5 kWh/sqm/day), rising electricity costs (PHP 9–12/kWh residential, per the W975/W1096 range), and the Renewable Energy Act (RA 9513) with its net metering provision make rooftop solar one of the fastest-growing product categories in Philippine home improvement retail. BuildRight stocks solar panels, inverters, batteries, mounting hardware, and balance-of-system components. However, solar is a complex technical sale: correct system sizing requires analyzing the customer's monthly consumption (kWh), roof orientation and tilt, shading, and local utility (MERALCO, Davao Light, VECO, etc.) net metering requirements. The ROI calculation must account for: system cost, installation cost, monthly savings, net metering credits, battery storage economics, and payback period. BuildRight's advisory service positions the store as a trusted energy advisor — not just a solar panel retailer — driving higher attach rates for inverters, batteries, mounting, and installation services.

### Steps

| # | Activity | Role (R) | Role (A) | Duration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Customer intake**: (a) Project Estimator captures: (i) monthly electricity bill amount (PHP) and kWh consumption from utility bill, (ii) roof type (tin/GI sheet, concrete slab), (iii) approximate roof area available, (iv) roof orientation (N/S/E/W facing), (v) property type (residential, commercial), (vi) customer objective: maximize savings vs. backup power vs. grid independence; (b) if customer has photos of roof and meter: upload to system | Project Estimator | — | 15–20 min |
| 2 | **System sizing**: (a) using BuildRight's solar sizing tool: (i) input monthly kWh consumption → system calculates recommended kWp size (target: offset 60–80% of consumption for optimal ROI), (ii) system checks roof area feasibility (kWp → number of panels → sqm required at ~6–7 sqm/kWp for standard panels), (iii) adjusts for orientation loss: south-facing optimal (100%), east/west (80–85%), north (60–70%), (iv) adjusts for shading if customer reports obstructions; (b) system recommends: (i) solar panel model and quantity (from BuildRight stock), (ii) inverter size and type (string vs. microinverter), (iii) battery storage size (if backup power objective: lithium battery kWh; grid-tied without battery is standard for ROI optimization), (iv) mounting system (roof-mount rails, clamps, flashings), (v) balance-of-system components (DC/AC cabling, disconnects, surge protection, monitoring system) | Project Estimator / System | — | 15–20 min |
| 3 | **ROI modeling**: (a) system calculates: (i) estimated annual energy production (kWh/year based on location-specific solar irradiation data from PAG-ASA/PVGIS), (ii) estimated monthly savings (kWh × local utility rate), (iii) net metering credits for excess production exported to grid (per ERC net metering rules), (iv) total system cost (materials + installation partner fee), (v) payback period (years to break even), (vi) 25-year total savings projection; (b) system generates customer-facing ROI summary: payback period, monthly savings, 25-year net savings, CO₂ offset | System / Project Estimator | — | 5–10 min |
| 4 | **Net metering application guidance**: (a) Project Estimator explains net metering process to customer: (i) customer applies to local distribution utility (DU) using ERC-prescribed forms, (ii) DU conducts technical review and interconnection agreement, (iii) DU installs bidirectional meter replacing existing unidirectional meter, (iv) process takes 30–90 days depending on DU; (b) provides BuildRight's net metering application guide (standardized packet with: ERC application form template, sample single-line diagram, electrical design specification, and BuildRight system specification sheet); (c) advises customer to engage licensed Electrical Engineer or Master Electrician for net metering application — can refer from BuildRight's contractor directory per W1521 | Project Estimator | — | 10–15 min |
| 5 | **Material quotation and order**: (a) system generates material quotation: itemized list of all components, quantities, unit prices, total; (b) checks stock availability at store and DC; (c) presents installation partner options (if customer wants turnkey): 2–3 accredited solar installers from BuildRight's contractor directory with estimated installation cost; (d) customer decides: (i) materials only (DIY or own electrician), (ii) materials + BuildRight installation partner; (e) if ordered: system creates sales order with scheduled delivery (solar panels require careful handling — no stacking, fragile glass) | Project Estimator | — | 15–20 min |
| 6 | **Post-installation follow-up**: (a) Project Estimator contacts customer 30–60 days after installation to verify system performance; (b) if customer reports underperformance: coordinates troubleshooting with installation partner and Category Manager (Electrical); (c) collects customer testimonial and performance data for future ROI model calibration | Project Estimator | Category Manager (Electrical) | 10–15 min/follow-up |

### System Touchpoints
- Solar sizing tool: consumption-to-kWp calculator, roof area feasibility check, orientation and shading adjustment, component recommendation engine (W1518.2)
- ROI model: location-specific irradiation data, utility rate lookup, net metering credit calculation, payback and 25-year projection (W1518.3)
- Net metering guide generator: standardized application packet with system specifications pre-filled (W1518.4)
- Material quotation with stock availability and installation partner pricing (W1518.5)
- Post-installation follow-up tracker with performance data collection for ROI model calibration (W1518.6)
- Integration with W990 (Solar Panel System Sizing — basic calculator), W1071 (Smart Home — complementary products), W1521 (Contractor Referral for installation partner)

### Pain Points / Risks
- **Technical complexity**: solar system design requires electrical engineering knowledge that most store associates do not possess; incorrect sizing (too small: disappointed customer; too large: poor ROI and wasted roof space) damages trust; training investment is significant
- **Net metering processing delays**: distribution utilities (especially provincial electric cooperatives) are notorious for 60–90+ day net metering processing times; customers blame BuildRight for delays that are outside the company's control
- **Panel damage in transit and handling**: solar panels (glass-fronted, aluminum-framed, 20–25 kg each) are fragile and awkward to handle; damage rate of 2–5% in transit to provincial stores erodes margin
- **Utility rate uncertainty**: ROI projections assume current utility rates; if DU rate increases are lower than projected (or net metering rules change per ERC), actual payback period extends, damaging credibility of BuildRight's advisory


### Automation Opportunity
- System auto-upload of "upload to system" (replaces manual Step 1).
- System auto-validate of "checks roof area feasibility" (replaces manual Step 2).
- System auto-calculate of "calculates" (replaces manual Step 3).

### Controls
- CTL-267 (ensure controlled execution — Project Estimation & Advisory (PA-09.2).)
- operational: design requires electrical engineering knowledge that most store associates do not possess

### Time Estimate
- Per consultation: 60–85 min (intake 15–20 min + sizing 15–20 min + ROI 5–10 min + net metering guidance 10–15 min + quotation 15–20 min)
- Follow-up: 10–15 min per customer
- **Chain-wide: ~60–100 consultations/month × 75 min average = ~4,500–7,500 minutes ≈ ~75–125 staff hours/month**
- **Growing 30–40% annually — staffing plan must account for demand growth**

### Staffing Implication
- Project Estimator (Electrical): ~60–100 consultations/month ÷ 200 stores = ~0.3–0.5/store/month; regional specialist model (similar to structural) with 1 electrical/solar specialist covering 8–12 stores = ~17–25 specialists chain-wide
- Category Manager (Electrical): ~4–6 hours/month on solar product training and pricing updates; absorbed
- Growing demand (30–40% YoY) may require 1 dedicated Solar Estimator per region (4 regions) within 2 years
- No immediate incremental headcount; investment in training existing Project Estimators on solar sizing tool
